# Manipulation (Capability) — Five-Perspective Review

> [!abstract] What this is
> A five-perspective adversarial review of every **cluster** and **research direction** in this doc, generated 2026-06-20.
> Each unit was put through a fixed 4-prompt lens by an independent analyst then an independent peer reviewer:
> **P1** five expert perspectives (Practitioner / Academic / Skeptic / Economist / Historian) · **P2** contradiction map ·
> **P3** synthesis briefing (for a researcher deciding whether to *commit* to the bet) · **P4** independent peer review (confidence 1-10 + Stanford-prof grade).
> Every novelty verdict was stress-tested against the live literature via the alphaxiv MCP. Cited paper ids are real (vault KH notes or alphaxiv hits).

> [!warning] Known stale figure (2026-08-12)
> This review's D3 (Exploration-Driven Emergent Dexterity) discussion repeatedly cites [[2603.15789|OmniReset]]'s real-world result as "25% zero-shot Peg Insertion vs 4% demo-DP" (e.g. finding #2, scored 9/10 as "directly verified"). That figure was a mis-scraped KH note — OmniReset's actual reported number is **85.37% zero-shot real Peg Insertion** (task-dependent: 56.36% Leg Twisting, 15.38% Drawer Insertion) vs a diffusion-policy baseline at **~2%** aggregated across tasks. The KH note and the live `Manipulation.md` D3 card were corrected on this date; this review transcript is left unedited as a historical record of the session it documents — treat every 25%/4% reference below as superseded.

## Manipulation — doc-level synthesis

### Capsule (the doc as a body of bets)

Manipulation is the most internally coherent and mechanically self-aware doc in the Capability axis: nineteen units (5 clusters A–E, 14 directions) organized along one honest meta-thesis — that the field has *settled the continuous substrate* (cross-hand function/contact-space transfer, soft force penalties, continuous residual contact policies all work now) and the only live bets are narrow *discrete/hard/structural wedges* carved out of that settled background. Every direction concedes its own background and stakes the paper on a single un-run A/B. This makes the doc unusually falsifiable and honest, but also makes its value thin and tie-prone: most directions land "partially-pre-empted," graded B/B+ (meanConf 5.8–7.8), with only two clean still-whitespace bets (A2, A3, D3 framing-wise) and two genuinely strong cards (D3, D4, B2 at A-/A- meanConf 7.6–7.8). Value concentrates not in the proposed *methods* (every component already exists in someone's table) but in two repeated assets the reviews keep surfacing: (1) the *matched-data head-to-head protocol* nobody has run, and (2) a small set of genuinely-novel *compositions* (B2's reversibility-gate, D3's emergent-multi-phase-dexterity-from-reset-diversity, D4's certifiable fragile-force ceiling). The doc's coherence is its strength; its fragility is that the same Historian argument recurs across A2/B2/D4 and predicts most of the discrete/hard headline claims lose on raw SR.

### Cross-direction pattern (the shared bottleneck)

**Every direction optimizes a one-shot / steady-state quantity and is blind to the closed-loop, post-contact, horizon-compounding regime where manipulation actually fails.** This is the single most consistent blind spot in the doc:
- A/A1/A2 optimize a *grasp distribution at contact* and never model slip / re-grasp / execution after contact (GTP-FA sits in A1's own table as the counter-evidence).
- A3 frames deformables in *steady-state* force-bounding terms, blind to the transient contact transition (first squeeze, slip onset) where a predictive model should win.
- B1/B2 measure single-step Fréchet / task-SR and never isolate *where in the trajectory* imagined tactile diverges under closed-loop action feedback — the failure that makes long-horizon assembly collapse silently.
- D2/D4/E1/E2 all measure *per-frame open-loop* force magnitude; none address reactive force *regulation* over a horizon, and a sensor-free policy (E1/E2) has no runtime error signal to close a loop on at all.

A second, structural pattern recurs underneath it: **the value keeps migrating from "method" to "protocol/benchmark."** In A2, B2, C2, D4, E2 the Economist (and often Academic) independently concludes the defensible contribution is the matched-data shoot-out or the new metric, not the architecture — because every architectural component is already individually occupied.

### Sharpest intra-doc contradiction

**A2 vs the rest of the doc on whether discrete/structured beats continuous.** A2's entire surviving novelty (H2) bets that a *discrete grasp-taxonomy latent beats continuous FAAS at cross-hand transfer* because discreteness "strips hand-specific residue." But the doc's own Historian voice — invoked nearly verbatim in B2, D4, and A1 — argues the exact opposite recurring lesson: discrete/symbolic structure *always loses on raw SR once continuous methods get data/capacity, and survives only in the interpretability or new-affordance niche.* B2 operationalizes this against itself ("bet on the reversibility predicate, the part continuous methods structurally cannot express — not the dynamics prediction continuous wins"); D4 does the same ("the pendulum has swung both ways; characterize the crossover, don't claim hard-beats-soft"). So A2 stakes its headline on precisely the inequality that A2's own Academic/Historian, and two sibling directions, predict is most-likely false. The doc contains both the bet and its refutation, and never reconciles them — the honest resolution (the hybrid: discrete prior + continuous residual) is named in A2 finding (v) as "the actual paper" but rated lowest-confidence and never promoted.

### Recurring blind spots

1. **Closed-loop / post-contact / horizon dynamics** (above) — the doc-wide bottleneck.
2. **Unsourced load-bearing stats from papers outside the corpus.** A striking number of the *most verdict-weighty* findings rest on a stat or a paper that is not in the card and not in the KnowledgeHub: Dexonomy 2504.18829 (the named reason to collapse A1+A2, grep-absent), FAWAM/T-Rex 2606.08555/2606.17055 (B1's recommended baselines, IDs nowhere in B1, the "85% vs 63.75%" likely mis-pulled from TacVLA), UniMorphGrasp's "0.5–16.1%" (A1, not in the note), SAI 2606.16490 + MonoDuo (C/C1's fold-recommendation, mis-identified as single-platform bimanual), UniForce "90–120% retention" (E, untraceable, makes E2's target self-contradictory), FTF 2506.01944 (E1's pivot, not in card). The doc's verdicts are repeatedly contingent on unverified external numbers.
3. **Force observability / sensor durability at deployment.** D4 assumes per-fingertip force is measurable at control rate (it is not on a 23-DoF hand); A3 assumes the dense tactile skin survives the wet/crushed/fouling regime it targets; E1/E2 assume a sensor-free policy can bound force it can no longer measure. The force *substrate* is the unaddressed moat under D4/E1/E2.
4. **The selector / specification problem.** Who picks the grasp type for a novel object (A2, a VLM), where the per-object force tolerance comes from (D4/A3), and whether the held-out unit is even constant (E2's within-sensor drift) — the doc parameterizes invariants but never closes who/what supplies the conditioning input.

### Commit picks (strongest, least-threatened)

- **D3 (Emergent multi-phase dexterity from reset diversity)** — A-, meanConf 7.8, highest in the doc; the four-way conjunction's only real weakness is an absence-of-evidence null sweep, fixable by operationalizing "emergent multi-phase" and re-searching by mechanism primitives.
- **D4 (Force-safety-constrained dexterous control)** — A-, meanConf 7.6; sharply self-aware, and the missing Certifier frame converts its own falsifier (soft-penalty tie) from fatal into the *result* ("the tie is the paper"). Commit *as a certification/guarantee contribution*, not a benchmark one.
- **B2 (5-state contact mode + reversibility gate)** — A-, meanConf 7.2; all five perspectives route value through H3 (reversibility on extraction is uncontested whitespace). Commit H3-first, demote the dynamics-switch to an ablation.
- **D2 (Reorientation interface bake-off)** — B+, meanConf 7.6; the strongest-sounding metric critique (finding 5) is largely moot because the card already routes around the saturated metric — a robust, low-risk characterization study.

### Reshape picks (most threatened)

- **A1 + A2 (cross-morphology grasp transfer)** — the de-risk/collapse recommendation hangs entirely on Dexonomy 2504.18829, which is unverified and outside the corpus; and A2's headline bet (discrete-beats-continuous) is the inequality the doc's own Historian predicts loses. Reshape A2 to the hybrid (discrete prior + continuous force residual) or to the protocol; verify Dexonomy before it carries any weight.
- **C3 (shared-channel bimanual force)** — B, meanConf 5.8 (lowest in doc); its load-bearing data-efficiency number (UME 3.3×) is teleop-collection speed, not representational sample-efficiency, and a closed-form analytic internal-force projection (~6-D squeeze wrench) is the un-cited baseline that may reduce C3 to "classical grasp-matrix control with a neural wrapper."
- **E2 (cross-sensor scaling law)** — B-, meanConf 6.4 (lowest grade); the saturate-early finding rests on 3 scaling-law papers absent from the vault, the curve has only ~3-5 distinct sensing principles to sweep (near-saturated), and the target is self-contradictory against the untraceable UniForce "90–120%." Reshape to lead with H2 (perception ≠ policy-SR, the clean negative) and the protocol, drop the count-based scaling-law framing.
- **C / C1 (single-platform bimanual)** — the "fold C1 into C2" recommendation rests on SAI mis-identified as single-platform bimanual when it is two-robot mobile collaboration; re-ground against arXiv, the fold collapses to "cite Co-VLA, sharpen H3."

### Doc grade

**B+.** A coherent, honest, mechanism-organized agenda with two genuinely strong bets (D3, D4) and a clear repeated insight (own the protocol, not the commodity method). Held below A- by three doc-wide liabilities: (1) the closed-loop/post-contact blind spot that no direction instruments yet every deployment failure lives in; (2) a pattern of verdict-critical findings resting on unsourced or mis-identified external papers; and (3) a self-contradiction the doc states but never resolves — half its discrete/hard headline bets are predicted to lose by the Historian argument the doc itself keeps invoking.

---

### A (Cluster review)

Reviewing the coherence + central shared-bottleneck thesis of Manipulation Cluster A — "Grasping & Grasp Synthesis" (A1 task-affordance-conditioned synthesis, A2 cross-morphology transfer, A3 deformable grasping under ill-defined contact), whose stated shared bottleneck is *"generating task-relevant, feasible grasps that transfer across objects and morphologies"* with [[2506.17198|Dex1B]]'s feasibility-constrained generation named as the shared substrate.

The cluster intro: *"Generating task-relevant, physically feasible grasp poses that transfer across object categories and hand morphologies — including the case where the grasp-pose itself is ill-defined and the gripper must create the contact rather than find it."*

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily)**
- *Core position.* The cluster is grouped by an academic abstraction ("grasp synthesis") but the three directions live on incompatible hardware-and-data regimes: A1/A2 are open-loop pose-generation in sim with optimization-validated grasps, A3 is closed-loop force-regulation that needs tactile skins or soft-body sim no production stack runs. What ships is none of these in isolation — it is a stable-grasp picker (AnyGrasp-class) plus a hand-coded post-grasp routine.
- *Strongest evidence.* The card's own deployment numbers are research-grade, not deploy-grade: [[2603.22264|UniDex]] zero-shot is 60% (Oymotion) / 40% (Wuji); [[2604.11674|AffordSim]] real Franka zero-shot averages **24%** (hanging only 10%); A3's [[2510.25405|Stress-Guided-RL]] is validated on tofu/foam in a lab. [[2212.08333|AnyGrasp]] alone hits 93.3% bin-picking at >900 MPPH — the thing actually deployed — and its "collapse" to 15%/3% only appears on AffordSim's *private* hard tier.
- *Only-this-perspective.* The shared substrate the cluster claims ([[2506.17198|Dex1B]]) is a *simulation* substrate — 1B grasps validated by SDF + post-optimization, 96% sim-to-real on *its own* objects. The cluster never costs the real-robot data or the per-hand exoskeleton ([[2505.21864|DexUMI]]) that A2 quietly depends on. The shared bottleneck is real but the shared *substrate* is sim-only, so "feasible" means "force-closure-feasible in MuJoCo," not "deployable."

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature)**
- *Core position.* The cluster is honestly scoped — each direction explicitly concedes its headline is *settled* and locates a narrower surviving wedge (A1: affordance-as-invariant + product-Q; A2: discrete-taxonomy-beats-continuous; A3: dense-tactile+differentiable-soft-body on held-out). That self-awareness is the cluster's strength, but it also means the cluster's *grouping* logic ("task-relevant + feasible + transfers") describes the consensus, not the bets — the bets are three unrelated falsification tests that happen to share a noun.
- *Strongest evidence.* Each direction's "Today's answers" row cites the consensus that ate its headline: A1 conditioning settled by [[2503.07360|AffordDexGrasp]] (45.1% open-set) / [[2603.08021|AffordGrasp]] (80.08% OakInk); A2 function-space transfer settled by [[2410.02479|Cross-Embodiment-DexGrasp]] (ICLR'25, 35.2% zero-shot) / [[2603.16806|DexGrasp-Zero]] (85% sim/82% real); A3 force-regulation settled by [[2510.25405|Stress-Guided-RL]] / [[2602.10013|Force-Regulated-Manipulation]] (38%→68%).
- *Only-this-perspective.* The literature already provides a *unifying* paper the cluster treats as three separate rows: [[2606.18092|EAGG]] (functional generation over 6 hands, 33.25% zero-shot) and [[2601.08246|FSAG]] (affordance-alone, swap only kinematics, KLD 2.491) jointly sit at the A1∩A2 seam — affordance-conditioned AND cross-morphology. The cluster's A1/A2 split is a presentational choice, not a natural joint; the real frontier paper would test A1's "affordance is the invariant" claim *as* A2's transfer protocol in one experiment.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks the bet is wrong or already solved)**
- *Core position.* The cluster's coherence claim is weakest exactly where it asserts whitespace: A2's "no one tested the discrete grasp-taxonomy as a transfer bottleneck" is contradicted by the grasp-taxonomy literature it half-cites. Dexonomy ([[2504.18829]], not in the A2 table but referenced as the "Dexonomy GSR" benchmark in A2's KPGrasp row) *builds the type-conditional generator across hands* (Allegro→Shadow, 31 GRASP types, 9.5M grasps, 82.3% real on unseen objects). The discrete-taxonomy machinery the bet calls unbuilt is built.
- *Strongest evidence.* Dexonomy synthesizes "*any grasp type, any object, any articulated hand*" from one template per hand/type and trains a type-conditional generator that generalizes to a *different* hand (real Shadow). Combined with the card's own [[2604.04138|Sparse-Taxonomy-Grasp]] (discrete taxonomy → control, 87.9% Objaverse), the *exact head-to-head A2 proposes* (discrete-latent zero-shot SR vs [[2603.22264|UniDex]] FAAS on a held-out hand) is the only un-run cell — but the substrate, the dataset, and the cross-hand demonstration all exist. That is "narrow open comparison," not "whitespace."
- *Only-this-perspective.* The cluster conveniently ignores that its three "settled-background" H1s are doing the load-bearing work: strip H1 from each direction and the surviving novelty (A1-H3/H2, A2-H2, A3-H2/H3) is, in every case, *one ablation cell* — affordance-conditioned-vs-stable-geometry transfer, discrete-vs-continuous transfer, dense-tactile+model-vs-vision-only on held-out. Three single-cell ablations do not make a publication bet each; they make one paper on "what survives a held-out / cross-hand split."

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives)**
- *Core position.* The cluster's structure mirrors the field's funding gradient: A1/A2 chase the VLA-foundation-model narrative (egocentric-video pretraining, "one policy for all hands") that attracts industry compute and citations; A3 chases the tactile-hardware-vendor narrative (GelSight, dense skins, soft-body sim). The shared-bottleneck framing is partly a *grant-coherence device* — it lets three differently-incentivized bets share one proposal.
- *Strongest evidence.* The data-cost numbers are the tell: [[2603.22264|UniDex]] sells a "5.2× cost cut", [[2505.21864|DexUMI]] "3.2× efficiency", [[2502.16420|AnyDexGrasp]] "millions of labels → hundreds of attempts" — every A2 anchor is priced in data-collection-cost, the currency academia competes on against industry's teleoperation farms. A1's [[2604.11674|AffordSim]] sells "≥93% of manual-annotation success *without annotation*" — the same label-cost pitch. The cluster is organized around *who can afford the data*, which is a real and underrated joint.
- *Only-this-perspective.* Benchmark saturation is the hidden risk: A1 leans on AffordSim's *private* 50-task benchmark and OakInk; A2 on a "de-facto 45-object YCB held-out-hand protocol" ([[2410.02479|Cross-Embodiment-DexGrasp]]) that no standard body owns; A3 on SoGraB / per-method self-reported damage. None is a saturating community leaderboard like LIBERO/RoboTwin — which is good for novelty but bad for *legibility*: reviewers cannot rank the result, so the bet's payoff depends on the author also *owning the benchmark*. That is a citation-incentive trap, not a science problem.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves)**
- *Core position.* A2's "discrete bottleneck strips hand-specific residue better than a continuous space" is the latent-vs-pixel / discrete-vs-continuous representation cycle replayed on hands — VQ-VAE-vs-continuous-latent, options-vs-continuous-control, grasp-taxonomies (Cutkosky 1989, Feix GRASP 2016) reborn as a neural bottleneck. These cycles historically end in "continuous wins on asymptotic performance, discrete wins on sample-efficiency / interpretability / transfer-under-shift" — which is *exactly* the regime A2 bets on but rarely the regime that gets deployed.
- *Strongest evidence.* The card itself contains the historical rhyme: [[2604.04138|Sparse-Taxonomy-Grasp]] uses the discrete taxonomy "only for controllability," and [[2602.08278|DexFormer]] shows transfer "*without any explicit coordinate*" (77.50% over 32 unseen variants) — i.e., the implicit-continuous-history pole already beats hand-crafted structure on raw transfer. Every prior discrete-structure revival (primitives, skills, options) lost the asymptote and survived only where data was scarce — A2's own data-cost framing is where it could survive.
- *Only-this-perspective.* A3 is the deformable replay of the model-based-vs-model-free RL war (Dreamer lineage): "differentiable soft-body world model beats model-free stress-RL on held-out" is the exact claim model-based RL made over SAC, which historically held *only* when the model was accurate and the horizon long, and lost on contact-rich discontinuity. A3's risk row already concedes soft-body sim runs at 80 FPS ([[2302.04659|ManiSkill2]]) — the same compute wall that sank model-based control on contact every prior cycle.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

**(1) Direct clashes.**
- *Skeptic vs Academic on A2 whitespace.* Academic reads A2 as honestly scoped ("the comparison no one ran"); Skeptic says Dexonomy ([[2504.18829]]) + [[2604.04138|Sparse-Taxonomy-Grasp]] already built the discrete-taxonomy cross-hand generator, so the claim "discrete taxonomy never tested as a transfer bottleneck" overstates the gap to *one un-run ablation cell*. Specific clash: is A2's bet a *direction* or a *single experiment*?
- *Historian vs the card (A2-H2).* The card bets discrete > continuous on transfer; Historian + the card's own [[2602.08278|DexFormer]] row say the *implicit, no-explicit-coordinate* pole already beats explicit structure on cross-hand transfer (77.50% vs LSTM 58.72%) — so the live competition is not discrete-vs-continuous but explicit-vs-implicit, an axis A2's H2 does not test.
- *Practitioner vs Economist on A3.* Practitioner says A3's dense-tactile + soft-body stack is undeployable (compute wall, no production skins); Economist says that is precisely why it is fundable (hardware-vendor + academic-differentiation narrative). Same fact, opposite valuation.

**(2) Strongest vs weakest.**
- *Strongest: Academic.* Every claim is anchored to a cited consensus paper with a metric, and it correctly identifies that the cluster's grouping noun ("grasp synthesis") is presentational while the bets are three independent falsifiers — the most defensible read.
- *Weakest: Historian.* The cyclic analogies (discrete-vs-continuous, model-based-vs-model-free) are illuminating but non-falsifiable as stated; "this rhymes with VQ-VAE" does not predict *this* outcome, and the analogy to model-based RL partly misfires because A3's twin is used as a *predictor for force-regulation*, not for long-horizon planning.

**(3) The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction.**
On a single held-out hand under matched data, does a *discrete grasp-taxonomy latent* ([[2604.04138|Sparse-Taxonomy-Grasp]] / Dexonomy-trained) beat *continuous FAAS* ([[2603.22264|UniDex]]) AND beat the *implicit-no-coordinate* pole ([[2602.08278|DexFormer]]) on zero-shot transfer SR? This resolves Skeptic-vs-Academic (is there whitespace) and Historian-vs-card (is discrete even the right contrast) in one experiment.

**(4) What every perspective agrees on (likely true).**
The shared bottleneck — *task-relevant grasps that transfer across hands at low real-data cost* — is real and correctly identified; the field has settled "conditioning beats filtering," "function-space transfers," and "force-regulation beats pose-selection," so the cluster is bidding on the *right* residual axis (cross-morphology / held-out generalization), not a solved or fake one. All five perspectives accept the diagnosis even while disputing the prescription.

**(5) The blind spot (addressed by none).**
*Closed-loop grasp execution and recovery.* The entire cluster is about *generating / parameterizing / regulating* a grasp; none of the three directions, and none of the five perspectives until prompted, addresses what happens *after* contact when the grasp fails — re-grasp, slip detection, regrasp-under-perturbation. [[2606.03385|GTP-FA]]'s failure-attribution (+65.6 pp, π0.5 11.2%→76.8%) is sitting in A1's table as evidence that the *stable-but-unusable* gap is an execution-loop problem, yet the cluster frames everything as a one-shot synthesis problem. The grasp-as-a-distribution-to-sample framing structurally omits the grasp-as-a-control-loop reality.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**(1) CEO summary.** Cluster A is a *correctly diagnosed, honestly bounded* theme whose grouping noun ("grasp synthesis") oversells its internal coherence: the three directions share a real bottleneck (task-relevant grasps that transfer across hands at low data cost) but their *bets* are three independent held-out / cross-hand ablation cells, not a unified mechanism. The cluster's intellectual honesty is its strongest asset — each direction concedes its headline is settled and isolates a narrow surviving wedge — but that same honesty exposes that the surviving novelty in each case is *one experiment*, and at least one of those (A2's discrete-taxonomy transfer) is more crowded than the card admits now that Dexonomy ([[2504.18829]]) has built the cross-hand type-conditional generator. The bet is committable, but as *one sharp cross-cutting paper on what survives a held-out split*, not as three parallel directions.

**(2) Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *The shared bottleneck is real and correctly carved (high).* Supported by all five; the field has genuinely settled the three H1 "background" claims, so the residual cross-morphology axis is the right place to bid. Challenged by none — only its *framing as three directions* is disputed.
2. *Each direction's surviving novelty is a single ablation cell, not a direction (high).* Supported by Skeptic + Academic; A1→affordance-conditioned-vs-stable-geometry transfer (H3) + product-Q (H2), A2→discrete-vs-continuous transfer (H2), A3→dense-tactile+model-vs-vision-only on held-out (H2/H3). Challenged by the card's framing, which presents each as a standalone bet.
3. *A2's whitespace is partially pre-empted by the grasp-taxonomy literature (medium-high).* Supported by Skeptic + Historian; Dexonomy ([[2504.18829]]) builds the type-conditional cross-hand generator (82.3% real Shadow, 31 types) and [[2604.04138|Sparse-Taxonomy-Grasp]] the discrete-control stack — only the *transfer-vs-continuous head-to-head* is un-run. Challenged by Academic (the head-to-head genuinely was not run; the wedge is narrow but intact).
4. *A1 and A2 share a seam the cluster splits artificially (medium).* Supported by Academic; [[2606.18092|EAGG]] and [[2601.08246|FSAG]] already sit at affordance∩cross-morphology, so A1's "affordance is the invariant" *is* A2's transfer question. Challenged by Practitioner (the hardware regimes differ enough that the split is operationally sensible).
5. *The cluster structurally omits closed-loop execution/recovery (medium).* Supported by the blind-spot analysis + [[2606.03385|GTP-FA]]'s presence as evidence; challenged by none, but unweighted because it is a scope choice, not an error.

**(3) Hidden cross-perspective connection.** Economist's "the cluster is organized around who can afford the data" and Historian's "discrete structure survives only where data is scarce" are the same observation from two directions: A2's discrete-taxonomy bet is not really a *representation* bet, it is a *sample-efficiency-under-data-scarcity* bet wearing a representation costume. The discrete latent will win (if it wins) for the economic reason — fewer demos per new hand — not the geometric reason ("strips hand-specific residue"), and it will lose the asymptote to continuous/implicit poles exactly as every prior discrete revival did. That reframes A2's falsifier: test it *at matched small data*, where the economic edge lives, not at matched large data where the asymptote dominates.

**(4) Actionable insight for the researcher (COMMIT / DE-RISK / DROP).** **DE-RISK then COMMIT — to one cross-cutting paper, not three directions.** Collapse A1/A2 into a single "is task-affordance the cross-morphology invariant?" study and run the resolving experiment from Prompt-2(3): on the [[2410.02479|Cross-Embodiment-DexGrasp]] 45-object YCB held-out-hand protocol (train 4 hands → hold out LEAP + Inspire), at *matched small data*, compare four parameterizations head-to-head — (i) continuous FAAS ([[2603.22264|UniDex]]), (ii) discrete grasp-taxonomy latent ([[2604.04138|Sparse-Taxonomy-Grasp]] / Dexonomy-trained), (iii) implicit no-coordinate ([[2602.08278|DexFormer]]), (iv) affordance-conditioned ([[2601.08246|FSAG]]-style, swap only kinematics) — reporting both zero-shot SR and the few-shot-data curve. This single experiment de-risks the Dexonomy pre-emption (Finding 3), tests the right axis (Historian/Finding-4), and converts three single-cell ablations into one publishable matrix. *Drop* A3 from the bet's critical path: keep it as a separate force-regulation paper, since its compute wall (80 FPS soft-body) and undeployable dense-tactile stack make it a different risk profile that should not gate the cross-morphology result. Concrete next experiment: the four-way matched-small-data held-out-hand matrix above, with the product-score $Q_{stable}\cdot Q_{task}$ (A1-H2) measured *only* on the affordance arm as the secondary claim.

**(5) Frontier question that would change everything.** Is "the grasp" even the right unit — or is the cross-morphology invariant a *closed-loop contact-establishment-and-recovery policy* rather than a generated pose, such that the whole synthesis framing (A1/A2/A3 alike) is optimizing a distribution when it should be optimizing a controller? If [[2606.03385|GTP-FA]]'s 11.2%→76.8% jump from treating the stable-vs-functional gap as an *execution-loop* problem generalizes, the cluster's entire pose-generation substrate ([[2506.17198|Dex1B]]) is the wrong shared substrate, and the right one is a recovery-aware control loop.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics faculty. Grounded against `Capability/Manipulation.md` lines 102-389 (Cluster A: A1 task-affordance synthesis, A2 cross-morphology transfer, A3 deformable force-regulation). The briefing under review is the CEO summary + 5 findings + cross-perspective connection + actionable insight + frontier question.*

---

##### 1. CONFIDENCE SCORES (each 1-10 for reliability)

**Finding 1 — "Shared bottleneck is real and correctly carved; only the three-direction framing is disputed." → 8/10.**
Solid and well-supported by the card itself. Each direction's "Assumption being challenged" bullet explicitly concedes the H1 background is settled (A1 line 122: conditioning is "consensus"; A2 line 218: "function-space transfers... now consensus"; A3 line 313: "force-regulation beats pose-selection... now consensus") and isolates a cross-morphology residual. The "right bid" judgment is sound. Half a point off because the claim is partly self-fulfilling: the card was *written* to carve the residual this way, so "correctly carved" is grading the briefing against the artifact it summarizes, not against the field. The one genuine over-reach the finding under-flags: A3's surviving wedge is *not* cross-morphology at all (it is dense-tactile-vs-vision modality on held-out objects), so the "shared cross-morphology bottleneck" noun does not actually cover A3, which the briefing's own CEO summary half-admits.

**Finding 2 — "Each direction's surviving novelty is a single ablation cell, not a direction." → 7/10.**
Accurate diagnosis, and the strongest analytical move in the briefing. The card hands this to the reviewer: every Hypotheses block names which H "carries the surviving novelty" (A1 line 170: "H3/H2 carry the surviving novelty"; A2 line 265: "H2 carries the surviving novelty"; A3 line 356: "H1/H2/H3 are the front-line falsifiers"). So the finding is verifiable from the text. Docked for two things. First, it slightly overstates: A1 carries *two* live cells (H2 product-separability AND H3 morphology-invariance), which are genuinely independent claims, not one — collapsing them into "a single cell" is the inverse error of the one the briefing accuses the card of. Second, "single ablation cell" is a rhetorical compression: a held-out-hand transfer study with a four-way parameterization sweep and a few-shot curve (which is what the actionable insight then proposes) is a real paper's worth of experiments, not "one experiment." The finding and the actionable insight quietly contradict each other on how much work the wedge is.

**Finding 3 — "A2's whitespace is partially pre-empted by Dexonomy (2504.18829)." → 4/10.**
This is the weakest-supported high-stakes claim and the one a referee must flag. Dexonomy (2504.18829) appears **nowhere** in the cluster file, nowhere in the A2 table, and — confirmed by grep across the vault — is not in the KnowledgeHub at all. The briefing introduces it from outside the card with specific, load-bearing statistics ("82.3% real Shadow, 31 types," "type-conditional cross-hand generator") that are **unverified against any vault source**. If those numbers are right, the pre-emption is real and serious; if Dexonomy is type-conditional *synthesis* on a single hand (Shadow) rather than a cross-*hand transfer* generator, the pre-emption evaporates and the wedge is intact. The briefing asserts the former without showing the paper does cross-hand transfer. A claim that materially changes the publishability verdict cannot rest on an un-ingested paper's remembered abstract. Score reflects "plausible and important if true, but currently unverifiable from the evidence base the rest of the review is held to."

**Finding 4 — "A1 and A2 share a seam the cluster splits artificially (EAGG, FSAG sit at affordance∩cross-morphology)." → 7/10.**
Verifiable and correct on the facts: EAGG (2606.18092) and FSAG (2601.08246) appear in *both* the A1 table (lines 160-161) and the A2 table (line 251 / cross-listed), and FSAG's "What's missing" in A1 explicitly frames it as the H3 invariance comparator while transferring "across hands by swapping only the kinematic model" — that is verbatim an A2 transfer claim living in A1. So the seam is real, not invented. Held at 7 because the Practitioner counter the briefing itself records is legitimate and the briefing does not adjudicate it: A1 is overwhelmingly parallel-jaw + single-hand affordance papers (AffordSim, GraspMolmo, TD-TOG are all parallel-jaw), whereas A2 is dexterous multi-hand. The seam is two papers wide; the bulk of each table is genuinely a different hardware regime. "Artificially split" overstates a real-but-narrow overlap.

**Finding 5 — "The cluster structurally omits closed-loop execution/recovery." → 6/10.**
True and worth raising — but the briefing over-credits its own novelty here. GTP-FA (2606.03385) is *in* A1's table (line 145) precisely because it reframes the stable-vs-functional gap as an execution-loop / failure-attribution problem (11.2%→76.8%), and the card flags it as "treats the gap as attribution, not conditioning." So the cluster did not blindly omit closed-loop; it logged the counter-evidence and chose pose-generation as the substrate anyway. The finding correctly notes this is "a scope choice... unweighted," which is the honest framing. Docked because the briefing then escalates the same observation into the Frontier question as if it overturns the cluster, without weighing that A2/A3 are explicitly about *grasp-establishment* phase (the card repeatedly cedes the in-hand control cycle to Cluster D1, lines 207, 1328), so "should be optimizing a controller" partly attacks a scope the card already drew on purpose.

---

##### 2. WEAKEST LINK

**Finding 3 (the Dexonomy pre-emption), confidence 4/10.** It is the only finding whose evidence is not in the reviewed corpus, yet it carries the heaviest verdict-weight (it is the named reason to "de-risk" and the justification for collapsing A1/A2). To verify, I need: (a) the actual Dexonomy paper — does it generate grasps for *multiple distinct hand morphologies under a shared type-conditional latent*, or is "31 types" a taxonomy of grasp *types on the Shadow hand only*? (b) Whether it reports a *held-out-hand* zero-shot number (the A2 protocol), or only held-out-object on a fixed hand. (c) Its publication date relative to the card's freeze, since the card's cross-ref note (line 1348) claims "no discrete comparator and no standalone transfer-benchmark paper exists" — if Dexonomy is a discrete type-conditional *cross-hand* generator, it falsifies that sentence directly, which is a much stronger and more useful finding than "partially pre-empted." The single action: ingest 2504.18829 into KH and check the held-out-hand vs held-out-object axis before any verdict rests on it.

---

##### 3. BIAS CHECK — which perspective over-dominated

**The Skeptic over-dominated, with the Economist supplying the one genuinely new reframe.** Four of five findings (1, 2, 3, 4) are debunking moves — "framing is wrong," "novelty is one cell," "whitespace is pre-empted," "split is artificial" — all in the Skeptic's voice, and the actionable insight is a pure de-risk-and-collapse recommendation. The Academic appears only as the loyal-opposition counter-weight ("the head-to-head genuinely was not run; wedge intact"), and is twice positioned as the *thing being challenged* rather than as a generative contributor. The Practitioner appears once (finding 4's counter) and is then ignored in the actionable insight, which proposes a four-way matched-small-data held-out-hand matrix without costing the hardware (LEAP + Inspire physical hands, real-robot evaluation across four parameterizations) — exactly the practitioner objection it waved away. The Historian/Economist fusion in the "hidden connection" is the briefing's best original idea (discrete-latent-as-sample-efficiency-bet-in-representation-costume) and it is the *only* place the synthesis produces a claim the card does not already make. Net: the review is Skeptic-heavy, Academic-as-foil, Practitioner-underweighted, with one strong Economist insight carrying disproportionate load.

---

##### 4. MISSING PERSPECTIVE — a 6th angle that would change the conclusions

**The Benchmark/Measurement Engineer.** The entire actionable insight hinges on one protocol — Cross-Embodiment DexGrasp's (2410.02479) "45-object YCB, train 4 hands → hold out LEAP + Inspire" — being a *fair and sufficient* arbiter of "is task-affordance the cross-morphology invariant." A measurement specialist would ask the questions the briefing never does: (i) Two held-out hands (LEAP, Inspire) is n=2 — the card's own cross-ref note (line 1348) admits "no standalone transfer-benchmark paper exists" and that EAGG's 6-effector and Canonical Hand's LEAP-variant splits are *different, non-comparable* protocols; a four-way parameterization sweep on n=2 held-out hands cannot statistically separate four parameterizations with the confidence intervals real-robot SR carries. (ii) The proposed product-score secondary claim ($Q_{stable} \cdot Q_{task}$ recovering ≥93% of the oracle) is measured against AffordSim's *manual-annotation* oracle, which the card's own Risk bullet (line 198) says is itself the success ceiling — you cannot validate a label-free scorer against an oracle that is the bottleneck. (iii) "Matched small data" is undefined — matched in demos? in objects? in wall-clock collection cost? The Economist reframe (test at matched *small* data) is correct in spirit but un-operationalizable without a measurement protocol, and the briefing presents it as if it were a clean knob. This perspective would **downgrade the actionable insight from "run this resolving experiment" to "first build the cross-hand benchmark that can resolve it,"** because the resolving experiment as specified is under-powered and its secondary metric is circular. That is a different and more honest conclusion than the briefing reaches.

---

##### 5. OVERALL GRADE + top fixes

**Grade: B+ / A-.**

This is a strong, intellectually honest review that correctly identifies the real tension (an honest card whose grouping noun oversells coherence) and produces one genuinely original synthesis (the Economist/Historian fusion). It earns the high mark for not rubber-stamping and for surfacing the A1/A2 seam from the actual table contents. It is held below A- by three fixable faults: a heavily-weighted finding (3) resting on an un-ingested paper, an over-claimed "single cell" compression that contradicts its own multi-experiment actionable insight, and an actionable insight that inherits exactly the practitioner/measurement objections it dismisses.

**Top fixes, in priority order:**
1. **Verify Dexonomy (2504.18829) before it carries any verdict-weight.** Ingest it; confirm whether it is cross-*hand* transfer (kills the wedge) or single-hand type-conditional synthesis (leaves the wedge intact). The whole "de-risk the pre-emption" recommendation is contingent on this and currently unfalsified.
2. **Add a benchmark-power caveat to the actionable insight.** n=2 held-out hands cannot separate four parameterizations on noisy real-robot SR. Either argue the experiment must *start* by extending the held-out-hand suite (≥4-5 held-out hands, shared across all four arms), or downgrade the claim from "resolving experiment" to "first resolvable on a benchmark that does not yet exist."
3. **Fix the circular secondary metric.** The product-score (H2) is validated against AffordSim's manual-annotation oracle, which the card itself flags as the ceiling. Specify an *independent* functional-intent ground truth (the card gestures at OakInk, line 178) as the actual target, or drop the ≥93% number as un-testable.
4. **Resolve the internal contradiction** between finding 2 ("one ablation cell") and the actionable insight ("a four-way matrix + few-shot curve = a publishable paper"). Pick one: either the wedge is one cheap cell (then it is thin novelty, weakening the COMMIT) or it is a full matrix (then "single cell" mischaracterizes it). The briefing wants both.
5. **Down-weight the Frontier question's implied verdict.** GTP-FA is already in A1's table and the card explicitly cedes the control cycle to D1 by design; the "optimizing a distribution when it should optimize a controller" reframe is a legitimate research question but is presented as if it indicts a blind spot the card actually documented and scoped out.

---

### A1 (Direction review)

Topic: A1 — Task-Affordance-Conditioned Grasp Synthesis. The bet (verbatim): "Task-affordance is the cross-morphology invariant and grasp quality is product-separable. (1) An affordance-conditioned generator transfers parallel-jaw→dexterous (SynGrasp-1B→Dex1B) with ≥10 pp less degradation than the stable-grasp geometry; (2) a product scorer Q(g)=Q_stable(g)·Q_task(g) recovers ≥93% of manual-annotation success (AffordSim's oracle) with zero per-object labels, at Dex1B-class stability (86.0% DexGraspNet)."

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys grasp policies daily).**
- *Core position*: The conditioning headline is already shippable: IFG (2511.09558) and AffordSim (2604.11674) both put a VLM/affordance signal in front of a force-closure generator and get functional grasps with no manual data. What A1 adds (a *tunable* exposed product score + a clean cross-morph transfer claim) is a research nicety, not a deployment blocker, and the real cost lives where the card barely looks: the affordance model's per-scene latency and its failure mode in clutter.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: AffordSim's own ablation says VoxAfford accuracy is "the primary success factor," and its real-Franka zero-shot averages only 24% (hanging 10%) despite 64-79% sim. That gap is the affordance model collapsing on real depth, not the grasp generator. IFG reports per-grasp real-time inference only *after* distilling the offline VLM+sim pipeline into a diffusion model: the expensive part is offline.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: The H2/H3 experiments are all single-frame open-loop grasp-pose metrics (DexGraspNet GSR, displacement, penetration). None of them include the post-grasp manipulation where AffordSim says failures actually concentrate ("precise post-grasp alignment and long-horizon action chains"). A label-free product score that wins on grasp-pose SR can still lose every downstream task — instrument the *whole* task, not the grasp.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
- *Core position*: The card is honest that conditioning is settled (AffordDexGrasp 2503.07360, AffordGrasp 2603.08021, Grasp-as-You-Say 2405.19291) and correctly localizes surviving novelty to H2 (product separability) and H3 (affordance-as-invariant). But the literature already contains the *components* of both, so the contribution is a measurement/isolation paper, not a new mechanism — and it must be framed that way to survive review.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: FSAG (2601.08246) literally "disentangles grasp semantics from specific hand kinematics" and transfers to a different dexterous hand (Linker L20) by swapping only the kinematic model — that is the H3 *claim* qualitatively demonstrated. IFG (2511.09558) is a de-facto Q_stable·Q_task (VLM useful-region × force-closure). The novel act is the controlled head-to-head (affordance-degradation vs geometry-degradation) and exposing the product as a tunable Pareto front, neither of which FSAG/IFG run.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: H3 as written conflates two different transfers. "Parallel-jaw→dexterous" (SynGrasp-1B→Dex1B) is a *kinematic-class* jump, but the card's own best evidence (FSAG, FunGrasp 2411.16755, EAGG 2606.18092) is all *dexterous→dexterous*. There is no clean baseline where an affordance-conditioned generator and a stable-geometry generator are trained on the *same* parallel-jaw data and evaluated zero-shot on the *same* dexterous hand — H3's falsifier needs that matched pair to exist, and it does not yet.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks the bet is wrong or already solved).**
- *Core position*: "Affordance is the cross-morphology invariant" is partly a tautology and partly already-claimed. It is near-tautological because affordance is *defined* on the object ("a hammer is gripped by the handle"), so of course it is hand-agnostic — the open question was never whether the *intent* transfers but whether the *realized grasp* transfers, and that is gated by hand-specific kinematics that the affordance latent cannot supply. The crowded cross-embodiment generator field (EAGG, UniMorphGrasp 2602.00915, CEDex 2509.24661, UniFucGrasp 2508.03339) already gets cross-hand transfer by conditioning on structure, so "≥10 pp less degradation than geometry" risks being a strawman: nobody serious transfers raw force-closure geometry across morphologies anyway.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: UniMorphGrasp (2602.00915, surfaced via alphaxiv, NOT in the card) reports topological/geometrical/embodiment zero-shot transfer to *unseen* hands with only 0.5-16.1% degradation — using morphology-aware structural conditioning and *no* affordance term at all. If structure-conditioned stable generators already transfer this well, the "geometry degrades much more" half of H3 is in question before A1 runs.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: The H2 product Q_stable·Q_task is an assumed functional form. If affordance and stability genuinely conflict (the card's own Risk #3 admits the functional grasp "may be less stable"), a *product* is the wrong model — a product says the optimum is where both are jointly high, but tool-use grasps are often a deliberate *trade* (you accept lower stability for function). The falsifier ("<93% of oracle → not separable") could fire simply because the true combiner is a constrained max, not a product.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
- *Core position*: "Affordance" is the current fundable narrative because it converts the expensive bottleneck (manual functional-grasp annotation) into a free VLM call — every paper here (AffordSim, IFG, GraspMolmo, SynGrasp-1B) is really selling *label-free data generation*, and A1's "zero per-object labels" hook rides that wave. The incentive gradient pushes toward benchmark-private wins (AffordSim's own 50-task split) over hard reproducibility.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The card's headline numbers (79%/64% vs AnyGrasp 15%/3%) are all on AffordSim's *self-defined* benchmark; the public anchors (OakInk 2203.15709, DexGraspNet 2210.02697, TD-TOG 70.9% 2506.05576) carry far less dramatic gaps. The 15%→79% jump is the marketing number; the OakInk/TaskGrasp deltas are the real ones. Whoever owns the affordance *model* (VoxAfford-style) owns the ceiling, so the value accrues to the affordance-foundation-model builders, not to the grasp-synthesis layer A1 sits in.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: A1 is structurally a *measurement* paper (does the product factor? does affordance transfer?), and measurement papers are cheap to run but hard to cite unless they ship a reusable artifact. The defensible economic moat is not the hypothesis result — it is publishing the *tunable Q Pareto front + the matched parallel-jaw→dexterous transfer benchmark* that the field currently lacks, because that becomes the thing others must compare against.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the task-oriented-grasp-quality-metric wave from 2019 (1907.04761, "Task Oriented Grasp Quality Metrics") returning with diffusion generators and VLM affordance maps — the *question* (a grasp's quality is relative to its task, not absolute) is 7 years old; what changed is the conditioning machinery. The "separable Q" instinct echoes the classical grasp-wrench-space × task-wrench-space decomposition that pre-dates deep learning.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: 1907.04761 already defined task-relative grasp quality; the differentiable-force-closure line (2104.09194, arbitrary hand structures) already solved cross-morphology *stable* synthesis analytically in 2021. The field's swing from analytic metrics → learned filters → generative conditioning mirrors the perception-vs-end-to-end and pixel-vs-latent cycles: each wave re-absorbs the prior wave's hand-built structure into the learned model.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: Every prior "X is the invariant" claim in robotics (DR-is-the-invariant, latent-is-the-invariant, contact-is-the-invariant) survived only when someone produced the *negative control* showing the alternative genuinely fails. A1 will be remembered for the falsifier, not the confirmation: the experiment that matters is the one where geometry transfer is given its *best* shot (structure-conditioned, à la UniMorphGrasp) and still loses to affordance — anything weaker is the strawman the field already saw through in the modular-vs-end-to-end debates.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *Skeptic vs Academic/Practitioner on H3's strength*: Skeptic says structure-conditioned stable generators (UniMorphGrasp: 0.5-16.1% degradation to unseen hands, no affordance) already transfer well, so "geometry degrades ≥10 pp more" is a strawman. Academic/Practitioner counter that the *fair* geometry baseline is exactly such a structure-conditioned generator, and FSAG/FunGrasp show affordance-alone transfers with *no* per-hand structural retraining — the clash is over what counts as the geometry baseline.
   - *Skeptic vs the card on H2's functional form*: card asserts a *product* Q_stable·Q_task; Skeptic says affordance/stability conflict (card's own Risk #3) implies a *trade*, so a product mis-specifies the combiner and could falsify H2 for the wrong reason.
   - *Economist vs Practitioner on where value sits*: Economist says the moat is the reusable benchmark/Pareto artifact; Practitioner says none of the single-frame grasp metrics predict downstream task success, so even the artifact may not transfer to deployment value.

2. **Strongest vs weakest.**
   - *Strongest*: ACADEMIC — it is the only perspective that both concedes the settled conditioning result and pinpoints the exact missing experiment (a matched parallel-jaw→dexterous pair with affordance-on/off), grounded in real card papers (FSAG, FunGrasp, EAGG).
   - *Weakest*: HISTORIAN — directionally right that the question is old (1907.04761) but it does not change the commit decision; the 2019 metric and 2021 differentiable-force-closure are context, not a refutation of the label-free generative claim.

3. **Question that resolves the biggest contradiction (H3 strawman vs real wedge)**: When the geometry baseline is given its *best* modern form — a structure-conditioned cross-embodiment stable generator (UniMorphGrasp / EAGG-stable / CEDex) trained on the same parallel-jaw data — does an affordance-conditioned generator still degrade ≥10 pp less on a held-out dexterous hand, or does structural conditioning close the gap to <10 pp? This single matched experiment decides whether H3 is whitespace or a strawman.

4. **Universal agreement (likely true)**: Affordance-as-generative-conditioning beats no-affordance and beats post-hoc filtering on functional/affordance-critical tasks, and it can be sourced label-free from a VLM. Every perspective accepts this (it is the *settled background*, H1), and the card itself classifies it as consensus.

5. **Blind spot (addressed by none)**: Temporal/closed-loop and post-contact behavior. The entire direction — every hypothesis, every metric — is about the *static grasp pose at contact*. None of the five perspectives, nor the card, addresses whether an affordance-correct grasp pose actually enables the *subsequent functional motion* (pour, hammer, cut) under contact dynamics, force, and slip. AffordSim's own data says failures concentrate post-grasp, yet no H tests the affordance→manipulation handoff.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**(1) CEO summary.** A1 is a well-aimed *measurement* bet, not a new-mechanism bet, and it knows it: the card correctly retires "affordance conditioning works" as settled and stakes its novelty on two isolations — that task-affordance is the term that survives a morphology change where stable geometry does not (H3), and that grasp quality factors into a tunable, label-free product Q_stable·Q_task (H2). Both survive a literature check as *unrun controlled experiments*, but each is one fair baseline away from collapsing into a strawman: H3 must beat a modern *structure-conditioned* stable generator (UniMorphGrasp/EAGG/CEDex), not raw force-closure geometry, and H2's product form may mis-specify a stability/function trade-off the card itself flags. The defensible deliverable is the artifact — a matched parallel-jaw→dexterous transfer benchmark plus an exposed Q Pareto front — more than any single confirmatory number.

**(2) Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
- *F1 (highest)*: Affordance conditioning, sourced label-free, beats no-affordance and beats filtering on affordance-critical tasks. Supported by all five perspectives and the card (settled, H1). Challenged by: none — but Practitioner notes the win is single-frame, not task-level.
- *F2*: The novelty is real but narrow — it is an isolation/measurement contribution, because the *components* of H2 (IFG's VLM×force-closure) and H3 (FSAG's affordance-from-kinematics disentanglement, FunGrasp's cross-hand functional transfer) already exist. Supported by Academic, Economist, Historian. Challenged by: the card's framing that H2/H3 are open (true only at the *controlled-test* level, not the component level).
- *F3*: H3's "≥10 pp less degradation than geometry" is at risk of being a strawman, because structure-conditioned stable generators (UniMorphGrasp: 0.5-16.1% degradation to unseen hands, no affordance term) already transfer well. Supported by Skeptic, Historian; surfaced via alphaxiv (2602.00915 not in card). Challenged by: Academic/Practitioner (FSAG/FunGrasp transfer affordance with *no* per-hand structural retraining, a real distinction).
- *F4*: The product form Q_stable·Q_task may mis-model a stability/function *trade*; the H2 falsifier could fire for the wrong reason. Supported by Skeptic, and by the card's own Risk #3. Challenged by: the card's mitigation (make Q a tunable product / Pareto front), which partially absorbs the objection.
- *F5 (lowest, but most consequential)*: All metrics are static grasp-pose metrics; none test the affordance→post-contact-manipulation handoff where AffordSim says failures concentrate. Supported by Practitioner; implied by AffordSim's 24% real / 10% hanging numbers. Challenged by: none directly — it is the blind spot, so it is unvalidated rather than contested.

**(3) Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Economist's "the value is the reusable artifact, not the result" and the Historian's "you're remembered for the falsifier, not the confirmation" are the same claim from different angles: because the confirmatory result is cheap and partly pre-empted (F2), A1's citable, defensible output is the *negative-control infrastructure* — the matched parallel-jaw→dexterous benchmark with affordance on/off and a best-effort structure-conditioned geometry baseline. The Skeptic's strawman warning (F3) is precisely what that infrastructure neutralizes: building the fair geometry baseline *is* the contribution.

**(4) Actionable insight — DE-RISK (do not DROP, do not blind-COMMIT).** The bet is worth pursuing but must be re-scoped from "two hypotheses" to "one falsifier-grade experiment + one artifact." Concrete next experiment: run H3 as a *matched* control — train two generators on the *same* parallel-jaw grasp data (SynGrasp-1B-style), one affordance-conditioned, one given the *strongest* modern stable baseline (structure/URDF-conditioned, in the spirit of UniMorphGrasp 2602.00915 / EAGG 2606.18092 / CEDex 2509.24661, NOT raw force-closure), and evaluate both zero-shot on a held-out dexterous hand on AffordSim's hard tier *re-confirmed on public OakInk*. Pre-register the ≥10 pp threshold against this *strong* baseline. In parallel, replace H2's hard product assumption with a learned/tunable combiner and report the stability-function Pareto front (absorbing Risk #3). Gate the full commit on the matched-H3 result: if affordance still wins ≥10 pp against structure-conditioned geometry, commit hard; if <10 pp, the contribution downgrades to the product-score + benchmark artifact and H3 is dropped. Add at least one post-contact task-completion metric so the result is not purely grasp-pose-static (closes the blind spot).

**(5) Frontier question.** Is task-affordance a genuinely *separable* representational axis from embodiment kinematics — such that a single affordance latent + a swappable kinematic model reconstructs functional grasps across arbitrary hands (the FSAG conjecture taken to its limit) — or is functional grasp success *irreducibly coupled* to hand-specific contact geometry, so that any "affordance invariant" is only as good as the per-hand kinematic decoder bolted onto it? Resolving this decides whether affordance is the field's cross-morphology substrate or merely a better conditioning signal on top of per-hand machinery.

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

Independent peer review of the A1 briefing ("Task-Affordance-Conditioned Grasp Synthesis", `Capability/Manipulation.md` L106-201). I read the card and spot-checked the load-bearing numbers against the KnowledgeHub notes (`2602.00915`, `2606.18092`, `2601.08246`, `2511.09558`, `2604.11674`). The briefing is unusually honest for a self-assessment and its central move — "this is a measurement bet, the artifact is the deliverable" — is correct. But it leans on its own strongest framing in two places and one verified number is not actually in the source.

##### 1. Confidence scores (1-10 reliability)

- **F1 — affordance conditioning beats no-affordance and beats filtering on affordance-critical tasks: 9/10.** This is settled, multiply-sourced (AffordDexGrasp, AffordGrasp, Grasp-as-You-Say), and the card correctly retires it as background (H1). The Practitioner's caveat (single-frame, not task-level) is the only real qualifier and it is logged. Lose one point only because "beats filtering" rests largely on AffordSim's own benchmark numbers (79/64 vs 15/3), which is a single benchmark's framing; the OakInk re-confirmation is proposed, not done.

- **F2 — novelty is real but narrow (isolation/measurement, components pre-exist): 8/10.** Verified directly from KH: FSAG (2601.08246) literally states it "disentangles grasp semantics from specific hand kinematics" and transfers to a different dexterous hand "without retraining" — that is H3's mechanism. IFG (2511.09558) is VLM-useful-regions × force-closure optimization — a de-facto Q_stable·Q_task. So the claim that H2/H3's *components* exist is correct, and the card itself concedes this in its "What's missing" columns. The framing tension the briefing flags (card says "fully open", true only at controlled-test level) is a fair catch. Docked for a definitional soft spot: "narrow" is a value judgment, not a measured quantity — a measurement/isolation contribution can be high-value, so "narrow" understates as easily as it warns.

- **F3 — H3's "≥10 pp less degradation than geometry" risks being a strawman: 7/10.** This is the most important finding and it is substantively correct: UniMorphGrasp (2602.00915) and EAGG (2606.18092) are strong structure-conditioned cross-morphology generators with NO affordance term, and they live in cluster A2 of the same document (L248-260), not in A1's H3 comparator set. A1's H3 (L181-185) compares affordance against generic "stable-grasp geometry", which is exactly the weak baseline the briefing warns about. Verified problem. BUT: the specific figure "UniMorphGrasp: 0.5-16.1% degradation to unseen hands" is **not in the KH note** — the note reports absolute zero-shot SR (87.4% / 91.3%) and qualitative "limited performance degradation", not a 0.5-16.1% delta. That number appears to be an external/inferred figure presented as if sourced. The *direction* of F3 is sound; the *quantification* is unverified and should be flagged.

- **F4 — product form Q_stable·Q_task may mis-model the trade-off; falsifier could fire for the wrong reason: 6/10.** Logically valid and the card's own Risk #3 (L200) concedes a functional grasp may be less stable, so a hard product penalizes a correct grasp for low stability. The briefing is right that the tunable-Pareto mitigation only partially absorbs this. Docked because it is a speculative "may" — there is no evidence yet that the product mis-specifies, and a multiplicative form is a defensible first hypothesis. This is a design-risk flag, not a finding about reality.

- **F5 — all metrics are static grasp-pose metrics; the affordance→post-contact handoff is untested: 8/10.** Verified and consequential. AffordSim's KH note independently states failures "concentrated in precise post-grasp alignment and long-horizon action chains", real avg 24%, hanging 10% — exactly the numbers F5 cites. The H1-H5 tests (L171-195) are indeed all static grasp-quality metrics (SR, P-Vol, KLD, force-closure); none score task completion after contact. This is a real blind spot, not a contested claim. Docked one point because it is the standard grasp-synthesis scope (A1 is a *grasp synthesis* card, not a manipulation-policy card) — so it is a scope-boundary critique as much as a defect, and the fix (add ≥1 post-contact metric) is cheap.

**Mean = 7.6, Min = 6 (F4).**

##### 2. Weakest link

The least-confident *claim as stated* is **F3's specific "0.5-16.1% degradation" figure for UniMorphGrasp**. The KH note does not contain this delta — it reports absolute zero-shot SR and a qualitative "limited degradation". To verify: pull the UniMorphGrasp paper's cross-morphology table and compute the seen→unseen-hand SR drop per hand, then confirm it is genuinely <16 pp without an affordance term. If that holds, F3 is airtight and the strawman warning is decisive; if the real degradation is larger (or only measured on easy object sets), the "geometry already transfers well" premise weakens and A1's H3 is less pre-empted than the briefing implies. Either way the *qualitative* F3 (strong structure-conditioned baselines exist and are not A1's comparator) is already verified from the A2 cluster, so the conclusion survives — only the magnitude is at risk.

##### 3. Bias check

Mildly **Skeptic-/Historian-dominated**. The synthesis's spine — "the confirmatory result is cheap and pre-empted, so the citable output is the negative-control artifact" — is the Skeptic's strawman warning (F3) and the Historian's "remembered for the falsifier" fused into the actionable insight. That fusion is elegant and the "hidden connection" explicitly names it, but it tilts the whole recommendation toward de-risking-by-building-a-harder-baseline. The Economist's "value is the artifact" is recruited to the same conclusion rather than offering independent tension. The Academic and Practitioner are present but subordinated: the Academic's genuine counter (FSAG/FunGrasp transfer affordance with no per-hand structural retraining — a real distinction from URDF-conditioned geometry) is logged as a "challenge" to F3 but does not move the final verdict, which still treats H3 as near-strawman. A more balanced synthesis would weight the Academic's point harder: if FSAG already shows affordance-alone transfer with a swappable kinematic decoder, then A1's H3 may be *confirmatory of FSAG* rather than strawman-vs-geometry — a different framing than "build a stronger geometry baseline."

##### 4. Missing perspective

A **measurement-validity / benchmark-construction perspective** (a 6th angle) would change the conclusions. The entire de-risk recommendation hinges on building a "matched parallel-jaw→dexterous transfer benchmark" and pre-registering a ≥10 pp threshold. But nobody in the five perspectives asks whether that benchmark is *constructible as a fair test*: SynGrasp-1B is parallel-jaw 2D-box→3D-grasp CoT, Dex1B is high-DoF CVAE with SDF feasibility — they do not share an object set, a success criterion, or an affordance annotation, so "train both generators on the same parallel-jaw data and evaluate zero-shot on a held-out dexterous hand" smuggles in a large harness-design problem (how is "the same affordance signal" instantiated for a 2-finger vs 5-finger embodiment, and is the ≥10 pp threshold meaningful when the two generators' SR scales differ?). If the matched benchmark cannot be built without arbitrary normalization choices, the falsifier-grade experiment degrades into a confound, and the "artifact is the deliverable" thesis loses its teeth — because a *contestable* benchmark is not a citable negative control. This perspective would push the actionable insight from "run the matched control" to "first prove the matched control is identifiable", which is a different next step.

##### 5. Overall grade and top fixes

**Grade: B+.** This is a strong, self-aware briefing: it correctly retires the settled headline, isolates the two surviving claims, finds a real strawman risk that the card itself missed (F3 via the A2 cluster), and lands a genuinely useful re-scope (one falsifier + one artifact, with a clean commit/downgrade gate). The reasoning chain is honest and the hidden-connection synthesis is sharp. It falls short of A-range on three things.

Top fixes, in order:
1. **Flag the unsourced number.** "0.5-16.1% degradation" is presented as UniMorphGrasp's result but is not in the source note (which gives absolute SR). Either cite the table it comes from or mark it as an external/estimated figure — otherwise the strongest finding rests on an unverifiable stat.
2. **Confront the benchmark-constructibility problem** (the missing 6th perspective) before pre-registering ≥10 pp. The de-risk plan assumes the matched harness is fair; demonstrate it is identifiable (shared object/affordance/success protocol across embodiments) or the falsifier is a confound.
3. **Re-weight the Academic's counter on F3.** Decide explicitly whether A1's H3 is "affordance vs strong geometry" (strawman risk) or "affordance-alone à la FSAG, confirming a swappable-kinematic-decoder conjecture" (genuine, but then A1 is partly replicating FSAG and must say what it adds). The briefing's frontier question already poses exactly this dichotomy — fold that resolution back into H3's framing rather than leaving it as an open coda.

### A2 (Direction review)

Topic: A2 — Cross-Morphology Grasp Transfer. The bet (verbatim): *"A discrete grasp-taxonomy latent (power/precision/lateral type + continuous force) transfers grasp-establishment to unseen hands better than continuous FAAS at matched data, beating UniDex's 60%/40% zero-shot, because the discrete bottleneck strips the hand-specific residue continuous spaces retain — at ≥5× lower per-hand cost and DexUMI-class in-domain SR (86%)."* Everything else in the card (function-space-transfers-cross-hand) is conceded as settled; H2 is the entire surviving novelty.

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies).**
- *Core position*: The interesting number is not zero-shot, it is few-shot-to-deploy and the discrete latent's worst failure mode is silent: a 3-way grasp-type code that picks the wrong type on a novel hand fails catastrophically (drops the object) rather than degrading gracefully, whereas a continuous space interpolates. On real hardware the bottleneck is rarely the action coordinate; it is contact-state estimation and the sim-to-real gap on the *new* hand's tendons/backlash.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: UniDex's headline zero-shot is 60% (Oymotion) / 40% (Wuji) — the card's own Risk row concedes "40% is a research result." DexUMI hits 86% in-domain but is exoskeleton-mediated, i.e. it needs a per-hand exoskeleton built, which is exactly the per-hand cost the bet claims to kill. AnyDexGrasp already cuts per-hand data to "hundreds of attempts" at 75–95% — so the ≥5× data-cut target is already beaten by a continuous contact-rep two-stage method without any taxonomy.
- *Only-this-perspective*: A discrete grasp-type code adds a discrete decision boundary that you cannot calibrate or smooth on a new hand without a few demos anyway, so the "zero-shot" framing is the wrong KPI; measure type-misclassification rate on held-out hands, because that is what will actually drop objects in the lab.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the literature).**
- *Core position*: The card's framing — "continuous-vs-discrete is the comparison no one has run" — is literally true as stated, but the literature already supplies the mechanism that would make discreteness lose: the strongest continuous transfers (DexGrasp-Zero 85% sim / 82% real zero-shot; D(R,O) 87.5%/89% over 3 hands) win precisely by encoding *interaction geometry / contact*, not joint angles, so they have already "stripped hand-specific residue" without a discrete code. Discreteness is a stronger inductive bias only if grasp *type* is the right quotient — but for cross-hand transfer the quotient that demonstrably works is contact/geometry, not the power/precision/lateral label.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: Sparse Taxonomy Grasp (2604.04138) does use 30 discrete templates and gets 87.9% Objaverse +28.57% contact precision — but per the KH note it is single-hand controllability, never a transfer test. Dexonomy (2504.18829, alphaxiv-confirmed) synthesizes all 31 GRASP-taxonomy types and trains a *type-conditional* generative model (82.3% real, Shadow) — but the taxonomy is a per-hand conditioning label generated from one template *per hand per type*; it never claims, nor tests, that the discrete code transfers cross-hand better than a continuous one.
- *Only-this-perspective*: The honest novelty is not "discrete beats continuous" (likely false at the limit) but "a discrete grasp-type bottleneck is a *competitive and more interpretable* cross-hand carrier" — reframe the contribution as an interpretability/controllability win with a transfer floor, because the raw SR comparison probably won't favor you against DexGrasp-Zero.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks it's wrong or solved).**
- *Core position*: The whitespace is a definitional artifact: the card concedes function-space transfer is "settled," then carves out the one un-run A/B (discrete vs continuous) and calls it novel — but a null or negative result there ("the discrete latent loses to FAAS") is the *likely* outcome by the card's own falsifier, and a paper whose central hypothesis is most-likely-false is a weak bet. Worse, three discrete-taxonomy systems (Sparse Taxonomy Grasp, Dexonomy, OmniDexVLG 2512.03874) already exist, so "no one has built the discrete stack" is false; only the head-to-head transfer table is missing, which is an experiment, not a discovery.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The card's H2 falsifier ("discrete loses → continuous already captures the invariant") combined with the field consensus it cites (DexGrasp-Zero +59.5pp over CrossDex; UniMorphGrasp 87.4–91.3% zero-shot to novel morphologies; EAGG within 1.10pp of specialists) means the continuous bar is *very* high and still rising. A discrete 3-class+force code throws away exactly the continuous force-distribution detail those methods exploit.
- *Only-this-perspective*: Run the cheap version of H2 first as a kill-switch — if a continuous FAAS already matches a taxonomy-conditioned policy on one held-out hand in sim, do not write the paper; the entire bet rests on a single uncertain inequality and you can resolve it in a week.

**4. ECONOMIST (money/incentives).**
- *Core position*: "Cross-morphology transfer" is a hardware-vendor and foundation-model narrative — every new dexterous hand (LEAP, Inspire, Wuji, Oymotion, XHand) needs a policy, and whoever owns the universal action space owns the moat, which is why six labs shipped a continuous universal coordinate in ~6 months (UniDex, DexGrasp-Zero, UniMorphGrasp, Canonical Hand, DexFormer, EAGG). The discrete-taxonomy angle is cheap to publish (no new hardware, reuses CrossDex's 45-object YCB protocol) which is *why* it's attractive to an academic, and also why it's crowded.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The card lists ~35 papers on this axis, the bulk from 2025-2026, clustered on the *exact same* benchmark (CrossDex's 45-object YCB held-out-hand split). That is textbook benchmark-saturation: the field is racing on one protocol where +1-2pp is publishable, which deflates the marginal value of yet another coordinate.
- *Only-this-perspective*: The defensible economic value is not the coordinate but the *protocol* — A2's real asset is being the first to run the discrete-vs-continuous-vs-implicit-vs-joint-space matched-data shoot-out on a fixed held-out hand; sell the benchmark/ablation, not the method, because the method is a commodity and the comparison is the scarce good.

**5. HISTORIAN (prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the eigengrasp cycle restarting. The continuous-low-dimensional-synergy idea is Santello's 1998 postural synergies → CrossDex's eigengrasps (2410.02479); the discrete-grasp-taxonomy idea is Cutkosky's 1989 / Feix's GRASP taxonomy. Robotics has oscillated between "discrete grasp primitives" and "continuous synergy spaces" for thirty years, and the continuous side has won every time scale and data arrived, because discreteness is a low-data prior that gets dominated once you can fit the continuous manifold.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The card's own consensus rows are the continuous side winning *now*: eigengrasps (CrossDex), FAAS (UniDex), PCA control basis (EAGG), VAE latent (Canonical Hand 16-d), morphology-graph diffusion (UniMorphGrasp). The discrete entrant (Sparse Taxonomy Grasp) is used only for *control/interpretability* — exactly the niche discrete primitives have historically retreated to (human-legible structure), not raw performance.
- *Only-this-perspective*: Bet on the hybrid the way the field eventually always does — discrete type as a *coarse prior / mode selector* over a continuous residual — because pure-discrete-beats-continuous is the side of this cycle that has lost before; frame H2 as "discrete prior + continuous force residual" and you're betting with history instead of against it.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *Academic vs the card's H2*: card claims discreteness "strips residue continuous spaces retain → cleaner carrier"; Academic + Historian say the *winning* continuous methods already strip residue via contact/geometry (DexGrasp-Zero, D(R,O)), so discreteness buys interpretability, not transfer SR.
   - *Practitioner vs Card on the KPI*: card optimizes zero-shot SR vs UniDex's 60/40; Practitioner says zero-shot is the wrong metric (type-misclassification + sim-to-real on the new hand dominate) and DexUMI's 86% needs a per-hand exoskeleton, undercutting the "≥5× lower per-hand cost" claim.
   - *Skeptic vs Economist on whitespace*: Skeptic says the un-run A/B is "an experiment not a discovery" and most-likely-null; Economist says the un-run A/B is *exactly* the scarce, publishable asset (the protocol). Same fact, opposite verdict on whether it's worth committing to.

2. **Strongest vs weakest.** Strongest: **Academic** — it is the only perspective that names the concrete mechanism (contact/geometry already strips residue) and grounds it in the specific winning numbers (DexGrasp-Zero 85/82, D(R,O) 87.5/89), turning a vibe into a falsifiable prediction about *why* H2 likely loses. Weakest: **Economist** — correct about saturation and incentives but offers no evidence about whether the bet is *true*, only about whether it's *fundable*; useful for framing, not for the go/no-go.

3. **Resolving question.** On a single fixed held-out hand (LEAP) at matched data on CrossDex's 45-object YCB split: does a discrete grasp-type+force latent beat continuous FAAS zero-shot, tie it, or lose — and if it ties/loses, does a discrete-prior + continuous-residual hybrid beat *both*? That one ablation collapses the card's H2, the Academic's mechanism claim, and the Historian's hybrid bet into a single table.

4. **Universal agreement (likely true).** Every perspective agrees the *non-discrete* premise is settled and real: function/contact/geometry-space transfer to unseen hands works, the per-hand-dataset-per-policy assumption is genuinely refuted (CrossDex, DexGrasp-Zero, UniMorphGrasp, AnyDexGrasp), and the continuous bar is high (~80%+ in-domain, 35-85% zero-shot depending on protocol). The cluster's *background* is solid; only the discrete-beats-continuous foreground is contested.

5. **Blind spot (none addressed).** Object/task *semantics of the grasp type itself*: who decides power-vs-precision-vs-lateral for a novel object, and is that selector (a VLM, per Dexonomy/OmniDexVLG) the real cross-hand bottleneck rather than the latent's continuity? Also unaddressed: dynamic/under-actuated and tendon-coupled hands where "joint-space" isn't even well-defined, which may break the whole coordinate framing before discreteness matters. No perspective costed the *force* half of the latent — "continuous force" on a hand with no force sensor (the E1 problem) is assumed free.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

1. **CEO summary.** A2's foundation is sound but its *only* novel claim is fragile. The card honestly concedes that cross-hand transfer in a continuous function/contact space is now settled science (CrossDex, DexGrasp-Zero, UniDex, EAGG, UniMorphGrasp), and stakes the paper on one un-run comparison: that a *discrete* grasp-type+force latent transfers to unseen hands *better* than continuous FAAS at matched data. Literature triangulation says the discrete stack already exists (Sparse Taxonomy Grasp, Dexonomy, OmniDexVLG) but no one has run it *as a cross-hand transfer test*, so the whitespace is real-but-narrow — an experiment, not a discovery — and by the card's own falsifier the most likely outcome (continuous wins on raw SR) kills the headline. The bet is salvageable only if reframed: as the field's first matched-data discrete-vs-continuous-vs-implicit shoot-out (the scarce asset is the protocol) and/or as a discrete-prior + continuous-force-residual hybrid (betting with the historical cycle, not against it).

2. **Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
   - (i) *Function/contact-space cross-hand transfer is settled; the per-hand-policy assumption is refuted.* Support: all five perspectives + card. Challenge: none. Most reliable — this is the safe background, not the contribution.
   - (ii) *The discrete-vs-continuous transfer A/B has genuinely not been run.* Support: Academic, Skeptic, Economist, alphaxiv (Dexonomy/Sparse-Taxonomy are per-hand/control-only, not cross-hand transfer tests). Challenge: mild — it is an experiment, so novelty is "first to run" not "first to conceive."
