   - (iii) *The discrete latent most-likely loses or ties continuous on raw zero-shot SR.* Support: Academic (contact already strips residue: DexGrasp-Zero 85/82), Historian (continuous wins at scale), Skeptic. Challenge: Card's H2 (discreteness strips more). Medium reliability, decisively testable.
   - (iv) *The "≥5× lower per-hand cost / 86% SR" targets are already met by continuous methods.* Support: Practitioner, Economist (AnyDexGrasp hundreds-of-attempts 75-95%; DexUMI 86% but needs exoskeleton). Challenge: card frames these as targets-to-beat. Medium-high.
   - (v) *A discrete-prior + continuous-residual hybrid is the likely winner.* Support: Historian, Academic (implicitly), Practitioner (graceful degradation). Challenge: untested, speculative. Lowest reliability but highest upside — this is the actual paper.

3. **Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Practitioner's failure-mode worry (a wrong discrete type = catastrophic drop, no graceful degradation) and the Historian's thirty-year cycle (discrete primitives always retreat to the interpretability niche once continuous spaces scale) are the *same* phenomenon: discreteness is a low-data prior with a hard decision boundary, which is exactly why it loses on raw SR and survives only as a coarse mode-selector. Both point, independently, at the hybrid as the structurally correct answer — and at the type-*selector* (a VLM, the blind spot) as the true bottleneck.

4. **Actionable insight (COMMIT / DE-RISK / DROP).** **DE-RISK, do not commit yet.** Run the Skeptic's one-week kill-switch *before* writing anything: train taxonomy-conditioned-latent vs continuous-FAAS vs (history-conditioned implicit, à la DexFormer) at *matched data* on CrossDex's 45-object YCB split, train on the 4 seen hands, hold out LEAP + Inspire, measure zero-shot SR. Concrete next experiment + decision rule: if discrete ties-or-loses continuous (the likely case), immediately add the hybrid arm (discrete type prior + continuous force residual) and *also* log type-misclassification rate on the held-out hand — then the paper becomes "discrete prior buys interpretability + competitive transfer + the first matched-data coordinate shoot-out," which survives even when the original H2 inequality fails. If discrete cleanly wins, commit hard to the original framing. DROP only if *both* discrete and the hybrid lose to plain continuous — then the invariant is just contact-geometry and there's no paper.

5. **Frontier question that would change everything.** Is the right cross-hand invariant a property of the *grasp* (type/force/contact) at all, or a property of the *hand-object interaction dynamics* that a world model captures embodiment-free (cf. Scaling Cross-Embodiment World Models, 2511.01177)? If transferring dynamics zero-shot to unseen 6-/12-DoF hands works without *any* grasp coordinate, then the entire function-vs-taxonomy axis A2 lives on is the wrong abstraction, and the cluster's shared premise — that there *is* a grasp invariant to parameterize — is itself the thing to bet against.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics professor. Card under review: `Capability/Manipulation.md` A2 (lines 202-296). Empirical claims in the briefing were spot-checked against the KH notes for 2410.02479, 2502.16420, 2505.21864, 2602.08278, 2603.16806, 2604.04138, 2512.03874, 2511.01177.*

---

##### 1. Confidence scores (1-10 reliability)

| # | Finding | Score | One-line justification |
|---|---|---|---|
| (i) | Function/contact-space cross-hand transfer is settled; per-hand-policy assumption refuted | **9** | The card itself concedes it; five independent transfer results (DexGrasp-Zero 85/82 +59.5pp over CrossDex, Cross-Embodiment-DexGrasp 35.2% zero-shot, UniDex 60/40, Canonical-Hand 71%, EAGG 33.25%) all KH-verified and mutually corroborating. This is background, not contribution — and that is exactly the problem. |
| (ii) | The discrete-vs-continuous transfer A/B has genuinely not been run | **8** | Verified: Sparse-Taxonomy-Grasp (2604.04138) tests taxonomy for *controllability*, OmniDexVLG (2512.03874) is VLM-guided *generation* with taxonomy-accuracy metrics — neither runs a held-out-hand SR comparison. But the briefing's third triangulation anchor, Dexonomy (2504.18829), is NOT in the KH and could not be checked from the vault; novelty is "first to run," which is weak whitespace (an experiment, not a discovery), and the briefing says so honestly. Docked for the unverifiable anchor + the inherently low ceiling of "first-to-run." |
| (iii) | Discrete latent most-likely loses or ties continuous on raw zero-shot SR | **7** | Directionally credible and, crucially, *decisively testable* in one week — the single most valuable property of any finding here. But the supporting mechanism is loosely argued: the briefing cites "DexGrasp-Zero 85/82" as proof "contact already strips residue," yet DexGrasp-Zero is a morphology-graph + motion-primitive method, not a contact-only rep (AnyDexGrasp is the contact-centric one). The conflation does not break the conclusion but reveals the prediction is a prior, not a derivation. |
| (iv) | The "≥5× cost / 86% SR" targets are already met by continuous methods | **7** | Numbers verified (AnyDexGrasp 75-95% / hundreds of attempts; DexUMI 86% but exoskeleton-mediated, NOT zero-shot). Strong as an indictment of the card's target-setting. Held below 8 because "already met" elides the regime mismatch: DexUMI's 86% requires a per-hand exoskeleton and AnyDexGrasp's hundreds-of-attempts is in-domain real-world, neither is the *zero-shot held-out-hand* number the bet actually targets. The targets are soft, but not as trivially beaten as stated. |
| (v) | A discrete-prior + continuous-residual hybrid is the likely winner | **5** | This is a hunch dressed as a conclusion. Zero direct evidence — no paper in the 30-row table runs it, the briefing concedes "untested," and "betting with the historical cycle" is an argument from analogy, not data. Highest upside, lowest reliability; honestly labeled as such. I would not let a student stake a thesis on row (v) without the (iii) kill-switch landing first. |

**meanConfidence = 7.2  lowestConfidence = 5**

---

##### 2. Weakest link

Finding **(v) — the hybrid is the likely winner (confidence 5).** It is the briefing's own nominee for "the actual paper," yet it rests entirely on a thirty-year-cycle analogy and the *assumption* that (iii) lands (discrete loses on raw SR). What would verify it: the proposed one-week kill-switch must (a) actually show discrete ties-or-loses to continuous at matched data on the 45-object YCB held-out split, AND (b) a *third* arm — discrete-type-prior + continuous-force-residual — must beat plain continuous FAAS on the same split, with the type-misclassification rate on the held-out hand logged. Until the hybrid arm is run, (v) is speculation. Secondary weak link: finding (ii)'s Dexonomy (2504.18829) citation is not in the vault and could not be triangulated — the briefing should either ingest it or downgrade it from a load-bearing "the discrete stack already exists" anchor.

---

##### 3. Bias check

**The Skeptic and the Historian over-dominated; the Practitioner is underweighted and the Academic is used selectively.** The synthesis is structurally a teardown: four of five findings (i, ii, iii, iv) are reasons the headline is fragile, and the one constructive finding (v) is explicitly the *least* reliable. That is a Skeptic-led frame — appropriate for a de-risking memo, but it means the briefing never seriously stress-tests the *upside* case for H2 (that a hard discrete bottleneck genuinely could win on a sufficiently out-of-distribution hand, e.g. the underactuated hands UniFucGrasp/2508.03339 covers that continuous eigengrasp methods omit). The Historian's "discreteness always retreats to the interpretability niche" is elegant but is an inductive prior doing the work of evidence, and it is allowed to settle (iii) and seed (v) without an adversary. The Practitioner's genuinely sharp contribution — the catastrophic-wrong-type failure mode — is folded into the "hidden connection" as confirmation rather than examined on its own (a wrong discrete type is a *worse* failure than a continuous near-miss, which is an argument *against* the discrete prior the briefing never scores). The Economist and alphaxiv are used as fact-checkers, not voices. Net: not balanced; it is a well-argued prosecution.

---

##### 4. Missing perspective

**The Benchmark/Protocol Methodologist.** Every finding is conditioned on "matched data on CrossDex's 45-object YCB split, train 4 seen hands, hold out LEAP + Inspire" being a *valid* comparison surface — and nobody audits whether it is. Three unexamined threats: (1) **Matched-data is ill-defined across coordinate systems** — a discrete-taxonomy latent and a continuous FAAS consume data at different sample-efficiencies, so "matched data" could mean matched demos, matched grasp-attempts, or matched information content, and the three give different winners. (2) **Two held-out hands (LEAP + Inspire) is n=2** — the whole conclusion (iii)/(v) could flip on hand selection; LEAP and Inspire are both relatively anthropomorphic, so the protocol may systematically favor whichever coordinate encodes human-hand priors and never test the gripper-side regime (GADP/2502.15613). (3) **The type-misclassification metric the briefing wants to log has no agreed ground truth** — who labels the "correct" grasp type for an unseen hand on a novel object? A methodologist would say the scarce asset the briefing correctly identifies (the protocol) is *also the thing most likely to be quietly broken*, and that the one-week kill-switch could produce a confident-but-artefactual answer. This perspective would change the actionable insight from "run the kill-switch, then decide" to "first prove the protocol discriminates coordinates at all (does FAAS beat joint-space by the predicted margin on *this exact* split? — H1), then run H2."

---

##### 5. Overall grade and top fixes

**Grade: B+.**

This is an unusually honest, well-triangulated briefing — it correctly refuses to rubber-stamp its own card, verifies its numbers (I confirmed the load-bearing ones against the KH), and lands the central diagnosis: A2's novel claim is an un-run experiment whose most likely outcome kills the headline, and the real paper is either the protocol or the hybrid. That diagnosis is correct and valuable. It is held below A- by three things: it lets the Historian's analogy substitute for evidence on the two findings that matter most for the go-forward (iii, v); it never audits the protocol the entire de-risk plan depends on; and it slightly over-claims finding (iv) by treating exoskeleton-gated and in-domain numbers as if they beat the zero-shot target.

**Top fixes, in priority order:**
1. **Add the H1 protocol-validity gate before H2.** Run FAAS-vs-joint-space on the *exact* held-out split first; if FAAS does not beat joint-space by the predicted margin, the split cannot discriminate coordinates and every downstream finding is moot. This is cheaper than the H2 kill-switch and strictly upstream of it.
2. **Define "matched data" operationally and pre-register it** (matched demos vs grasp-attempts vs info), and expand held-out hands beyond n=2 to include at least one strongly non-anthropomorphic / underactuated hand, so (iii) cannot flip on hand selection.
3. **Score the Practitioner's wrong-type failure mode explicitly as a cost** — a catastrophic discrete-type miss vs a graceful continuous near-miss is a quantitative argument against the discrete prior the briefing currently only treats as a downstream bottleneck.
4. **Resolve the Dexonomy (2504.18829) anchor** — ingest it or stop leaning on it for the "discrete stack already exists" claim.
5. **Carry the frontier question into the cluster premise, not just A2.** If 2511.01177's embodiment-free world model transfers dynamics to unseen 6-/12-DoF hands with *no* grasp coordinate, the function-vs-taxonomy axis is the wrong abstraction and Cluster A's shared premise is the thing to bet against — this deserves a sibling card, not a footnote.

*Bottom line: the briefing's own recommendation (de-risk with a one-week kill-switch, reframe the paper as the matched-data shoot-out + hybrid) is the right call. My only structural disagreement is that the kill-switch as specified could give a confident wrong answer because the protocol itself is unaudited — fix that first.*

---

### A3 (Direction review)

Topic: the A3 bet, "dense tactile + a differentiable soft-body world model beats the vision-only stress-RL solution ([[2510.25405|Stress-Guided-RL]]) on held-out / unseen deformables," reviewed as a publication commitment decision for an embodied-AI / robot-learning researcher.

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys deformable-grasp policies).**
- *Core position*: The two distinctive ingredients in the bet, dense tactile and a differentiable soft-body world model, are each individually expensive and brittle to deploy, and stacking them multiplies the failure surface. The vision-only stress-RL incumbent ([[2510.25405|Stress-Guided-RL]]) is winning precisely because it is deliberately model-free and sensor-light, which is why it transfers zero-shot to tofu.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: [[2510.25725|HumanoidVTA]] needed **2,124 tactile sensors** to show dense beats sparse, yet its own ACT policy showed only a "small MAE gap" dense-vs-sparse: the discrimination did not translate to control. [[2302.04659|ManiSkill2]] runs soft-body at ~80 FPS vs ~2000 FPS rigid (the card's own Risk-3), so a differentiable soft-body twin in the control loop is a real-time problem, not a free lunch.
- *Only-this-perspective tells the researcher*: The held-out test that matters commercially is held-out *across the tactile sensor*, not across the object. [[2604.20444|VTouch++]] (120K episodes) and [[2510.25725|HumanoidVTA]] use different sensor hardware; a dense-tactile policy that does not survive a sensor swap is undeployable regardless of held-out-object generalization. Budget the sensor-transfer experiment or the result is a lab artifact.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
- *Core position*: The card has already half-conceded the novelty: it lists [[2410.24091|3D-ViTac]] (dense visuo-tactile fusion, +25-45pp on grape-from-bag and egg-steaming) and [[2510.13324|FARM]] (dense GelSight force-as-action, 95-100% on grape/plant) as papers that *already* beat vision-only on fragile-object control. The live novelty is narrow: the **conjunction** of dense tactile AND a differentiable soft-body model, measured on a **held-out deformable split**, which none of these run.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The dense-tactile-beats-vision claim (H2) is effectively settled by [[2410.24091|3D-ViTac]]'s real-robot ablation (dense-continuous beats sparse/binary; vision-only fails). The differentiable-soft-body half (H3) has strong forward-modeling evidence ([[2601.17251|EMPM]] Chamfer 0.0082 vs PhysTwin 0.0177; [[2512.13214|Differentiable-MPM-Control]] 33x MPPI speedup) but, as the card itself notes, those are demonstrated on *active-damping / shape goals*, "the control loop still to be closed," not on grasp-integrity.
- *Only-this-perspective tells the researcher*: H1 ("force-regulation wins where pose-selection has no target") is dead weight: the card declares it "settled" three times. Including it as a front-line falsifier weakens the paper, a reviewer will read it as padding. The real paper is H2-on-held-out plus H3, nothing else.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks the bet is wrong or already pre-empted).**
- *Core position*: The bet is an unverified conjunction whose two halves point in opposite directions: dense tactile is a *reactive* signal at the contact, a differentiable soft-body model is a *predictive* world model, and the card never argues why combining them beats either alone, much less beats the cheap vision-only incumbent on held-out objects. The whitespace may be an artifact of the held-out-object framing rather than a real mechanism gap.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: alphaxiv surfaced **TacForeSight ([[2606.11184]], 2026-06-09)**, a force-conditioned tactile world model with a flow-matching policy that already does anticipatory (not reactive) tactile-world-model control, 79% over 5 contact-rich tasks, 86.7% under perturbation, beating reactive RDP/FoAR baselines. It pre-empts the "tactile world model" half of the framing, even though it is latent-dynamics not differentiable-physics and tested on rigid insertion/wiping, not fragile deformables. **OmniVTA ([[2603.19201]])** and **Dream-Tac ([[2606.08737]])** are the same wave.
- *Only-this-perspective tells the researcher*: The falsifier the card buries is the cheapest experiment and the one most likely to fire: "if the vision-only stress-RL policy ties dense-tactile+soft-body on held-out deformables, the substrate buys nothing." Given [[2510.25725|HumanoidVTA]]'s dense-doesn't-translate-to-control result, the prior on that tie is *high*. Run it first or the bet is unfounded.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money / incentives).**
- *Core position*: This direction sits at the confluence of two hardware-vendor narratives, dense tactile skins and high-throughput differentiable soft-body sim, both of which have strong supply-side incentives to be declared "necessary." The 4-order-of-magnitude tactile-data gap vs [[2310.08864|OXE]] (cited in the Cluster-E bottleneck) means the data substrate, not the policy, is where value accrues, and data substrates are being built ([[2604.20444|VTouch++]], [[2603.25725|SoftMimicGen]], [[2606.15338|SimWeaver]]).
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The card's own table shows the field racing on the *cheap* axes: [[2606.04269|Instant-Fold]] gets 60.9% zero-shot on 8 unseen garments from one human demo *without any tactile or soft-body model*; [[2604.08544|SIM1]] gets 76% zero-shot at 27x cost cut with synthetic data. The money is flowing to data-engine and one-demo routes that sidestep the bet's expensive substrate entirely.
- *Only-this-perspective tells the researcher*: Frame the contribution as the *standardized held-out fragile-object integrity benchmark* ([[2411.19408|SoGraB]]'s Density-Aware Chamfer, the card's H4) rather than as another tactile policy. A benchmark that forces every dense-tactile and soft-body vendor to report on a common held-out split captures citation value that a single policy paper cannot, and it is the part of A3 nobody else owns.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen the prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the pixel-vs-latent and model-based-vs-model-free world-model cycle replaying on the tactile/soft-body axis. Every wave, the model-based, richer-sensor route wins on paper accuracy (EMPM Chamfer, DexDeform EMD) and loses on deployment to the cheaper reactive route, until the substrate matures, exactly the Dreamer/model-based-RL arc.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: [[2304.03223|DexDeform]] (2023) already showed a differentiable soft-body controller beating RL/trajopt on dexterous deformable tasks (EMD 0.842->0.972), sim-only on plasticine, no real-robot transfer. Three years later the card still lists "control loop still to be closed" against [[2601.17251|EMPM]], [[2602.02402|SoMA-Sim]], [[2606.16202|EgoPhys]]. The model-based deformable-control route has a multi-year history of accuracy-without-deployment.
- *Only-this-perspective tells the researcher*: The winning move historically was not "model-based beats model-free" but "model-based *as a training-time oracle* for a model-free deployable policy", which is *exactly what [[2510.25405|Stress-Guided-RL]] already does* (MPM as a train-time stress oracle). The bet is structurally betting against the lesson its own incumbent embodies. De-risk by asking whether the differentiable soft-body model adds value *at deployment* or only *at training*; if only at training, the incumbent already captured it.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *Academic vs Skeptic on H2*: Academic says dense-tactile-control is settled by [[2410.24091|3D-ViTac]]/[[2510.13324|FARM]] (so H2 is safe background); Skeptic says [[2510.25725|HumanoidVTA]]'s "small MAE gap" shows dense does NOT translate to control on the relevant deformable-soft regime, so H2 on held-out objects is the live risk, not settled. Same papers, opposite reads of whether the dense-control gap transfers to *unseen* deformables.
   - *Historian vs the card on H3*: The card frames differentiable-soft-body-at-deployment as the novel win; Historian says the incumbent [[2510.25405|Stress-Guided-RL]] already extracted soft-body value as a train-time oracle, so the only novelty is deployment-time model use, which has lost every prior cycle.
   - *Practitioner vs Economist on where effort goes*: Practitioner says budget the sensor-transfer experiment (deploy-side); Economist says budget the benchmark (citation-side). Both agree the policy itself is not where the value is.

2. **Strongest vs weakest perspective.**
   - *Strongest*: SKEPTIC, because it is the only one anchored to a paper the card does not cite (TacForeSight [[2606.11184]]) AND to the card's own most-likely-to-fire falsifier plus [[2510.25725|HumanoidVTA]]'s null control result. Its claim is the most evidence-bound and the most decision-relevant.
   - *Weakest*: ECONOMIST, because the "follow the money" reframe to a benchmark is sound strategy but does not actually test whether the bet's *mechanism* is true; it changes the deliverable rather than resolving the scientific question.

3. **The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction (Academic vs Skeptic on H2/H3):** On a held-out deformable split, does dense-tactile + differentiable-soft-body beat vision-only stress-RL ([[2510.25405|Stress-Guided-RL]]) on a *control* success/integrity metric, after the soft-body model's contribution is ablated into train-time-only vs deployment-time? This single experiment separates "settled background" from "live whitespace" and separates "deploy-time model value" from "the incumbent already has it."

4. **What every perspective agrees on (likely true):** Force-regulation beats rigid pose-selection on deformables (H1 is settled, nobody contests it), and the *data/sensor/benchmark substrate*, not the policy architecture, is the field's true bottleneck (the 4-order tactile-data gap, the missing standardized held-out fragile-object integrity metric).

5. **The blind spot none addressed:** The bet is stated entirely in *steady-state* terms (grasp integrity, held-out object), but deformables fail dynamically, during the *contact transition* (the moment of first squeeze, slip onset, plastic-vs-elastic regime change). No perspective and no hypothesis tests whether the dense-tactile + soft-body advantage lives in *transient* contact-state estimation rather than steady-state force-bounding, which is precisely where a *predictive* model should beat a *reactive* one and where TacForeSight already plants a flag.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**CEO summary.** A3's bet is real whitespace but a *narrow and shrinking* one: no single paper combines dense tactile, a differentiable soft-body world model, and a held-out-deformable control evaluation, so the exact conjunction survives a literature check. But the card over-claims by bundling a settled premise (force-regulation, H1) and an already-demonstrated result (dense-tactile control wins on fragile objects, via [[2410.24091|3D-ViTac]] and [[2510.13324|FARM]]) into the "bet," leaving the genuinely novel and genuinely risky core, *does a deployment-time differentiable soft-body model add value over the incumbent's train-time-oracle use of the same physics, on unseen objects*, under-argued. A fast-moving 2026 wave of tactile world models (TacForeSight, OmniVTA, Dream-Tac) is populating the "tactile world model" framing from the rigid-contact side, so the window to claim the deformable/fragile + differentiable-physics corner is months, not years. Commit only if the first experiment is the buried falsifier, not the showcase demo.

**Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *Force-regulation beats pose-selection on deformables (H1) is settled and should be demoted from the bet.* Supported by Academic, Skeptic, Historian, Practitioner (all treat it as background); challenged by none. Evidence: [[2510.25405|Stress-Guided-RL]], [[2509.18830|DexSkin]] (20%->60% integrity), [[2602.10013|Force-Regulated-Manipulation]] (38%->68%).
2. *Dense tactile already beats vision-only on fragile-object control in-distribution; the open question is held-out.* Supported by Academic (cites [[2410.24091|3D-ViTac]] +25-45pp, [[2510.13324|FARM]] 95-100%); challenged by Skeptic via [[2510.25725|HumanoidVTA]]'s null "small MAE gap" on soft objects. Net: H2 is safe in-distribution, live on held-out.
3. *The exact conjunction (dense tactile + differentiable soft-body + held-out control) is unoccupied.* Supported by the alphaxiv discovery sweep: 3D-ViTac/FARM are model-free, EMPM/DexDeform/DiffMPM-Control have no tactile and no closed control loop, TacForeSight/OmniVTA are latent-dynamics not differentiable-physics on rigid tasks. Challenged mildly by Historian (the corner is empty because the route loses).
4. *The differentiable soft-body model's deployment-time value over a train-time oracle is unproven and historically the weak point.* Supported by Historian ([[2304.03223|DexDeform]] 2023 -> still "loop to be closed" in 2026) and the card's own table caveats; challenged by the H3 optimists ([[2601.17251|EMPM]], [[2510.21447|PhysWorld-Deformable]] accuracy numbers). This is the genuine scientific bet and the least reliable claim.
5. *The substrate (sensor-transfer, standardized held-out integrity benchmark), not the policy, is where durable value sits.* Supported by Practitioner and Economist; not contradicted. Evidence: 4-order tactile gap vs [[2310.08864|OXE]], [[2411.19408|SoGraB]] as the unowned metric, sensor heterogeneity across [[2604.20444|VTouch++]]/[[2510.25725|HumanoidVTA]].

**Hidden cross-perspective connection.** Practitioner (sensor-transfer is the real held-out axis), Historian (the winning pattern is model-as-train-oracle, not model-at-deploy), and the blind-spot (failures are transient, not steady-state) converge on one reframing: the defensible A3 contribution is *not* "richer substrate beats cheaper substrate on held-out objects," it is "the differentiable soft-body model earns its deployment cost only on *transient contact-state prediction across an unseen sensor*", a claim that is simultaneously novel, hard for the cheap incumbent to capture, and untouched by the TacForeSight wave (which is rigid + same-sensor).

**Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK, do not yet COMMIT.** The mechanism is plausible whitespace but the most likely outcome of the headline experiment is the falsifier firing (a tie), given [[2510.25725|HumanoidVTA]]. Concrete next experiment, run before committing: on a fixed deformable-grasp task with a SoGraB-scored integrity metric and an explicit train-objects / held-out-objects split, run three policies, (a) vision-only stress-RL ([[2510.25405|Stress-Guided-RL]] reimplementation), (b) dense-tactile model-free ([[2410.24091|3D-ViTac]]/[[2510.13324|FARM]]-style), (c) dense-tactile + differentiable soft-body world model, with the soft-body model ablated into train-time-oracle-only vs deployment-time-predictor. Two go/no-go gates: (1) does (b) beat (a) on the held-out split by the H2 ≥10pp margin; (2) does deployment-time (c) beat train-time-oracle (c) at all. If gate 1 fails, the bet collapses to a sensor/benchmark paper (Economist's reframe). If gate 1 passes but gate 2 fails, drop the differentiable-soft-body half and ship the dense-tactile held-out result, still publishable, much cheaper. Only if both gates pass is the full bet worth committing compute to.

**Frontier question that would change everything.** Is the deformable-manipulation advantage of dense tactile + differentiable physics a *steady-state force-bounding* property (which the cheap reactive incumbent can approximate) or a *transient contact-transition prediction* property (which only a predictive physics model can supply), and does that transient advantage survive an unseen-sensor swap? If transient + sensor-robust, A3 is a strong standalone bet; if steady-state, A3 is a benchmark contribution riding on a wave that has already passed it.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics professor. Grounded against `Capability/Manipulation.md` lines 297-389 (A3). I am hunting soft claims and over-reach, not rubber-stamping.*

---

##### 1. Confidence scores (5 key findings, 1-10 reliability)

**Finding 1 — Force-regulation beats pose-selection on deformables (H1) is settled; demote from the bet. → 9/10**
The card itself flags H1 as a "settled-background check" (line 357) and labels it "consensus" (line 313). Three independent methods with concrete deltas (DexSkin 20%→60% integrity line 308, Force-Regulated Manipulation 38%→68% line 308, Stress-Guided RL 36.5% stress cut line 322). The synthesis's recommendation to demote it from the bet is exactly right and is what the card already does in prose. The only reason this is not a 10 is that "settled" rests on three 2025-26 papers, not a decade of replication; it is consensus-by-recency, not consensus-by-saturation. But for the purpose of "should not be sold as the novel bet," the claim is airtight.

**Finding 2 — Dense tactile already beats vision-only on fragile control in-distribution; held-out is the open question. → 8/10**
Well-supported by two real-robot rows: 3D-ViTac +25-45pp (line 340) and FARM 95-100% (line 341), both explicitly model-free with no held-out split. The Skeptic's HumanoidVTA challenge is real and is corroborated by the card's own risk callout (line 385: "dense barely beats sparse"). Docking two points: (a) 3D-ViTac and FARM measure *fragile/deformable* control but neither is a *held-out-object* result, so "already beats in-distribution" is solid but the framing quietly imports the unsolved half; (b) HumanoidVTA is a *discrimination* benchmark (line 325), so calling its result a "null on control" is a category slip — it never ran control, so it cannot null control. The finding's reliability is high but the Skeptic's specific evidence is weaker than stated.

**Finding 3 — The exact conjunction (dense tactile + differentiable soft-body + held-out control) is unoccupied. → 6/10**
This is the load-bearing novelty claim and it is only partially verifiable from the card. The card's 30+ row table does support the *internal* decomposition: every model-based row (DexDeform, Diff-MPM Control, EMPM, PhysWorld-Deformable, ACID, Particle-Grid) is flagged "control loop still to be closed" or "no held-out / no tactile," and every tactile-control row (3D-ViTac, FARM, Cross-Modal VT) is flagged "model-free, no differentiable soft-body." So the *conjunction* genuinely has no row. But the briefing's freshness argument leans on three papers (TacForeSight 2606.11184, OmniVTA 2603.19201, Dream-Tac 2606.08737) that **do not appear anywhere in the card**, and I cannot confirm from this document that they are (a) rigid-only, (b) latent-not-differentiable-physics, or (c) same-sensor. An absence-of-evidence novelty claim is only as strong as the sweep behind it, and the sweep's most time-sensitive inputs are unaudited here. The Historian's "empty because the route loses" caveat is the correct adversarial pressure and is under-weighted.

**Finding 4 — Differentiable soft-body's deployment-time value over a train-time oracle is unproven; the genuine bet and least reliable claim. → 5/10**
The synthesis correctly identifies this as the crux, and the card supports the diagnosis: Stress-Guided RL "uses MPM only as a train-time stress oracle" (line 309, 322), and DexDeform (2023, line 344) plus every twin row have an open control loop. So the *gap* is real. But the *finding* as stated ("deployment-time beats train-time-oracle is the bet") is the least-evidenced claim in the entire card — by construction, since no paper has run the comparison. The H3 optimists (EMPM, PhysWorld-Deformable) cited as challengers are *accuracy* results (Chamfer 0.0082, 47× speed), not *control-value-over-oracle* results, so they do not actually challenge the claim; they are mis-filed as counter-evidence when they are upstream substrate. This is the honest core of A3 and it is honestly labeled as the weak point — but a finding whose support is "the card's own caveats" and whose challengers are off-target is, definitionally, low-confidence. The synthesis is right to call it the bet and right to call it least reliable.

**Finding 5 — The substrate (sensor-transfer, standardized held-out integrity benchmark), not the policy, is where durable value sits. → 6/10**
This is the most *interesting* claim and the one I most want to push on. It is well-motivated: SoGraB (line 304, 374) is genuinely an unowned gripper-agnostic metric, sensor heterogeneity across VTouch++/HumanoidVTA is real, and the 4-order data gap vs OXE is a fair structural observation. But this finding is doing double duty — it is simultaneously a *research-strategy* claim (build the benchmark) and an implicit *demotion* of the headline bet (the policy result won't be durable). That is a strong, partly normative claim supported by only two perspectives (Practitioner + Economist), "uncontradicted" largely because the other three perspectives weren't asked the sensor-transfer question. "Uncontradicted" here means "untested," not "robust." The pivot to sensor-transfer as the *real* held-out axis is a genuine insight but it is one synthesizer's bet dressed as a convergent finding.

**Mean of the five: 6.8. Lowest: 5 (Finding 4).**

---

##### 2. Weakest link

**Finding 4 (deployment-time differentiable model beats a train-time oracle) is the weakest claim — and it is weak by construction, because it is the unrun experiment.** That is acceptable for a *bet* but the briefing slightly launders it: it cites EMPM and PhysWorld-Deformable as "challengers" (H3 optimists) when those papers report twin *fidelity*, not deployment-time *control gain over an oracle*. They cannot challenge a claim they never tested.

What would verify it: a single controlled ablation holding the policy class, object set, sensor, and reward fixed, and varying **only** how the soft-body physics enters the loop — (i) train-time privileged oracle vs (ii) deployment-time differentiable predictor — on an explicit train/held-out deformable split, with integrity scored by SoGraB. That is precisely the experiment the synthesis proposes (gate 2), which is the right instinct. Until that ablation exists in *some* paper or pilot, Finding 4 is a hypothesis, not a finding, and the briefing should not let H3-optimist accuracy numbers stand in as support either for or against it.

Secondary weak link: Finding 3's freshness sweep depends on three papers absent from the card. The cheap fix is to confirm TacForeSight / OmniVTA / Dream-Tac are rigid-contact + latent-not-differentiable before the "months not years" window claim is allowed to drive urgency.

---

##### 3. Bias check — which perspective over-dominated

**The Economist/Practitioner axis over-dominated the synthesis.** The two most consequential moves in the briefing — "the durable value is the substrate, not the policy" (Finding 5) and "DE-RISK, do not COMMIT, the falsifier most likely fires" (the actionable insight) — are both portfolio-risk framings, not scientific-mechanism framings. The "hidden cross-perspective connection" then routes the *entire defensible contribution* through the Practitioner's sensor-transfer axis, which quietly redefines A3 away from its own stated thesis (a control result on held-out deformables) toward a benchmark/transfer paper. That is a defensible recommendation, but it is the Economist steering, and it should be named as such rather than presented as five perspectives converging.

The Academic perspective is well-represented on the positive evidence (3D-ViTac, FARM) but the Historian is under-weighted: the single most dangerous possibility — that the conjunction is empty *because the differentiable-model-at-deploy route has lost for three years* (DexDeform 2023 → still loop-to-be-closed 2026) — gets one sentence and is then overridden by the optimists. A more balanced synthesis would let the Historian's "empty-because-it-loses" hypothesis carry equal weight to the "empty-because-it's-whitespace" hypothesis, since the card cannot distinguish them.

---

##### 4. Missing perspective (6th angle that would change conclusions)

**A hardware/sensor-reliability engineer (the "does the dense tactile sensor survive the task" perspective).** Every positive result in A3 — 3D-ViTac taxels, DexSkin capacitive skin, HumanoidVTA's 2,124 sensors, GelSight in FARM — assumes a high-density tactile surface that stays calibrated through repeated *fragile-and-deformable* contact: wet tofu, crushed berries, adhesive dough. Dense tactile skins drift, foul, and delaminate exactly in the regime A3 targets. This perspective would change two conclusions: (1) it reframes Finding 5's "sensor heterogeneity" from a *transfer* problem into a *durability* problem, which is far harder and possibly fatal to any deployment claim; and (2) it sharpens the go/no-go — if the dense-tactile signal degrades over an episode, then the H2 "dense beats vision-only" margin is an *opening-trial* artifact, and the differentiable model (Finding 4) might earn its keep precisely as the thing that *compensates for a drifting sensor*, which is a stronger and more novel framing than any in the briefing. None of the five perspectives owns sensor physics, and for a tactile-heavy bet that is a real hole.

(A close runner-up sixth angle: a control-theorist asking whether "differentiable soft-body model at deploy" is even *stable* in closed loop on a non-smooth contact manifold — the briefing treats the model's value as an empirical accuracy question and never asks whether gradient-through-contact gives usable, stable feedback at control rates.)

---

##### 5. Overall grade + top fixes

**Grade: B+.**

This is a strong, honest briefing. Its best move is correct and rare: it un-bundles a card that had quietly packed a settled premise (H1) and an already-won result (dense-tactile in-distribution) into "the bet," and it isolates the one genuinely risky claim (deployment-time differentiable model). The two-gate de-risk plan is exactly the right experimental design and the frontier question (steady-state force-bounding vs transient contact-transition prediction) is genuinely sharp and would, if answered, settle the direction. That is A-grade synthesis thinking.

It is held to B+ by four over-reaches:

1. **Top fix — stop citing twin-fidelity numbers as evidence for/against the deployment-time bet.** EMPM and PhysWorld-Deformable Chamfer/speed results are mis-filed as H3 "challengers." They are upstream substrate; they neither support nor refute Finding 4. Re-label them and the weakest claim becomes honestly unsupported (which is fine for a bet) instead of falsely contested.

2. **Audit the freshness sweep before letting "months not years" drive urgency.** The three 2026 tactile-world-model papers are absent from the card; the urgency claim and the entire "window is closing" frame rest on three unaudited inputs. Confirm rigid + latent + same-sensor, or soften the timeline.

3. **Name the Economist's hand.** The "substrate not policy" pivot and the "DE-RISK not COMMIT" verdict are portfolio-risk recommendations, not five-perspective convergence. Present them as the Economist's well-argued bet, and give the Historian's "empty-because-it-loses" hypothesis equal billing with the whitespace hypothesis.

4. **Add the sensor-durability perspective.** A tactile-heavy fragile-contact bet that never asks whether the dense skin survives wet, crushed, sticky contact is missing its highest-probability failure mode — and that mode, if taken seriously, actually *strengthens* the differentiable-model case rather than weakening it.

Fix 1 and 2 are cheap and would move this to A-. The synthesis's core judgment — "real but narrow whitespace, commit only if the buried falsifier fires first" — is sound and I would sign off on the de-risk plan as written.

---

### B (Cluster review)

Topic: the COHERENCE + central shared-bottleneck thesis of Cluster B — *"Contact-Rich Assembly & Precision: sub-millimeter contact where vision is blind to the contact state, the policy is open-loop, and the in-distribution benchmark is already saturated, so the bet moves onto the prediction delta and out-of-distribution transfer."* Two directions: B1 (predictive-tactile contact imagination — forecast force and act *before* contact) and B2 (a 5-state physical contact-mode latent that switches *dynamics* + a reversibility gate). Reviewing whether the grouping carves the problem at the right joint and whether the shared bet is sound, NOT re-reviewing each direction in depth.

The shared-bottleneck claim: B1's predicted contact-state trajectory is what B2 conditions its discrete-mode dynamics on; both face the same "vision is blind, policy is open-loop, in-distribution SR is saturated" wall, and the escape for both is *the prediction delta on OOD/unseen transfer*, not the absolute SR. Anchors: SPARR ([[2602.23253]]) real residual, DreamTacVLA ([[2512.23864]]) tactile imagination.

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily).**
Core position: The cluster's "saturation → move to the delta" framing is correct at the bench but the binding constraint it underweights is the *tactile-data and tactile-hardware tax* — DreamTacVLA (95.0% Peg, +22.3% Dream ablation) and VT-WM (35% real SR gain) are each single-platform with bespoke sensors (Digit 360, GelSight-class), and a tactile world model that must fit OmniVTA's 60 Hz reflexive loop (B1 H2) is a latency gamble on real hardware. The most deployable-looking result in the whole cluster is the *sensor-free* line, not the imagination line.
Strongest supporting evidence: HapticVLA (2603.15257, alphaxiv, NOT in card) hits 86.7% on fragile pick-place with *zero inference-time tactile sensor* by distilling a tactile token, beating even its own tactile-equipped teacher (81.7%); FBI ([[2508.14441]]) deploys Vision-Only at 34.4 ms vs 17.4 ms visuotactile. SPARR ([[2602.23253]]) gets 95–100% AutoMate with *no human supervision* and a real residual — the practitioner's actual default.
The one thing only this perspective says: build B2 first, not B1. A 5-state mode latent supervised from REASSEMBLE phase annotations ([[2502.05086]]) is cheap (force-torque, no GelSight), runs at controller rate, and the reversibility gate maps to a real deploy pain (wedged pegs jam the line). B1's tactile world model is a research luxury until the 60 Hz horizon-vs-frequency gate (H2) is shown to close on real hardware.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
Core position: The cluster honestly concedes its own central mechanism is no longer contrarian — "predict-then-act is now *co-discovered consensus*" with VT-WM ([[2602.06001]]), VTAM ([[2603.23481]]), and the 2019 root Deep Tactile MPC ([[1903.04128]]) all building action-conditioned tactile predictors. What the literature actually supports is the *reframed* bet: not "prediction helps" but "the gain is concentrated at contact-onset and survives sensor removal" (B1 H1+H3), and "a 5-state physical mode beats a binary bit on unseen transfer + yields reversibility" (B2 H1+H3).
Strongest supporting evidence: ContactWorld ([[2606.13877]]) shows the tactile gain *grows with horizon* (16.0%→20.5% at 48-step offset) — direct evidence the gain is non-uniform, which is exactly B1 H1's phase-stratification premise, yet ContactWorld ranks representations and never runs the matched predict-vs-react *delta*. On B2, Predicate State Estimation ([[2203.02468]]) already shows a non-binary multi-state classifier generalizing to unseen tasks (0.80 acc vs 0.38 direct) — so "multi-state beats binary on generalization" is partly settled; the live novelty narrows to *dynamics-switching* + *reversibility*, which the card correctly flags as add-not-replace.
The one thing only this perspective says: the falsifiers are real and dangerous. B1 H1 ("flat margin across phases → WM buys nothing") and B2 H1 ("binary bit ties 5-state on unseen NIST → physical-mode latent redundant") are both plausible *null* outcomes the consensus literature has not excluded — the contribution is the *measurement*, so a clean null is still publishable but kills the "bet."

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks the bet is wrong or already solved).**
Core position: The cluster's coherence is partly an illusion of shared *vocabulary* (contact, force, prediction) over two bets at very different maturity — B1's mechanism is decade-old (Deep Tactile MPC, 2019) and its remaining novelty is a single ablation table, while B2's surviving novelty is two narrow sub-claims (dynamics-switch + reversibility) sitting next to several papers that already do pieces. The "shared bottleneck" is generic ("vision blind, open-loop, saturated") and would equally describe Clusters A and D — it does not uniquely carve B.
Strongest supporting evidence: B1's own table concedes every predictor row (DreamTacVLA, VT-WM, VTAM, Deep Tactile MPC, ViTacFormer, AdapTac, MLA, FBI) does part of the conjunction; the card's defense is that *none run the exact matched-delta + onset-stratification + sensor-removal conjunction* — a contribution that is one good ablation harness ([[2505.18472]] ManiFeel), not a new mechanism. For B2, Support-Safe Variational Hybrid Filtering (2605.16398, alphaxiv, NOT in card) already does *contact-mode + stick-slip recovery* as a hybrid filter over {free, impact, stick-slip}; MATCH ([[2604.19677]]) avoids breaks via force control without any mode model.
The one thing only this perspective says: the cluster risks being two "measurement papers" dressed as bets. If H1 nulls in either direction, you have a negative result, not a method — pre-register the falsifier and decide *now* whether a clean null is an acceptable outcome.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
Core position: The narrative is shaped by benchmark saturation economics — AutoMate/LIBERO-class in-distribution SR is maxed (SPARR 95–100%), so the field's reward gradient has *already* shifted to "OOD delta + bound the force + new benchmark," which is precisely where the cluster points. That alignment is a feature (publishable, low-saturation headroom) and a risk (everyone is crowding the same delta-on-unseen niche right now, mid-2026).
Strongest supporting evidence: the cluster cites a wave of 2026-Q2 papers (ContactWorld 2606.13877, Dream-Tac 2606.08737, TacForeSight 2606.11184, OmniVTA 2603.19201) — a burst pattern that signals a hot, fundable, and *fast-saturating* sub-area. New-benchmark incentive is explicit: CONTACT Disassembly ([[2603.08560]]) and ManiFeel ([[2505.18472]]) are benchmark plays that the cluster smartly co-opts as substrate (own the harness → own the metric).
The one thing only this perspective says: the durable asset is the *harness*, not the method. The matched predict-vs-react split on ManiFeel (B1) and the graded extraction/reversibility eval on CONTACT (B2) are benchmark contributions that survive even if the method nulls — that is where the citation annuity is. Commit to publishing the harness regardless of the method's outcome.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
Core position: This is the tactile re-run of the pixel-vs-latent world-model cycle and the model-based-RL lineage. Deep Tactile MPC (2019, [[1903.04128]]) is the GelSight-image-forecasting + CEM ancestor — the same arc as Dreamer/PlaNet pushed model-based RL from pixel prediction to latent prediction; B1 H4's "predict a sensor-agnostic *latent* (TaF-VLA/Sparsh-X) not raw tactile" is the exact pixel→latent move that mattered historically.
Strongest supporting evidence: the consensus burst (VT-WM/VTAM/Dream-Tac all 2026) mirrors how Dreamer spawned a year of near-simultaneous latent-WM papers; the open question that decided *that* wave was never "does prediction help" but "does the predicted thing have to be the observation or a learned latent," which maps to B1 H4. On B2, the discrete-mode-vs-continuous-smoothing debate is the hybrid-systems/contact-mechanics revival (DPGMM modes in Hybrid-GP [[1909.04915]] is 2019-era machinery) — historically discrete-mode dynamics models worked but *mode-conditional policies* rarely beat a well-tuned continuous residual once data scaled.
The one thing only this perspective says: in every prior wave, the discrete-structure bet (modes, options, skills) lost to scale unless the discrete label was *cheaply supervisable and causally load-bearing*. B2 lives or dies on H5 (mode-classification accuracy from REASSEMBLE phases + sim) — if the label is expensive or noisy, history says the continuous residual (SPARR/ResiP) wins.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

**(1) Where perspectives clash.**
- *Build order:* PRACTITIONER says build B2 first (cheap force-torque, controller-rate, real deploy pain) and treats B1's tactile WM as a latency luxury; HISTORIAN+SKEPTIC say B2's discrete-mode bet is the historically *fragile* one (loses to continuous residual unless H5 holds), implying B1's measurement is the safer contribution. Direct clash on which direction de-risks the cluster.
- *Is the whitespace real:* the card frames B1 as a clean unfilled-slot (matched delta + onset + sensor-removal conjunction); SKEPTIC counters it is one ablation table over a decade-old mechanism, and ECONOMIST notes the niche is crowding fast (Q2-2026 burst). ACADEMIC sits between: the *reframed* bet (onset-concentration + sensor-removal) is genuinely unmeasured, but partly pre-empted in pieces (HapticVLA sensor-removal, ContactWorld horizon-growth).
- *Sensor-free deployment (B1 H3):* PRACTITIONER calls sensor-free the most deployable line and points to HapticVLA (86.7%, no test-time sensor); SKEPTIC uses the *same* HapticVLA + FBI to argue H3 is already occupied. Same evidence, opposite verdict on novelty.

**(2) Strongest vs weakest.**
Strongest: ACADEMIC — it is the most evidence-bound, correctly separates the *settled* claim (prediction helps; multi-state generalizes) from the *live* claim (onset-concentration + sensor-removal; dynamics-switch + reversibility), and names the exact falsifiers that decide the bet. Weakest: HISTORIAN — illuminating analogies (Dreamer, hybrid-systems revival) but its prediction ("discrete structure loses to scale") is a prior, not evidence about *these* sub-millimeter, low-data contact tasks where scale is precisely what is missing, so the analogy may not transfer.

**(3) The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction.**
On a *matched-capacity* harness (ManiFeel for B1, held-out NIST for B2): is the prediction/mode gain (a) concentrated at contact-onset / on unseen tasks, AND (b) does it survive against a strong continuous reactive-or-residual baseline (RDP/ResiP/SPARR)? If yes to both, COMMIT; if the margin is flat or the continuous baseline ties, the cluster collapses to a benchmark contribution. This single matched ablation resolves the build-order, whitespace, and historical-fragility clashes at once.

**(4) What every perspective agrees on (likely true).**
In-distribution SR is genuinely saturated (SPARR 95–100% AutoMate, DreamTacVLA 95.0% Peg), so the *only* defensible headline is the OOD/unseen-transfer delta with a force bound — never an absolute in-distribution number. All five accept the saturation premise and the reframing onto the delta; the disagreement is only about *which* delta and whether it survives a matched baseline.

**(5) The blind spot (addressed by no perspective).**
None addressed *closed-loop error compounding / horizon credit-assignment of the prediction*: every B1 metric is task-success or single-step Fréchet, but a tactile world model that is locally accurate can still drift over a multi-step insertion (the imagined-tactile rollout error compounds), and no perspective or hypothesis isolates *where in the trajectory the imagined tactile diverges from measured* under closed-loop action feedback. The risks box mentions micro-slip and latency but not rollout-error accumulation — the failure mode most likely to make H3 (imagined-tactile substitution) collapse silently in long-horizon assembly.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**(1) CEO summary.** Cluster B is a *coherent* theme carved at a real joint — contact-rich precision where vision is blind, policies are open-loop, and benchmarks are saturated — and its strategic move (abandon the saturated absolute, bet on the OOD/unseen-transfer delta + a force bound) is correctly aligned with where the field's reward gradient now points. But the two directions sit at different maturity: B1's mechanism (action-conditioned tactile prediction) is decade-old and now consensus, so its surviving contribution is a *measurement* — a matched reactive-vs-predictive delta, stratified at contact-onset, with imagined tactile substituting the absent sensor — that no single paper has run as a conjunction, even though every piece exists in isolation. B2's surviving novelty is narrower and more fragile: not "discrete modes help" (partly settled) but specifically *dynamics-switching* on a 5-state physical latent plus a *mode-derived reversibility gate*, riding on whether the mode label is cheaply supervisable (H5). The honest read: this is two strong *measurement/benchmark* bets wearing the costume of mechanism bets — high-value and publishable, but each carries a credible null outcome that must be pre-registered.

**(2) Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *In-distribution SR is saturated; only the OOD/unseen delta + force bound is defensible.* (All five support; none challenge.) Most reliable — it is the cluster's load-bearing premise and is unanimous.
2. *Predict-then-act and multi-state-beats-binary are now co-discovered consensus, not contrarian.* (ACADEMIC, SKEPTIC, HISTORIAN support; PRACTITIONER neutral.) Strongly grounded: VT-WM/VTAM/Dream-Tac (B1) and Predicate State Estimation 0.80-vs-0.38 (B2) prove the base claims are settled, narrowing both bets to their reframed sub-claims.
3. *The surviving novelty is a measurement/conjunction, not a new mechanism.* (SKEPTIC, ACADEMIC, ECONOMIST support; PRACTITIONER partly challenges by valuing B2's deploy relevance.) Reliable for B1; for B2 the dynamics-switch is a genuine method delta, so this is ~80% true.
4. *The durable asset is the harness, not the method.* (ECONOMIST, PRACTITIONER support; ACADEMIC neutral; SKEPTIC agrees by implication.) Medium-high: ManiFeel-based matched split (B1) and CONTACT graded-extraction eval (B2) survive even a method null.
5. *The discrete-mode bet (B2) is historically the fragile one — it loses to a continuous residual unless H5 (cheap mode supervision) holds.* (HISTORIAN asserts; PRACTITIONER challenges, valuing B2's force-torque cheapness; ACADEMIC neutral.) Lowest reliability — a prior from prior waves, not evidence about these low-data sub-mm tasks.

**(3) Hidden cross-perspective connection.** PRACTITIONER's "build the cheap, sensor-free, controller-rate thing" and HISTORIAN's "discrete structure only wins when the label is cheap and causal" and ECONOMIST's "the harness is the annuity" all converge on the *same* hidden variable: **supervision cost of the predicted/latent quantity**. B1 H4 (predict a sensor-agnostic *latent* not raw tactile) and B2 H5 (distill the mode label from REASSEMBLE phases + sim) are the *same de-risking move* — make the thing you predict cheap and platform-portable — even though the card files them as unrelated hypotheses in different directions. That is the cluster's real unifying bet, and it is currently buried as H4/H5 rather than stated as the thesis.

**(4) Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK (do not yet COMMIT, do not DROP).** The bet survives a literature check but is one matched ablation away from either commit or collapse. Concrete next experiment, run *before* committing: on ManiFeel's modality/architecture-swap pipeline, run the single matched-capacity contrast that resolves the biggest contradiction — (i) predictive (Dream-on) vs a *strong* reactive baseline (RDP 2503.02881 / ResiP 2407.16677) at equal parameters, (ii) stratify the margin by phase (approach / onset / settled), (iii) re-run deployment with imagined tactile replacing the sensor, logging *where in the rollout* imagined diverges from measured (closes the blind spot). Decision rule, pre-registered: if the margin is concentrated at onset AND survives sensor-removal AND beats the reactive baseline → COMMIT to B1 as the lead, fold B2 in as the dynamics-switching extension; if the margin is flat or the reactive baseline ties → pivot to publishing the ManiFeel matched-split harness as the contribution and DROP the mechanism claim. Build B2's cheap force-torque mode classifier (H5) in parallel as the low-cost hedge, since it needs no GelSight and de-risks independently.

**(5) Frontier question that would change everything.** Is a single *unified* contact latent — a learned, sensor-agnostic, action-conditioned representation that is simultaneously (a) forecastable (B1's imagined tactile), (b) discretely mode-structured (B2's 5-state physical mode), and (c) reversibility-decodable — learnable from cheap force-torque + sim, such that one prediction target serves both directions? If yes, B1 and B2 are not two bets but one, and the cluster's thesis should be rewritten around that shared latent rather than around "contact-rich precision."

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics faculty. I read Manipulation.md lines 390-567 (Cluster B: B1 Predictive-Tactile Contact Imagination, B2 Contact-Mode-Conditional Precision & Reversibility) and graded the briefing against what the card actually contains, not against its self-summary.

**Headline judgment.** The briefing is honest where it matters: it concedes the mechanisms are settled and reframes the contribution as a measurement/conjunction. That self-awareness is the briefing's strongest feature and the main reason it earns a respectable grade. But it overstates the cleanliness of the "no one has run this conjunction" whitespace, it imports an attribution error from B2 into a B1-framed finding, and its "de-risk, one ablation from commit/collapse" actionable is more decisive than the evidence licenses. The single experiment proposed will not, by itself, resolve B1 because the card already lists papers that occupy most of the proposed slot.

---

**1. CONFIDENCE SCORES (1-10 reliability, with justification)**

1. **In-distribution SR saturated; only OOD-delta + force-bound defensible — 9/10.** Load-bearing and well-evidenced inside the card: DreamTacVLA 95.0% peg / 85.7% USB (line 401, 419), SPARR 95-100% AutoMate (line 488, 506), both flagged as the saturated reference and both bets explicitly moved onto the unseen/perturbation delta. This is the premise the whole cluster rests on and it is correct. Docked one point only because "saturated" is asserted from a handful of headline numbers on narrow peg/USB/gear suites, not from a saturation curve across task difficulty — REASSEMBLE (line 514) and CONTACT R5 15.0% (line 515) show plenty of *un*-saturated contact tasks, so the claim is "saturated on the easy canonical benchmarks," which is the defensible and intended reading but slightly stronger as stated.

2. **Predict-then-act / multi-state-beats-binary are co-discovered consensus, not contrarian — 8/10.** Strongly grounded for B1: VT-WM, VTAM, Deep Tactile MPC (2019), Dream-Tac all build action-conditioned tactile WMs (lines 405, 421-426), and the card itself says "predict-then-act is now co-discovered consensus." For B2 the "multi-state beats binary" base is supported by Predicate State Estimation 0.80-vs-0.38 (line 512). Docked because the finding silos this cleanly into "settled, so the bet narrows" without crediting that several cited papers narrow the *reframed* sub-claim too (FBI 2508.14441 already does sensor-free inferred tactile; Direction Matters 2602.14174 already does anisotropic mode-aware control) — the consensus has crept further into the "surviving" slot than the finding admits.

3. **Surviving novelty is a measurement/conjunction, not a new mechanism — 6/10.** Reliable for B1, weaker than stated overall. The briefing's own hedge ("~80% true; for B2 the dynamics-switch is a genuine method delta") is honest, but it cuts against the finding's headline: B2 is *not* primarily a measurement bet — its H1/H2/H3/H4 are method deltas (5-state dynamics-switching latent, mode-gated physics losses, per-mode residual, reversibility gate). So finding 3 is really two different findings stapled together (B1 = measurement, B2 = method) and reported as one. Also: the B1 measurement is less unoccupied than claimed. The card lists FBI (sensor-free imagined tactile, line 447), Deep Predictive Vision-Tactile (predictive-tactile survives occlusion, line 444), AdapTac (future-force-prediction as regularizer, line 445), and ManiFeel (matched modality/architecture swap, line 440). The "matched reactive-vs-predictive, phase-stratified at onset, with imagined-tactile substitution" conjunction is genuinely un-run as a *single* protocol, but each of its three legs is individually instantiated, so the novelty is "assemble three existing legs into one matched table," which is a real but modest contribution, not a clean greenfield.

4. **The durable asset is the harness, not the method — 7/10.** Medium-high and probably the most strategically valuable finding. ManiFeel's matched split (line 440) and CONTACT's graded extraction eval (line 515) do survive a method null, and the ECONOMIST framing (harness = annuity) is sound. Docked because the card flags ManiFeel "contains no native predict-vs-react split" (line 440) — so the "harness survives" claim requires *building* the predict-vs-react split into ManiFeel first, which is itself contribution-work, not a pre-existing annuity you can fall back on for free. The asset is real but not yet built; the finding treats it as already in hand.

5. **The discrete-mode bet (B2) is historically the fragile one — loses to a continuous residual unless H5 holds — 4/10.** Lowest reliability, correctly self-rated as such. This is a HISTORIAN prior ("discrete structure only wins when the label is cheap and causal") transplanted onto sub-mm low-data assembly where it may not apply. The card actually supplies *counter*-evidence the finding underweights: Hybrid-GP (1909.04915, line 526) shows discrete dynamics-modes + reset maps beat one continuous model on slip velocity-jumps from only 15 trials — i.e. discrete structure already wins at low data on exactly this physics. So the "fragile, loses to continuous residual" prior is contradicted by a cited paper. The finding's own caveat ("a prior from prior waves, not evidence about these tasks") is the correct read; I rate it 4 because it is a weakly-supported claim presented as a ranked finding.

Mean = 6.8. Min = 4.

---

**2. WEAKEST LINK**

Finding 5 (the discrete-mode fragility prior). It is the only finding asserted by a single perspective (HISTORIAN), challenged by another (PRACTITIONER), and *contradicted by a paper already in the card* (Hybrid-GP, 15-trial low-data discrete-mode win, line 526). To verify or kill it, you need exactly one number the briefing does not have: **the head-to-head SR (and wedge-failure rate) of a 5-state dynamics-switching latent vs a matched-capacity continuous residual on unseen NIST at the briefing's actual data budget.** That is B2's H1/H4 experiment. Until it is run, finding 5 is a vibe, not a finding — and notably, the briefing's proposed "concrete next experiment" runs B1's ManiFeel ablation, not B2's H1, so the actionable does *not* address the weakest link. That is a planning gap: the briefing de-risks the strong direction and leaves the fragile one asserted.

---

**3. BIAS CHECK — which perspective over-dominated**

**SKEPTIC-dominant, with ECONOMIST as co-pilot.** Every finding is framed as "the bold claim is already settled, so the surviving contribution shrinks to X" — that is the SKEPTIC's signature move, and it is applied uniformly to both directions even though B2 has a live method delta that deserves a more generative reading. The HISTORIAN supplies finding 5 but is overruled in spirit (its prior is contradicted by the card's own Hybrid-GP row, which no one reconciled). The PRACTITIONER is the consistently-pushed-back-on voice ("PRACTITIONER neutral," "PRACTITIONER partly challenges," "PRACTITIONER challenges") — its deploy-relevance and cheap-force-torque arguments are noted but never allowed to move a conclusion. The ACADEMIC is mostly "neutral." Net effect: the synthesis is tuned to *shrink* claims rather than to size the *opportunity*, which is appropriate for a peer review but makes the briefing read as more bearish than the evidence requires. A balanced synthesis would let the PRACTITIONER's "B2's force-torque mode classifier is cheap and the discrete win is real at low data" land as a genuine counterweight to finding 5, not a footnote.

---

**4. MISSING PERSPECTIVE (a 6th angle that would change conclusions)**

**The EXPERIMENTALIST / metrologist** — someone who asks "can the proposed measurement actually be measured cleanly, and will the result be interpretable?" The whole briefing pivots on a single matched ablation (predictive vs reactive at equal parameters, phase-stratified at contact-onset, with sensor-removal). But the card never establishes that "contact-onset" is detectable with enough temporal precision to *stratify* by it, nor that "matched capacity" is well-defined when one policy carries a forecasting head and the other does not (a predictor and a reactor are not the same architecture with one switch — equalizing parameters does not equalize inductive bias). An experimentalist would flag that the pre-registered decision rule ("margin concentrated at onset AND survives sensor-removal AND beats reactive → COMMIT; flat or tie → DROP") has no power analysis: how many seeds, what effect size at onset counts as "concentrated," and what confound (the forecasting head doubling as extra capacity / regularization) would masquerade as a prediction win? This perspective would convert the briefing's clean binary commit/collapse into a likelier *ambiguous* outcome ("small onset-skewed margin, partial sensor-recovery, no clear baseline tie"), which is the realistic result and which the current decision rule cannot adjudicate. It changes the conclusion from "one ablation from commit or collapse" to "the proposed ablation is necessary but under-specified and will probably need a second, confound-controlled round before it decides anything."

---

**5. OVERALL GRADE — B+**

A strong, intellectually honest briefing that correctly identifies the joint (contact-rich precision where vision is blind), correctly diagnoses benchmark saturation, and correctly refuses to rubber-stamp its own directions as mechanism novelty. The "hidden connection" (supervision cost of the predicted/latent quantity unifies B1-H4 and B2-H5) and the "frontier question" (one unified contact latent serving both directions) are genuinely the best ideas in the document and should be promoted to thesis-level rather than left in the closing paragraphs. It loses the A-range for four fixable reasons.

**Top things to fix (ranked):**
1. **Split finding 3.** B1 = measurement/conjunction; B2 = real method delta. Reporting them as one finding ("~80% true") launders B2's method novelty into B1's measurement frame and is the single most misleading move in the briefing.
2. **Reconcile finding 5 against Hybrid-GP (line 526).** The card already contains a low-data (15-trial) discrete-mode-beats-continuous result on the exact physics. Either the fragility prior is wrong for this regime or Hybrid-GP needs to be distinguished — right now finding 5 is asserted over a cited contradiction.
3. **Align the actionable with the weakest link.** The proposed next experiment de-risks B1 (the strong direction); the fragile claim is B2-H1. Run B2's matched 5-state-vs-continuous-residual contrast in parallel with — or before — the B1 ablation, or stop calling finding 5 a finding.
4. **Specify the decision rule's statistics.** "Margin concentrated at onset" and "matched capacity" need an effect-size threshold, a seed count, and a confound control for the forecasting head as extra capacity. Without them the pre-registration is theater and the likely ambiguous result will not trigger either branch.

Minor: the briefing's claim that "every piece exists in isolation" for B1 is closer to "most pieces exist and two are already partially combined (FBI, AdapTac)" — soften "no single paper has run as a conjunction" to "no single paper runs all three legs under one matched protocol," which is the defensible version.
