### B1 (Direction review)

Topic: B1 — Predictive-Tactile Contact Imagination. The bet (verbatim): "The world-model gain over a *matched* reactive policy is concentrated at contact-onset and survives sensor removal." Triple conjunction nobody has isolated: (a) the +22.3% world-model delta is phase-concentrated at contact-onset, not flat; (b) *imagined* tactile, substituted for an absent sensor at deployment, recovers most of sensor-on SR; (c) measured against a *matched-capacity* reactive baseline.

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily).**
- *Core position*: The bet's deployment payoff (H3, sensor-free via imagined tactile) is the only part a practitioner cares about, because tactile hardware is the real blocker: GelSight-class sensors are fragile, drift ([[2606.17055|T-Rex]] explicitly lists "sensor distortion, calibration drift, lack of dense palm sensing" as hardware bottlenecks), and don't survive a duty cycle of sub-mm insertions. But the world-model loop must fit a control budget, and that is where this dies or lives.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: [[2508.14441|FBI]] already deploys "Vision-Only" mode by inferring dense tactile from visual flow (Flow2Tactile) at **34.4 ms** vs 17.4 ms with the real sensor, real-robot **35.0%** / sim **66.5%** — proof imagined tactile *can* substitute the sensor at real-time speed. [[2603.19201|OmniVTA]]'s reflexive controller runs the corrective loop at **60 Hz** and holds **60–63%** SR under perturbation; that 60 Hz is the budget the dream-loop must not break.
- *Only-this-perspective tells the researcher*: A "Think–Dream–Act" loop ([[2512.23864|DreamTacVLA]]) that forecasts a tactile *image* and re-plans cannot run at 60 Hz on real hardware; the horizon you can afford is set by inference latency, not by accuracy. H2 (optimal horizon below reflexive frequency) is not a nice-to-have ablation, it is the feasibility gate — and FBI/OmniVTA already show the only deployable answer is a *short* forecast feeding a fast reactive layer, i.e. the architecture is half-decided before you start.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
- *Core position*: The card is honest that predict-then-act is "co-discovered consensus, not contrarian" — and the literature backs that fully: [[1903.04128|Deep-Tactile-MPC]] forecast GelSight images for CEM planning in 2019, [[2602.06001|VT-WM]] (UW/FAIR) fuses visuo-tactile latents *with actions* and plans-in-imagination (+35% real SR over V-WMs), [[2603.23481|VTAM]] hits 90/85/95%. The genuinely unclaimed slot is narrow and methodological: the *matched reactive-vs-predictive delta, phase-stratified at contact-onset*.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: [[2606.13877|ContactWorld]] is the closest prior art and the card cites it precisely — it isolates *which representation property* drives the gain and shows tactile gain **grows with horizon (16.0%→20.5% at 48-step offset)**, but it "ranks representations, never runs the matched predict-vs-react delta." [[2602.06001|VT-WM]] plans toward *goal tactile states*, never reports the delta. So the whitespace is real but it is a *measurement* contribution, not a *mechanism* contribution.
- *Only-this-perspective tells the researcher*: The bet's mechanism is decade-old (Deep Tactile MPC, 2019) and the card admits it. A measurement-only paper ("we ran the ablation nobody ran") is reviewable but lands as an *analysis paper*, not a method — its acceptance hinges entirely on whether the phase-stratified delta is a *surprising* result. If onset-concentration is what everyone already assumes, the finding is null-shaped even if true.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks the bet is wrong or already solved).**
- *Core position*: Two of the three conjuncts are individually pre-empted, so the "novelty" is the *staple holding them together*, which is the weakest kind of contribution. H3 (imagined tactile substitutes the sensor) is FBI; the predict-then-correct mechanism is now a crowded row ([[2606.08555|FAWAM]] 85% / [[2606.09337|TORL-VLA]] / [[2606.08737|Dream-Tac]] / [[2606.11184|TacForeSight]]); the only un-run experiment is a *phase decomposition* of a number everyone already believes.
- *Evidence the card conveniently under-weights*: [[2606.08555|FAWAM]] (alphaxiv-confirmed) already does **predict-future-wrench → online residual correction**, jointly predicts action+wrench, beats ForceVLA/TA-VLA (85% vs 63.75% avg) AND runs a clean component ablation (Obs 56.25% → +Pre 73.75% → +Res 85.00%) isolating that *prediction* (the "Pre" stage, +17.5%) buys most of the gain over passive observation. That is uncomfortably close to H1's claim "prediction beats consumption," already measured — just not stratified by phase. The card cites TORL-VLA but FAWAM is the sharper, more recent instantiation and its ablation is the one B1 says nobody ran.
- *Only-this-perspective tells the researcher*: If you run H1 and the gain turns out *flat* across phases (the card's own falsifier), you have refuted your own thesis and written a negative-result paper. The base rate that a believed effect is also phase-concentrated *and* survives sensor removal *and* beats a matched reactive baseline at the same capacity is low — each conjunct is a coin-flip and you need all three.

**4. ECONOMIST (money/incentives).**
- *Core position*: The tactile-WM space is in a 2026 land-grab — dataset+method bundles are the unit of publication ([[2603.19201|OmniVTA]] ships OmniViTac 21K trajectories; [[2606.17055|T-Rex]] ships a 100-hour dataset; [[2602.06001|VT-WM]] is FAIR/Meta-backed). A pure-measurement paper with no new dataset and no new SOTA absolute (the card concedes the absolute is "saturated" at 95.0%) competes badly for attention against these resourced bundles.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The card's own "Key targets" lead with *deltas under perturbation* (DreamTacVLA +22.3%, OmniVTA 60–63%) precisely because the in-distribution absolute is saturated — an honest read of the incentive landscape: headline-SR is taken, so the only currency left is the controlled comparison. Benchmark substrate exists ([[2505.18472|ManiFeel]] modular swap, [[2411.12503|ManiSkill-ViTac-2025]] standardized V+T) so the marginal cost of the measurement is low — which is *why* someone with a dataset will run it as a side-ablation first.
- *Only-this-perspective tells the researcher*: Whoever owns a large visuo-tactile dataset (Meta on VT-WM, the OmniViTac team, the T-Rex/NVIDIA-Berkeley team) can run B1's phase-stratified ablation as a one-figure addendum to their next release and claim it. The window is months. The defensible asset is not the measurement — it is a *reusable phase-stratified protocol* (a benchmark contribution on top of ManiFeel), not a single number.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the pixel-vs-latent world-model cycle replaying in the tactile modality. Deep Tactile MPC (2019) forecast raw GelSight *pixels* + CEM; the field then moved to *latent* action-conditioned WMs ([[2602.06001|VT-WM]], [[2606.13877|ContactWorld]]'s JEPA latent) exactly as the Dreamer lineage moved model-based RL from pixel reconstruction to latent imagination. B1 sits at the same inflection the visual WM field already passed.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The lineage is explicit in the card's own rows — [[1903.04128|Deep-Tactile-MPC]] (pixel forecast, 2019) → [[2602.06001|VT-WM]]/[[2603.19201|OmniVTA]] (latent, action-conditioned, 2026) → [[2606.13877|ContactWorld]] (JEPA latent, asks "what matters"). The visual-WM history says the *winning* finding was never "prediction helps" (assumed) but "where the representation should live" — which is ContactWorld's question, not B1's.
- *Only-this-perspective tells the researcher*: In every prior wave (Dreamer, sim2real DR, end-to-end vs modular) the *reactive/fast* layer never disappeared — it absorbed the predictive layer as a short-horizon prior. The historically-supported bet is not "predict beats react" (the swing always settles on *predict-feeds-react*, which is literally OmniVTA's slow-fast and T-Rex's MoT split). B1's framing as a *contest* (matched reactive vs predictive) may be asking the wrong question; the field already answered it as a *composition*.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *Skeptic vs Academic on whitespace*: Skeptic says H1 is essentially pre-empted by [[2606.08555|FAWAM]]'s Obs→Pre→Res ablation (prediction "Pre" = +17.5% over passive observation, already isolated). Academic says no — FAWAM isolates *prediction-vs-observation* but never stratifies by *contact phase* (approach/onset/settled), so the specific onset-concentration claim is unmeasured. The clash is whether "phase-stratified" is a real delta or cosmetic re-slicing of a measured number.
   - *Historian vs the card's framing*: Historian says the matched *reactive-vs-predictive contest* (H1's design) is the wrong frame — the field settled on *predict-feeds-react composition* ([[2603.19201|OmniVTA]] slow-fast, [[2606.17055|T-Rex]] MoT). The card frames it as a head-to-head delta. If the historian is right, a "matched reactive policy" that *ties* the predictive one is not a falsifier of the mechanism but evidence the two should be composed, not raced.
   - *Practitioner vs Academic on contribution type*: Practitioner only values H3 (sensor-free deployment, a *capability*); Academic values H1 (the phase-stratified delta, a *measurement*). These pull the paper in opposite directions — a deployment demo vs an analysis paper.

2. **Strongest vs weakest perspective.**
   - *Strongest*: SKEPTIC. It is the only one grounded in a paper the card under-weights ([[2606.08555|FAWAM]], alphaxiv-confirmed to run the prediction-vs-observation ablation and beat the cited consumed-force baselines), and it correctly identifies the conjunction-fragility (three coin-flips).
   - *Weakest*: PRACTITIONER, not because it is wrong but because its core concern (latency) is already addressed by the card's H2 risk-gate and by FBI's 34.4 ms / OmniVTA's 60 Hz existence proofs — it adds urgency but little new information.

3. **The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction (Skeptic vs Academic):** *On a fixed contact-rich insertion protocol (ManiFeel), when you decompose the prediction-over-consumption gain by manipulation phase, is the margin statistically concentrated at contact-onset, or is it flat / settled-phase-dominated?* If concentrated at onset → Academic wins, B1 has a real, surprising, defensible finding. If flat → the gain is just "more capacity / a better regularizer" (FAWAM/AdaTac story) and Skeptic wins.

4. **What every perspective agrees on (likely true):** Predictive/anticipatory tactile *helps* contact-rich manipulation over purely reactive consumption — every cited method (DreamTacVLA, VT-WM, VTAM, OmniVTA, FAWAM, FBI) and every perspective concedes the direction of the effect. The disagreement is only about *why*, *where*, and whether the specific decomposition is novel or already-implied.

5. **The blind spot no perspective addressed:** *Whether the imagined-tactile forecast is actually trustworthy at contact-onset* — the exact phase where the bet claims the gain lives. At onset the contact state is maximally uncertain (the sensor hasn't touched yet), so the forecast is least grounded precisely where H1 says it matters most and H3 says it must replace the sensor. None of the five perspectives interrogated the *epistemic reliability of the forecast as a function of phase* — the card's own risk ("imagined tactile diverges from measured") and H1's onset-concentration claim may be in direct tension: the model is asked to be most valuable exactly where it is least informed.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

1. **CEO summary.** B1 bets that a tactile world model's advantage over a matched reactive policy is (a) concentrated at contact-onset and (b) survives replacing the sensor with its own forecast. The *direction* of the effect is settled consensus and the *mechanism* is decade-old (Deep Tactile MPC, 2019); the genuine whitespace is narrow and methodological — nobody has run the matched, capacity-controlled, phase-stratified reactive-vs-predictive delta, nor the sensor-removal test, on one protocol. This is a real but fragile bet: it is a *measurement/analysis* contribution riding on a three-way conjunction (onset-concentration AND sensor-substitution AND matched-baseline-beat), each of which can independently come out null, and two adjacent 2026 papers ([[2606.08555|FAWAM]], [[2508.14441|FBI]]) already cover one conjunct each. Commit only if you reframe it as a *reusable phase-stratified protocol + a falsifiable surprising claim*, not as a method chasing a saturated SOTA.

2. **Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
   1. *Predictive tactile beats reactive consumption in direction* — **highest reliability**. Supported by all five perspectives and every cited paper (DreamTacVLA +22.3%, VT-WM +35%, FAWAM 85% vs 63.75%, FBI). Challenged by none. This is also why it is *not* the contribution — it is assumed.
   2. *The phase-stratified contact-onset delta is genuinely un-run* — **high reliability** (Academic, confirmed by alphaxiv: ContactWorld ranks representations and shows horizon-growth but not the matched delta; VT-WM never reports it; FAWAM ablates prediction-vs-observation but not by phase). Challenged by Skeptic, who says FAWAM's Obs→Pre ablation is close enough that reviewers may call it incremental.
   3. *Imagined tactile can substitute the sensor at real-time speed* — **medium-high reliability** (Practitioner; FBI is an existence proof at 34.4 ms, 35% real). Challenged by the blind-spot argument: substitution proven *on average* over a task, not *at contact-onset* where the forecast is least grounded.
   4. *The "matched reactive vs predictive contest" may be the wrong frame* — **medium reliability** (Historian; the field's slow-fast/MoT compositions, OmniVTA + T-Rex, suggest predict-feeds-react, not predict-vs-react). Challenged by Academic, who notes a clean head-to-head delta is still informative even if the deployed system composes them.
   5. *The window to claim this is short and dataset-owners are favored* — **medium reliability** (Economist; the measurement is cheap on existing ManiFeel/ManiSkill-ViTac substrate, so a resourced lab can run it as a side-figure). Challenged by nobody directly, but mitigated if B1 ships a *protocol* asset rather than a single number.

3. **Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Practitioner's H3 (deploy with imagined tactile) and the Academic's H1 (onset-concentrated gain) are not two independent hypotheses — they *collide* at the blind spot. H1 locates the value at contact-onset; H3 demands the forecast replace the sensor; but onset is exactly where the forecast is least grounded (no contact has happened yet to condition on). So the two headline hypotheses are coupled through forecast-reliability-vs-phase, a curve no perspective and no cited paper has plotted. That curve *is* the paper's defensible core: if onset-gain and onset-forecast-reliability co-vary, the bet is coherent; if they anti-correlate, the bet is internally contradictory.

4. **Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK, then commit narrowly.** Do not commit to B1 as framed (a method beating saturated SOTA). Commit to the *measurement* only after a cheap kill-test. **Concrete next experiment**: on [[2505.18472|ManiFeel]]'s modular contact-rich pipeline (peg / search / gear), take one architecture at *matched capacity*, instantiate three variants — reactive-consume, predict-then-act (Dream on), and predict-with-imagined-tactile-only — and produce the single figure nobody has: SR-margin decomposed by phase (approach / onset / settled) *overlaid with* forecast-MSE-vs-phase. Use [[2606.08555|FAWAM]] and [[2606.17055|T-Rex]] as the *matched reactive* baselines (both are strong, recent, reactive — they make the comparison credible). Decision gate: (i) if the margin is onset-concentrated AND forecast-MSE is acceptable at onset → COMMIT, the surprising-finding paper exists; (ii) if margin is onset-concentrated but forecast-MSE explodes at onset → COMMIT to a *different* paper ("prediction helps at onset but for reasons other than an accurate forecast — it is a useful regularizer not an oracle"), which is more surprising and harder to scoop; (iii) if margin is flat → DROP B1, the gain is capacity/regularization (the AdaTac/FAWAM story) and there is no whitespace.

5. **Frontier question that would change everything.** Is the contact-onset tactile forecast *causally* used by the policy, or is it an auxiliary regularizer whose benefit would survive even if the forecast were frozen-noise of matched statistics? If a forecast replaced by phase-matched noise recovers the same +22.3%, then the entire predict-then-act literature — DreamTacVLA, VT-WM, VTAM, FAWAM — is buying a *representation-shaping* effect, not *anticipation*, and B1's first principle ("force is forecastable, so predict it before contact") is the wrong story for the right number.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics professor. The briefing is unusually self-aware — it pre-emptively calls B1 "fragile" and "a measurement contribution riding on a three-way conjunction." That honesty is the strongest thing about it, and it is also what I will hold it to. A briefing that already knows the bet is fragile must not then quietly smuggle the fragility back out through soft citations. It does, in two places.*

---

##### 1. Confidence scores (1–10, reliability)

| # | Finding | Score | One-line justification |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Predictive tactile beats reactive *in direction* | **9/10** | The most defensible claim and, as the briefing concedes, the least interesting. Direction is corroborated by every cited predictor (DreamTacVLA +22.3% over its *own* no-Dream ablation, VT-WM 35% over V-WMs, ContactWorld 36.1 vs 32.1). The −1 is because nearly all evidence is each method beating its *own* weaker ablation, not a head-to-head against a *capacity-matched* reactive policy — exactly the gap B1 claims. So "direction is settled" rests on within-paper deltas that do not control capacity. The conclusion survives; the cited support is softer than stated. |
| 2 | The phase-stratified contact-onset delta is genuinely un-run | **7/10** | Strongest *novelty* claim and largely credible: ContactWorld stratifies by *horizon* not *contact phase*, VT-WM reports no matched delta, the table's "What's missing" column honestly says so for every predictor row. But "nobody has plotted SR-margin-by-phase" is an absence-of-evidence claim, and the briefing's own Skeptic note (FAWAM's Obs→Pre ablation "may read as close enough") is the live threat. Un-run is plausible; un-runnable-into-incremental is the risk that drops this below 8. |
| 3 | Imagined tactile can substitute the sensor at real-time speed | **6/10** | FBI (2508.14441) is genuinely in the table and does deploy sensor-free at 34.4 ms — so the *speed* and *substitution-exists* claims are grounded. But the briefing's own blind-spot note is decisive: substitution is shown on-average, on dexterous in-hand tasks, *not* at contact-onset on rigid sub-mm insertion where the forecast is least grounded. The claim B1 needs (substitution holds *at onset, on insertion*) is unproven; the claim the table supports (substitution exists *somewhere*) is weaker. Scored on the gap between the two. |
| 4 | The 'matched reactive-vs-predictive contest' may be the wrong frame | **6/10** | Genuinely important and under-weighted in the cluster framing. OmniVTA (predicted *+ reflexive*, line 420), TORL-VLA (consumed + future-wrench reference, line 432) and AdapTac (prediction-as-*auxiliary-regularizer*, line 445) all already compose predict-with-react rather than pit them against each other — the document *lists* this evidence but the thesis still frames it as a contest. Academic's rebuttal (a head-to-head delta is informative even if deployment composes) is fair, which is why this is a real tension not a refutation. Medium. |
| 5 | The window is short and dataset-owners are favored | **5/10** | The softest finding and correctly the lowest-ranked. "Measurement is cheap on ManiFeel/ManiSkill-ViTac" is asserted, not costed — running matched-capacity, phase-stratified, sensor-removal *and* cross-sensor variants is several controlled training runs per task family, not a "side-figure." The economic claim is directionally reasonable (resourced labs are favored) but the cheapness premise is hand-waved, and the whole finding is a strategic-risk intuition with no falsifier. |

**Mean confidence: 6.6/10. Lowest: 5/10 (Finding 5).**

---

##### 2. Weakest link

The weakest *load-bearing* claim is not Finding 5 (which is openly soft and low-stakes). It is the **citation backbone of the "actionable insight" and CEO summary**: the briefing names **FAWAM (2606.08555)** and **T-Rex (2606.17055)** as (a) the two adjacent 2026 papers that "already cover one conjunct each," and (b) the recommended "matched reactive baselines" for the kill-test. **Neither paper appears anywhere in the B1 section under review (lines 394–480).** Worse, the headline number the briefing attributes to FAWAM — "85% vs 63.75%" — matches the *TacVLA* row almost exactly (line 433: "83.75% avg disassembly vs π0.5 63.75%"). That is the signature of a number lifted from the wrong row and re-badged.

*Info needed to verify:* confirm whether 2606.08555/2606.17055 exist in `_KnowledgeHub_/`, what their actual headline results are, and whether either is in fact reactive (T-Rex is described as a "MoT" / mixture-of-transformers compositional model in Finding 4, which sounds *predict-feeds-react*, i.e. **not** a clean reactive baseline). If FAWAM's real number is the TacVLA number, the entire "two adjacent papers already cover one conjunct each" risk argument and the recommended baseline choice are built on a mis-citation, and the kill-test would compare predictive policies against a baseline that is itself predictive — silently nulling the very delta B1 wants to measure. This is the single most consequential thing to verify before any commit.

A secondary weak link: H3 and the "frozen-noise" frontier question are the genuinely novel core, yet the briefing supplies **no power analysis** — how many seeds/tasks distinguish "onset-concentrated" from "flat" at the +22.3% effect size? Without it, decision-gate (iii) "margin flat → DROP" is un-actionable, because "flat" is a null result and the protocol never specifies the n to reject it.

---

##### 3. Bias check

**The Skeptic over-dominated, and the Academic is the silent co-author.** The synthesis reads as a Skeptic-driven de-risking memo: every finding is framed as a conjunct that "can independently come out null," the headline recommendation is a "kill-test," and the grade-defining move is to reframe B1 from method to measurement. That is good adversarial hygiene, but it has crowded out the two perspectives that would *defend a commit*: the Practitioner appears only as a single latency datapoint (FBI 34.4 ms) and the Economist's contribution (Finding 5) is the weakest in the set. The Historian's genuinely sharp point (Finding 4: the field already *composes* predict+react) is raised and then parked rather than allowed to reframe the thesis.

Net effect: the briefing is well-calibrated on *downside* and thin on *what a win looks like*. It tells me precisely how B1 dies three ways but never quantifies the size or defensibility of the prize if it lives — which is itself a Skeptic bias (risk is vivid, upside is abstract).

---

##### 4. Missing perspective

**The Controls / contact-mechanics theorist.** All five perspectives argue at the level of policies, benchmarks, and deltas; none asks the physics question that decides whether the whole bet is even well-posed: *is next-step force actually a deterministic function of the action at contact-onset?* The first principle (line 409: "the next-step tactile signal is a deterministic consequence of the action given the contact state") is **false at exactly the phase B1 cares about.** Contact-onset is where the dynamics are most discontinuous — make/break, the friction-cone edge, stick-slip bifurcations, micro-impact — i.e. where the action→force map is *least* smooth and *most* sensitive to un-observed alignment. (B2, the very next cluster at line 481, is built on precisely this "contact physics is locally discontinuous, piecewise" premise — so the document contradicts B1's determinism assumption one section later.)

This perspective changes the conclusion: it predicts the briefing's *frontier question* resolves toward "auxiliary regularizer, not anticipation." If force is chaotic at onset, the forecast cannot be a usable oracle there, so any onset-concentrated gain is more likely representation-shaping than predictive — which would make outcome (ii) of the decision gate the *expected* result, not the edge case, and would mean B1 should be scoped as "prediction-as-regularizer" from the start rather than discovering that empirically. A controls theorist would also note the H3/H1 collision the briefing flags is not a coincidence: forecast reliability and signal determinism are the *same* axis, so the "coupling at the blind spot" is structural, not bad luck.

---

##### 5. Overall grade + top fixes

**Grade: B / B−.**

This is a strong *analytical* briefing — the conjunction decomposition, the H1/H3 blind-spot coupling, and the "frozen-noise-of-matched-statistics" frontier question are PhD-qualifying-exam-grade insights, and the self-aware "commit only if reframed as a protocol" framing is exactly right. It is held to B-range by three fixable defects: a probable mis-citation in its load-bearing risk argument, an un-falsifiable decision gate (no power analysis behind "flat"), and a first-principle that the document itself contradicts one cluster later.

**Top fixes, in priority order:**

1. **Verify FAWAM (2606.08555) and T-Rex (2606.17055) before anything else.** They are absent from B1 and FAWAM's quoted "85% vs 63.75%" collides with TacVLA's row — confirm the IDs, the real numbers, and crucially whether either is *actually reactive*. If T-Rex is compositional/predictive, it is the wrong baseline and the kill-test is rigged. Replace with a genuinely matched reactive baseline (RDP 2503.02881 or ForceVLA 2505.22159 are *in the table* and explicitly reactive/consumed — use those).
2. **Add a power/falsifiability spec to the decision gate.** State the n (seeds × task families) and effect-size threshold that separates "onset-concentrated" from "flat" at +22.3%. Without it, gate (iii) "DROP" cannot be triggered honestly and the whole de-risk is theater.
3. **Replace the determinism first principle with a contact-mechanics-honest one.** Concede that force is *least* forecastable at onset (per B2's own piecewise premise) and reframe the thesis around the regularizer-vs-oracle question the frontier already names — i.e. make decision-outcome (ii) the *hypothesis*, not the fallback. This converts B1's biggest internal contradiction into its sharpest claim.
4. **Cost the "cheap measurement" claim.** Finding 5 asserts side-figure cheapness; spell out the actual run budget (variants × tasks × seeds) so the "dataset-owners are favored" timing risk is quantified rather than vibes.

---

### B2 (Direction review)

Topic restatement: B2 bets that a policy reading a **5-state physical contact-mode latent** ($c_t \in$ {free, making, in-contact, sliding, breaking}) and **switching its dynamics model per mode** (a) transfers to *unseen* NIST assembly tasks beyond a binary-contact policy ([[2604.19677|MATCH]]) at [[2602.23253|SPARR]]'s +74.5% relative SR / 36.5% cycle-time level, and (b) exposes a **mode-derived reversibility gate** (`making` retreat safe, `in-contact` possibly wedged) that cuts wedge-failures a binary or mode-blind policy cannot. The wrong assumption being challenged: a *binary* contact/free bit is enough.

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (deploys assembly policies on real hardware).**
- *Core position*: The reversibility gate (H3) is the only part of this bet that solves a problem I feel daily; the 5-state-dynamics-switch (H1/H2/H4) is mostly an architecture story that the field already approximates with a binary bit + force control. On a real FR3, [[2604.19677|MATCH]]'s 0.0% peg-breaks via force control already buys me the safety the card claims only modes can buy.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: [[2603.08560|CONTACT-Disassembly]] real R1→R5 collapse (95.0%→15.0%) and [[2502.05086|REASSEMBLE]] "Insert is the hardest action … multi-step" — wedge/jam on extraction is a genuine, graded, unsolved failure surface. [[2603.08342|PhaForce]] explicitly names "jamming" as the failure indiscriminate force causes, and [[2210.10765|PAINT]]'s reversibility classifier (80 resets vs 3000+) proves reversibility-gating pays off operationally.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: Mode-classification latency and label noise will kill you at deploy time. A 5-state classifier that mislabels `in-contact` as `making` for even 2% of steps will trigger an unsafe retreat into a wedge — the failure the gate exists to prevent. Build H5 (the classifier) first and measure its *per-mode confusion on the breaking↔in-contact boundary* before any policy work, because that boundary IS the reversibility decision.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the contact-rich literature).**
- *Core position*: The "binary is insufficient" claim is no longer a live wrong assumption — it is becoming the consensus, which both validates and erodes the card's framing. [[2203.02468|Predicate-State-Estimation]] already ships a *multi-state, non-binary* discrete-contact classifier that *generalizes to unseen tasks at 0.80 acc* and conditions a runtime policy (0.72 online SR), and 2605.28812 ("Beyond Binary") makes the anti-binary case its title.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: [[1909.04915|Hybrid-GP-Contact-Model]] is the dynamics-side proof that *discrete modes + reset maps beat one continuous model* (best NLL/RMSE on 7-DOF YuMi from 15 trials, captures slip velocity-jumps) — but as a *forward model*, not a policy. The literature has the multi-state classifier (Predicate), the discrete-dynamics forward model (Hybrid-GP), and the reversibility classifier ([[2210.10765|PAINT]]) as *separate* pieces. The card's genuine novelty is the *composition*: a learned multi-state latent that drives a dynamics-switching *policy* AND derives reversibility.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: The card's falsifier is dangerously generous to itself. [[2203.02468|Predicate-State-Estimation]] is not just a "FSM-trigger" footnote — if a multi-state predicate classifier conditioning a policy already generalizes unseen at 0.80, the burden of proof is that *switching dynamics* (not just gating control) adds measurable transfer SR over Predicate-class conditioning. That ablation (H1's third arm) is the whole paper, not a row.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks the bet is wrong or already solved).**
- *Core position*: H1 (dynamics-switch beats binary on unseen) is at serious risk of being a distinction without a difference, because [[2604.19677|MATCH]] avoids breaks via *force control* and [[2602.23253|SPARR]]'s mode-blind residual already hits 95–100% in-distribution and +74.5% on unseen NIST. The card's own falsifier ("binary ties 5-state on unseen NIST and matches wedge-rate → latent redundant") is plausibly the true outcome.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The card concedes [[2602.23253|SPARR]] is mode-blind yet owns the unseen-NIST ceiling B2 wants to claim; [[2602.23648|FAVLA]] gets implicit mode-awareness from a force-variance head without any discrete latent (80.8% at 7.7 N); [[2602.14174|Direction-Matters]] beats baselines 91% vs 67% with a *continuous* normal-force-direction signal. Every "missing discrete mode" cell in the table is occupied by a *continuous* method that already works. The discreteness is an aesthetic preference, not a demonstrated necessity.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: The card stakes novelty on "5 states" but never argues *why 5*. {free, making, in-contact, sliding, breaking} is asserted, not derived; REASSEMBLE's named phases (free-space/contact/pushing/twisting) are 4 and different. If the real win is the reversibility *property*, you don't need 5 dynamics-switching states — you need a 2-state reversible/irreversible classifier ([[2210.10765|PAINT]] already), which collapses 80% of the architecture.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows incentives and benchmark dynamics).**
- *Core position*: The card is structurally honest about benchmark saturation ([[2407.08028|AutoMate]] at 95–100%) and correctly flees to *unseen* NIST + a *new* property (reversibility) — which is exactly the right move when in-distribution SR is a saturated commodity that NVIDIA-scale labs ([[2602.23253|SPARR]] = Narang/Akinola/Gupta) already own. The incentive gradient points away from SR-chasing and toward a *new measurable axis*.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: [[2602.23253|SPARR]]'s industrial-grade-without-human-supervision framing and [[2407.08028|AutoMate]] saturation show the SR axis is captured by industry compute. A reversibility/wedge-rate metric on [[2603.08560|CONTACT-Disassembly]]'s graded suite (S1–S5/R1–R5) is an *uncontested* leaderboard an academic lab can define and win.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: The defensible publishable unit is the *reversibility metric + CONTACT Disassembly extraction benchmark*, not the 5-state architecture. Whoever defines "mode-derived reversibility rate" as a standard metric owns the citation surface; the dynamics-switch is a commodity that a better-funded lab can replicate in a quarter. Lead with H3, brand the metric.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen the modular-vs-end-to-end and hybrid-systems waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the hybrid-automaton / hybrid-systems-control idea (guards, modes, reset maps — [[1909.04915|Hybrid-GP-Contact-Model]] is literally a hybrid automaton from 2019/2020) reincarnated as a learned latent inside a deep policy. The field has cycled discrete-symbolic structure ↔ end-to-end continuous several times; discrete structure wins on *data-efficiency and interpretability in low-data regimes* and loses when continuous methods get enough data/capacity.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: [[2203.02468|Predicate-State-Estimation]] (2022, symbolic predicates) and [[1909.04915|Hybrid-GP-Contact-Model]] (hybrid automaton) are the prior-wave symbolic peak; [[2602.23253|SPARR]]/[[2407.16677|ResiP]]/[[2602.14174|Direction-Matters]] are the continuous-end-to-end counter-wave that now matches them. [[2410.00841|Diffusion-Contact-Search]] (A* over mode *sequences*) shows the discrete-planning revival is already underway.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: Discrete-mode structure historically survives only where it buys something continuous methods *structurally cannot* express — here that is the *reversibility decision* (a discrete safe/unsafe predicate), not the dynamics prediction (which continuous models like SPARR already do well). Bet on the part of the discrete structure that is a genuine new *affordance*, not the part that re-litigates discrete-vs-continuous prediction, which history says continuous eventually wins.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *Skeptic vs Academic on H1*: Skeptic says the dynamics-switch is redundant over a binary bit + force control (MATCH already gets 0% breaks; SPARR already owns unseen NIST). Academic says it is novel *as a composition* but only if it beats *multi-state* Predicate-conditioning, not just the binary MATCH — i.e. the real baseline is harder than the card admits.
   - *Practitioner vs Skeptic on H3*: Practitioner says reversibility-gating is the one deployable win and CONTACT Disassembly's R5 collapse proves the need. Skeptic says reversibility only needs a 2-state ([[2210.10765|PAINT]]-style) classifier, so the 5-state machinery is unnecessary overhead for the one part that matters.
   - *Economist vs the card's H1-centric framing*: Economist says the publishable unit is the reversibility *metric* (lead with H3); the card's "FP bet" and falsifier are written H1-first (dynamics-switch on unseen NIST).

2. **Strongest vs weakest perspective.**
   - *Strongest*: ACADEMIC — it is the only one that correctly identifies the real baseline-to-beat ([[2203.02468|Predicate-State-Estimation]], a multi-state generalizing classifier the card down-weights to a "FSM-trigger" footnote) and frames the surviving novelty precisely as the composition, not the discreteness. Grounded in the most load-bearing prior.
   - *Weakest*: HISTORIAN — directionally right (discrete structure survives only where it adds an affordance) but its evidence is the same papers the others cite; it adds framing, not a new decision-relevant fact.

3. **The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction (Skeptic vs Academic on H1).**
   Does conditioning the *dynamics model* on the 5-state latent yield measurably higher unseen-NIST transfer SR than (i) MATCH's binary-bit *controller* switch and (ii) a [[2203.02468|Predicate-State-Estimation]]-style multi-state classifier *gating* the same policy — at matched capacity and matched force bound? If neither delta is significant, H1 collapses and only H3 (reversibility) survives.

4. **What every perspective agrees on (likely true).**
   The **reversibility / wedge-failure axis on extraction (H3) is genuine, uncontested whitespace** — [[2603.08560|CONTACT-Disassembly]]'s graded R1→R5 (95%→15%) collapse is a real unsolved failure surface, no surveyed paper derives a safe-retreat decision *from a contact state*, and [[2210.10765|PAINT]] proves reversibility-gating pays off. All five route value through H3.

5. **The blind spot no perspective addressed.**
   None addressed whether the **reversibility decision is even well-posed as a function of the instantaneous contact mode** alone. Wedging is a *geometric/history-dependent* condition (jam depends on insertion depth, friction-cone angle, accumulated misalignment) — two trajectories can be in the same instantaneous `in-contact` mode with opposite reversibility. If reversibility requires *trajectory history / pose*, not just $c_t$, the card's central mechanism ("the mode tells you whether a retreat is safe") is under-specified, and H3 needs a state-augmented gate, not a mode-derived one.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**(1) CEO summary.** B2's bet is a 5-state physical contact-mode latent that switches *dynamics* and derives a reversibility gate. The honest read after a literature check: the bet is **two papers wearing one coat**. The reversibility/wedge half (H3) is genuine, defensible whitespace on a real and graded failure surface ([[2603.08560|CONTACT-Disassembly]]) that no surveyed work touches; the dynamics-switching half (H1/H2/H4) is at real risk of being redundant over a binary contact bit plus force control ([[2604.19677|MATCH]] already gets 0% breaks) and a continuous residual that already owns unseen NIST ([[2602.23253|SPARR]] +74.5%), and its *true* baseline is not binary-MATCH but the multi-state [[2203.02468|Predicate-State-Estimation]] classifier the card under-weights. Commit to the reversibility framing; de-risk by treating the dynamics-switch as an ablation that must earn its place, not a premise.

**(2) Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
- **F1 (most reliable): The reversibility/wedge axis (H3) is uncontested whitespace.** No surveyed or alphaxiv-discovered paper derives a safe-retreat decision from a contact state; [[2603.08560|CONTACT-Disassembly]] R1→R5 (95%→15%) is real and graded; [[2210.10765|PAINT]] proves the payoff. *Supported by*: all five. *Challenged by*: none — but Skeptic narrows it to needing only a 2-state classifier.
- **F2: The real H1 baseline is multi-state, not binary.** [[2203.02468|Predicate-State-Estimation]] already conditions a policy on a *non-binary* discrete contact state and generalizes unseen at 0.80 acc. *Supported by*: Academic, Historian. *Challenged by*: the card (down-weights it to a "FSM-trigger"); Practitioner (cares about deploy, not baseline taxonomy).
- **F3: The discreteness/"5-state" claim is under-justified; the dynamics-switch may be redundant.** Continuous methods occupy every "missing discrete mode" cell (SPARR, FAVLA 80.8%, [[2602.14174|Direction-Matters]] 91% vs 67%); MATCH gets 0% breaks via force control. *Supported by*: Skeptic, Historian. *Challenged by*: Academic ([[1909.04915|Hybrid-GP-Contact-Model]] is dynamics-side proof discrete beats continuous *as a forward model* in low-data), Practitioner (modes useful for the gate).
- **F4: The publishable, defensible unit is the reversibility metric + extraction benchmark, not the architecture.** Saturated SR ([[2407.08028|AutoMate]] 95–100%) is industry-owned commodity. *Supported by*: Economist, Practitioner. *Challenged by*: none directly; Academic notes the metric still needs the composition to be real.
- **F5 (least settled, highest stakes): Reversibility may not be a function of $c_t$ alone.** Wedging is history/geometry-dependent; same instantaneous mode, opposite reversibility. *Supported by*: implied by Practitioner (label-noise on breaking↔in-contact). *Challenged by*: the card's core mechanism assumes it is mode-derivable. This is the blind spot and the make-or-break risk.

**(3) Hidden cross-perspective connection.** Practitioner ("measure the breaking↔in-contact confusion before any policy work"), Skeptic ("you only need a 2-state reversible/irreversible classifier"), and the blind spot ("reversibility may need history, not just $c_t$") converge on one object: **the reversibility decision boundary is the entire paper, and it is the part most likely to NOT be a clean function of the instantaneous mode.** Three perspectives reach this from different directions — that convergence is the signal.

**(4) Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK, then commit H3-first.** Do NOT commit to the 5-state-dynamics-switch as the headline. Restructure so H3 (reversibility) is the thesis and H1/H2/H4 are supporting ablations. *Concrete next experiment (the gate, ~2 weeks, no policy training yet)*: On [[2603.08560|CONTACT-Disassembly]]'s real R1–R5 extraction suite, collect force-torque + pose trajectories labeled wedged/recoverable at retreat-initiation. Train two predictors of "is a retreat now reversible": (a) from the instantaneous contact mode alone, (b) from mode + short pose/force history ([[2210.10765|PAINT]]-style, history-augmented). If (a) ≈ (b), the mode-derived gate is well-posed and H3 is real — commit. If (b) >> (a), reversibility is history-dependent (F5/blind-spot confirmed), and the contribution pivots to a *history-augmented* reversibility gate — still novel, but a different, honest paper. Either outcome is decision-grade and cheap. Only after this gate clears should H1's three-way ablation (5-state-dynamics-switch vs MATCH-binary vs Predicate-multistate, matched capacity/force) run.

**(5) Frontier question that would change everything.** Is "reversibility of a corrective retreat" a *learnable, sensor-observable predicate at all* under sensor-free / proprioception-only deployment ([[2505.20829|Unified-Force-Position-Control]]-style sensorless force estimation) — or does it fundamentally require a tactile/force channel the deployed robot won't have? If reversibility can be estimated sensor-free, B2 unlocks a deployable safety primitive for the entire E1 sensor-free cluster; if it cannot, the whole bet is gated on hardware most fleets lack.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics professor. The briefing reviewed is the 5-perspective synthesis of B2 (Manipulation.md, lines 481-567). Grounded against the card itself.*

---

##### 1. Confidence scores (each finding, 1-10 for reliability)

- **F1 — Reversibility/wedge axis (H3) is uncontested whitespace. → 8/10.**
  The card backs every factual leg: CONTACT Disassembly R1 95% → R5 15% (line 515) is real and graded; PAINT (line 524) is reversibility-but-mode-blind; MATCH "has zero occurrences of reversib" (line 493). The synthesis is honest that no surveyed paper derives retreat-safety from a contact state. Held back from 9 only because "uncontested" is a claim about *absence*, and the search surface is one card's curated table plus an alphaxiv pass, not a systematic sweep of the assembly/RL-reset literature. PAINT itself shows reversibility-gating is a known idea; the novelty is narrowly the *conditioning on contact mode*, which is a thinner wedge than "uncontested whitespace" connotes.

- **F2 — The real H1 baseline is multi-state, not binary. → 8/10.**
  This is the briefing's sharpest and most defensible move. Predicate State Estimation (line 512) is on the card as a non-binary multi-state classifier conditioning a policy and generalizing unseen at 0.80 acc — and the card *itself* down-weights it to an "FSM-trigger" (line 538), which is precisely the bias F2 names. A reviewer cannot beat a binary strawman (MATCH) and claim multi-state novelty when a 2022 multi-state generalizing classifier already exists. The card already lists it as "H1 baseline-to-beat," so this is less a discovery than a correct insistence that it be weighted as *the* baseline. Docked for the open question of whether predicate-symbolic states (aligned/inserted/contact) are the same construct as physical-dynamics modes (slip/stick/break) — they may not be commensurable, which would partially rescue the novelty.

- **F3 — Discreteness / 5-state under-justified; dynamics-switch may be redundant. → 7/10.**
  Strongly supported on the policy side: continuous methods occupy the cells (SPARR line 506, FAVLA line 508, Direction Matters line 527 at 91% vs 67%), and MATCH gets 0% breaks via force control (line 509). The genuine counter is Academic's: Hybrid-GP (line 526) is real evidence that discrete modes + reset maps beat one continuous model *as a forward dynamics model in low data* (15 trials). So "redundant" is over-stated — it is *unproven at the policy level*, not refuted. The briefing's own remedy (treat the switch as an ablation that must earn its place) is the right calibration, which is why this lands at 7 not lower: the finding correctly identifies risk without over-claiming refutation.

- **F4 — Publishable unit is the reversibility metric + extraction benchmark, not the architecture. → 6/10.**
  Defensible taste (AutoMate 95-100% is saturated, line 506/564), and Economist/Practitioner convergence is real. But this is a *strategic* judgment, not a verifiable fact, and it partly contradicts F1's own caveat that the metric needs a real composition to matter. A reviewer should note the risk the briefing under-weights: a pure "reversibility metric + benchmark re-score on CONTACT Disassembly" contribution, with the architecture demoted to ablation, may read as *incremental benchmark analysis* rather than a method paper — a real venue risk the briefing frames as pure upside. The grade reflects that this is opinion dressed as finding.

- **F5 — Reversibility may not be a function of c_t alone (history/geometry-dependent). → 7/10.**
  This is the most important *insight* and the briefing rightly flags it as make-or-break. First-principles plausible: wedging is path-dependent, two identical instantaneous modes can have opposite reversibility. But it is asserted, not evidenced — there is no citation or data point in the card showing the breaking↔in-contact reversibility confusion empirically. It is a well-posed hypothesis (and the proposed 2-week experiment would settle it), but as a *finding* it is currently a conjecture. Confidence reflects strong priors, zero measurement.

**Mean = 7.2. Min = 6.**

---

##### 2. Weakest link

**F4 (the "publishable unit is the metric, not the architecture" claim, 6/10).** It is the least-anchored to the card and the one most likely to mislead the research plan, because demoting the architecture to an ablation is irreversible-sounding strategic advice presented as a finding. To verify it I would need: (a) a venue-calibration check — has any recent CoRL/RSS/ICRA paper landed on a *reversibility-metric-plus-benchmark-rescore* contribution without a new method, and did it clear review? and (b) confirmation that CONTACT Disassembly's TacFF scoring (line 515) actually exposes a *reversibility* axis distinct from raw extraction success — if the benchmark only reports extraction SR, there is no clean reversibility metric to publish, and F4's "defensible unit" evaporates. F5 is a close second-weakest, but it is honestly labeled a conjecture; F4 is a conjecture labeled a conclusion.

---

##### 3. Bias check — which perspective over-dominated

**Skeptic-dominant, with Practitioner as the amplifier.** Four of five findings (F2, F3, F4, F5) are deflationary "the bet is smaller than claimed" moves, and the CEO summary's framing ("two papers wearing one coat," "de-risk," "earn its place") is pure teardown register. The Skeptic's "you only need a 2-state classifier" and the blind-spot's "history not c_t" and the Practitioner's "measure the confusion first" are explicitly merged into a single object in section (3) — the briefing *celebrates* this convergence as signal, but three deflationary perspectives agreeing is also a structural echo-chamber risk, not independent triangulation. Academic is the only constructive voice (Hybrid-GP as genuine pro-discrete evidence) and it is repeatedly logged as the *challenger* / minority. Economist appears once (F4). The net effect: the synthesis is well-calibrated toward killing the dynamics-switch and may *under-weight the upside case* for it. A balanced synthesis would have given the Hybrid-GP / discrete-dynamics-wins thread its own finding rather than burying it as a challenge to F3.

---

##### 4. Missing perspective (a 6th angle that would change conclusions)

**The Controls / hybrid-systems theorist.** Every perspective here treats "5-state discrete mode" as a *learning* design choice to be ablated empirically. None asks the prior question a hybrid-dynamical-systems person asks first: is a 5-state *partition* even the right granularity, or is contact a continuum where any fixed discretization (5 or otherwise) is a modeling artifact that will leak at the boundaries (chattering at mode switches, Zeno behavior near the friction-cone edge)? This reframes the whole bet. The empirical question "5-state vs binary vs multi-state" (H1) presupposes a *correct* discretization exists; the controls theorist would argue the continuous methods (SPARR, Direction Matters' normal-force direction) are winning precisely because contact mode is *not* cleanly piecewise at policy timescales, only at the instantaneous-dynamics timescale Hybrid-GP operates on. That distinction — discrete is real for a *forward model* (Hybrid-GP) but possibly not for a *runtime policy latent* — is the actual crux, and it would push the conclusion from "demote dynamics-switch to ablation" toward "the discreteness may be a category error at the policy layer, and the publishable object is the reversibility *predicate*, full stop." It sharpens F4 and F5 into a single thesis the current five perspectives only circle.

---

##### 5. Overall grade + top fixes

**Grade: A-.**

This is a strong, intellectually honest review-of-a-review. It does the hard thing — it argues *against* its own card's headline (the 5-state dynamics-switch), correctly identifies the real baseline the card under-weights (Predicate State Estimation), and proposes a cheap, decision-grade, falsification-first experiment whose *both* outcomes are publishable. The confidence ordering (H3 most reliable, F5 least settled) is sound. It loses the plus tier because: (i) it over-rotates on the Skeptic and lets three deflationary perspectives masquerade as independent triangulation; (ii) F4 is strategic opinion presented with finding-level confidence and partly self-contradicts F1; (iii) it never interrogates whether a 5-state partition is the right object at all (the missing controls lens), which is the deepest available critique and the one a Stanford committee would press hardest.

**Top fixes, in order:**
1. **Re-status F4 from "finding" to "recommendation," and stress-test it.** Before demoting the architecture, confirm CONTACT Disassembly actually exposes a reversibility metric distinct from extraction SR (line 515) — if it only reports SR, the "metric + benchmark" contribution does not exist and the whole de-risk strategy needs the architecture back.
2. **Promote the Hybrid-GP / discrete-dynamics-wins thread to a standalone finding.** Right now the strongest pro-bet evidence is buried as a "challenge to F3." Give the upside case equal billing so the synthesis is not a foregone teardown.
3. **Run the section-(4) experiment first and literally — but add the controls-theorist control.** Beyond (a) mode-alone vs (b) mode+history reversibility predictors, add (c) a *continuous* force-history predictor with *no* discrete mode. If (c) ties (a), the discreteness is a category error at the policy layer (the missing 6th perspective), and F3/F5 collapse into one clean conclusion.
4. **Disambiguate "predicate state" vs "physical dynamics mode."** F2's whole force depends on Predicate State Estimation being the *same* construct as the 5 physical modes. If symbolic predicates (aligned/inserted) are not commensurable with dynamics modes (slip/stick/break), the novelty claim is partially restored and H1's baseline framing needs revision.

---

### C (Cluster review)

Topic: the coherence and central shared-bottleneck thesis of Cluster C — Bimanual & Dual-Arm Coordination ("Two-arm coupling is non-factorizable and bimanual data is scarce"; intro: "composing single-arm priors, generating data with coordination structure, and sensing the inter-arm force that vision cannot see"). The question under review: does this grouping carve the bimanual problem at the right joint (C1 policy / C2 data / C3 force), and is the shared bet — that the *coupling* is a separable, structured, learnable object — sound enough to commit to as a publication bet?

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys daily).**
- *Core position*: The cluster's three-way split (policy / data / force) maps cleanly onto the real bottlenecks I hit — but the shared "coupling is separable" thesis is an *architecture* convenience that the hardware does not respect. On a real bimanual rig the coupling is the synchronized teleop channel, the wrist-camera occlusion during handover, and the sub-ms timing skew between two arm controllers — none of which a "separable coupling term" captures.
- *Strongest evidence*: TwinVLA ([[2511.05275|TwinVLA]]) actually delivers the practitioner's dream — 76% on ~50 episodes / ~25 H100-days vs RDT-1B's thousands-of-GPU-day pretraining — so composition is genuinely the cheap deployable path. But the same card shows the data substrate is teleop-bound: DuoBench ([[2606.11901|DuoBench]]) needed 750 teleop episodes / 442,907 frames, and UME ([[2606.14218|UME]]) only gets force-observable data via a torque-feedback exoskeleton (3.3× efficiency, but bespoke hardware). VTouch++'s 120K synchronized episodes ([[2604.20444|VTouch++]]) is the rare exception, and it took industrial scale.
- *Only-this-perspective*: C3's shared-force-channel bet is gated on instrumentation almost no lab has — synchronized bimanual tactile *plus* through-object force. Before committing, confirm a platform exists where you can measure the "transmitted coupling" the first principle is about; otherwise C3 is a data-collection paper wearing a representation-learning hat.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the literature).**
- *Core position*: The cluster's framing is honest about what is *settled* (composition ≥ monolith; tactile > vision; structure-aware generation works) and correctly relocates the novelty to second-order questions. That is good taste, but it means the contribution is a *measurement/isolation* paper, not a new-mechanism paper — and the field rewards mechanisms.
- *Strongest evidence*: The card already concedes the settled headlines across *multiple* coupling primitives — Joint-Attention ([[2511.05275|TwinVLA]]), energy-sum ([[2603.20236|EnergyAction]] 86.4% RLBench2), skill-recompose ([[2603.03836|SkillVLA]] 0%→51%), capacity-efficient decoupling ([[2503.09186|Decoupled-Bimanual]] +23.5% at 1/6 size). That breadth is exactly what makes "composition beats monolith" robust *and* unpublishable on its own. The live questions (C1-H2 data-floor on 4×-harder BiCoord tasks, C1-H4 coupling-localized late-degradation, C2-H1 structure-vs-volume, C3-H1 shared-vs-per-arm) are all genuinely un-run in the cited set.
- *Only-this-perspective*: ATOM-Bench's AS/CFS metrics ([[2606.16826|ATOM-Bench]]) already separate skill-execution failure from composition failure (compositional SR 15.8% despite AS 83.3%) — that is *almost* C1-H4's instrument. The academic warning: H4's "localize degradation to the coupling" may be answerable today with existing benchmark instruments, which would make it a measurement note, not a paper.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks it is wrong or already solved).**
- *Core position*: The "coupling is separable, structured, and cheap" thesis quietly assumes coordination *is* a factorizable term — but the strongest cited evidence is that coordination is *emergent and entangled*, and the most threatening new paper learns directed coupling end-to-end without any separable coupling term at all.
- *Strongest evidence (the card under-weights)*: (a) Imaginative Coordination ([[2507.11296|Imaginative-Coordination]], +24.9% ALOHA) and Bimanual Flow Video Prediction ([[2505.24156|Bimanual-Flow-Video-Prediction]]) get coordination from a *shared imagined/predicted future* — coupling is implicit in a world-model, never separated, and it works. (b) Physics-Driven Data Gen ([[2502.20382|Physics-Driven-Data-Gen]]) treats *both arms as one combined kinematic chain* with *no coordination primitives* and still scales 26%→74% — a structure-*free* result the C2 card itself admits is the missing matched-volume foil, but which also directly threatens C2's "structure carries it" prediction. (c) On C1's directed-coupling sub-bet, [[2606.16490|SAI]] (Sequential Asymmetric Imitation, uncited) learns *directed asymmetric* coordination — phase-alignment, yielding, conflict-mitigation — through a curriculum with *no explicit coupling term*, reporting the exact phase-sync/yield metrics C1 wants, and Co-VLA ([[2606.20285|Co-VLA], uncited) is a coordination-aware structured-action VLA on the same axis. The skeptic's verdict: "directed/asymmetric coupling is the missing primitive" (C1-H3) is partially pre-empted.
- *Only-this-perspective*: If coordination is reliably learnable as an emergent property (video-prediction, combined-kinematic-chain, curriculum), then the cluster's entire "separable coupling object" framing is a hypothesis the cited evidence is *already voting against*. The bet survives only on the narrow claim that *isolation/typing* beats *emergence at matched data* — which no cited paper has falsified, but several have rendered non-obvious.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows incentives).**
- *Core position*: This cluster is shaped by a benchmark-and-compute-asymmetry incentive: academia cannot afford π0/RDT-scale bimanual pretraining, so "composition + isolation on ~50 episodes / ~25 H100-days" is the *only* economically viable bimanual story for a solo researcher — which is why the card (rightly) anchors there.
- *Strongest evidence*: The cost gap is explicit — TwinVLA 76% at ~25 H100-days vs RDT-1B's "thousands of hours" ([[2511.05275|TwinVLA]]); DexFlyWheel beats DexMimicGen "with 10× less human data" ([[2509.23829|DexFlyWheel]]); RoboTwin's "300 sim + 20 real ≈ 300 real" ([[2504.13059|RoboTwin]]). Every flagship result is a *data/compute-efficiency* claim — the genre academia is forced into. The proliferation of bimanual benchmarks (BiCoord, BiGym, RoCo, ATOM-Bench, DuoBench, PerAct2, RoboEval, TeleOpBench) signals a land-grab for the *evaluation* layer, where a solo lab can plant a flag cheaply.
- *Only-this-perspective*: The incentive risk is benchmark-saturation: RoboTwin 2.0 / BiCoord are this cycle's LIBERO, and a paper whose contribution is "isolate structure-vs-volume on these benchmarks" rides a wave that may crest before review. The economically durable asset is the *instrument* (a coupling-vs-skill decomposition metric), not the SR delta — own the metric, not the number.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen the waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the modular-vs-end-to-end pendulum and the pixel-vs-latent world-model cycle replaying on two arms. The cluster bets on the *modular/decomposed* swing ("separable coupling") right as the field's strongest new results (video-prediction coupling, combined-kinematic-chain) are the *end-to-end/emergent* counter-swing — exactly the tension that decided prior waves.
- *Strongest evidence*: The card's own table encodes the pendulum: explicit-coupling architectures (InterACT [[2409.07914|InterACT]] CLS-token sync, Dual-Level MoE [[2606.13279|Dual-Level-Bimanual-VLA]], EquiBim [[2603.08541|EquiBim]] symmetry prior) vs emergent-coordination monoliths (Imaginative Coordination, RDT-1B). Classical model-based coupling (BiRP [[2307.05933|BiRP]] TP-GMM+LQT, 2023; Bimanual Tactile-Reactive MPC [[2509.22421|Bimanual-Tactile-Reactive-MPC]] learned coupling matrix Cf) is the *prior wave's* explicit-coupling answer — and history shows learned-emergent usually wins on scale while explicit wins on data-scarcity and interpretability, which is precisely the regime this cluster targets.
- *Only-this-perspective*: Every prior "decompose the hard joint" wave (options/hierarchical RL, modular sim2real) won early on sample-efficiency and lost late on asymptotic performance. The historian's bet: the cluster will be *right at the ~50-episode regime and wrong at scale* — so frame the contribution as "the data-scarce coupling regime," not "the way to do bimanual," and the bet ages well.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *SKEPTIC vs ACADEMIC/CARD on separability*: Card asserts the coupling is "a separable inter-arm coupling term" (C1 FP); Skeptic cites Imaginative Coordination, Physics-Driven Data Gen ([[2502.20382|Physics-Driven-Data-Gen]] combined-kinematic-chain, no primitives), and SAI ([[2606.16490|SAI]] emergent directed coordination) as evidence coordination works *without* a separable term. The specific clash: is the coupling an *object you isolate* (card) or a *property that emerges* (skeptic)?
   - *PRACTITIONER vs CARD on C3 instrumentation*: Card treats the shared inter-arm force channel as a learnable representation given VTouch++'s 120K episodes; Practitioner says the "transmitted coupling" quantity requires through-object force sensing that almost no platform has — the bet's first principle may be unmeasurable on available hardware.
   - *ECONOMIST vs ACADEMIC on where value sits*: Economist says own the *metric/instrument* (durable); Academic warns the instrument (ATOM-Bench AS/CFS, RoboEval progression) may *already exist*, collapsing C1-H4 to a measurement note.
   - *HISTORIAN vs CARD on scope*: Card frames the bet as the structure of *bimanual coupling* broadly; Historian insists it is only valid in the data-scarce (~50-episode) regime and loses at scale.

2. **Strongest vs weakest perspective.**
   - *Strongest: SKEPTIC* — it is the only perspective grounded in *new* evidence outside the card (SAI 2606.16490, Co-VLA 2606.20285) that materially moves the novelty verdict, and it correctly identifies that the card's own cited results (video-prediction coupling, combined-kinematic-chain) vote against the separability premise.
   - *Weakest: ECONOMIST* — accurate on incentives but offers no falsifiable technical claim; its "own the metric" advice is strategy, not evidence, and partly duplicates the Academic's instrument observation.

3. **The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction (separable vs emergent).**
   At *matched data and matched compute*, does an explicitly-isolated/typed coupling term beat an emergent-coordination monolith (video-prediction or combined-kinematic-chain) on BiCoord's coordination metrics (SMT/SMP) and its later-stage degradation — i.e., is there *any* operating point where isolation strictly dominates emergence? If yes anywhere, the cluster commits; if emergence ties everywhere at matched data, the separability thesis is dead and only the data/instrument contributions survive.

4. **What every perspective agrees on (likely true).**
   The settled headlines are real and the *shared bottleneck is correctly identified*: two-arm coordination is non-factorizable in the sense that it carries non-additive value, bimanual data is genuinely scarce/expensive, and the cheap viable path for a solo researcher is composition-of-single-arm-priors + structure-aware generation + a tactile channel — all five perspectives accept TwinVLA's ~50-episode / ~25-H100-day result and the tactile>vision contrast (VT-Refine ~40% lift) as established. The *cluster grouping* (policy / data / force) carves the problem at a defensible joint.

5. **The blind spot (addressed by none).**
   *Asymmetric / >2-arm and human-in-the-loop coordination as the real frontier.* Every perspective debated two symmetric robot arms; none addressed that the highest-value coordination is asymmetric or multi-agent (Decoupled Bimanual's +28% three-arm, SAI's mobile dual-*robot* collaboration, Intent-Aware Co-Manipulation human-robot co-carry [[2409.00215|Intent-Aware-Co-Manipulation]]). The cluster's "two-arm coupling" framing may be under-scoped relative to where the field and the money are heading — physical human-robot and robot-robot collaboration where the partner is *not* controllable.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**CEO summary.** Cluster C is a *coherent and well-bounded* theme: its three directions decompose bimanual manipulation along a defensible joint (coordination-native policy / coordination-structured data / inter-arm force), and they share a single, correctly-identified bottleneck — non-factorizable two-arm coupling under scarce data. The cluster is intellectually honest: it explicitly marks the settled headlines (composition ≥ monolith, tactile > vision, structure-aware generation works) as background and relocates its novelty to crisp, un-run second-order questions (C2's structure-vs-volume isolation, C3's shared-force-channel + balance, C1's coupling-localized degradation). The vulnerability is not coherence but the cluster's *central premise* that the coupling is a *separable object* — several of its own cited results (video-prediction coordination, combined-kinematic-chain generation) and one uncited threat (SAI's emergent directed coordination) show coordination working *without* any separable coupling term, which makes the bet a contest of "isolation/typing vs emergence at matched data," not a settled win. Commit selectively: C2 and C3 hold as whitespace; C1's "directed coupling is the missing primitive" sub-claim is the most exposed.

**5 key findings (ranked by reliability).**
1. *The cluster grouping and shared bottleneck are sound* (policy/data/force, non-factorizable coupling + data scarcity). Supported by ALL five perspectives; challenged by none. Highest reliability.
2. *The settled headlines are genuinely settled* — composition ≥ monolith across ≥4 primitives ([[2511.05275|TwinVLA]], [[2603.20236|EnergyAction]], [[2603.03836|SkillVLA]], [[2503.09186|Decoupled-Bimanual]]); tactile > vision ([[2510.14930|VT-Refine]] ~40% lift). Supported by Academic, Practitioner, Historian; this is exactly *why* they are not publishable alone (Academic, Economist). High reliability.
3. *C2's structure-vs-volume isolation and C3's shared-force-channel + lifted-balance-loss are real un-run whitespace.* Supported by Academic (un-run in cited set) and confirmed by alphaxiv (no paper runs the matched-volume strip-structure ablation; no shared-inter-arm-force + two-arm balance-loss paper exists; card's own [[2502.20382|Physics-Driven-Data-Gen]] is the missing structure-free foil, [[2509.22421|Bimanual-Tactile-Reactive-MPC]]/[[2602.13689|Symmetry-Aware-VT-Fusion]] are the closest force points). Challenged mildly by Skeptic (Physics-Driven Data Gen's structure-free 26%→74% pre-shadows a possible null result for C2-H1). Medium-high reliability.
4. *C1's "directed/typed coupling is the missing primitive" is partially pre-empted.* [[2606.16490|SAI]] (uncited) learns directed asymmetric coordination (phase-align/yield/conflict-mitigation) via curriculum with no explicit coupling term and reports C1's target metrics; [[2606.20285|Co-VLA]] (uncited) is a coordination-aware structured-action VLA on the same axis. Surfaced by Skeptic via alphaxiv; Historian frames it as the emergent counter-swing. The *typed async/sync/ordered + data-floor-on-BiCoord* slice survives, but the headline "directed beats symmetric" is now contested. Medium reliability.
5. *The bet is regime-bound: right at ~50 episodes, likely wrong at scale.* Supported by Historian (every prior decompose-the-joint wave won early on sample-efficiency, lost late) and Economist (academia is forced into the data-efficiency genre); challenged by no direct evidence but unfalsified at scale. Medium/speculative reliability — actionable as scoping, not as a result.

**Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Practitioner's "the coupling is the synchronized teleop channel" and the Skeptic's "coordination emerges without a separable term" are the *same observation* from opposite ends: the coupling is hard to isolate precisely because in practice it lives in the *data-collection process* (synchronized teleop, partner distribution) rather than in the policy architecture — which is exactly why SAI ([[2606.16490|SAI]]) induces it through a *curriculum* and why C2 (data) may be the cluster's true center of gravity, with C1's architectural coupling term being downstream of the data that encodes it.

**Actionable insight for the researcher (COMMIT / DE-RISK / DROP).**
*DE-RISK, then COMMIT to C2+C3; reframe C1.*
- **C1**: Do NOT lead with "directed coupling is the missing primitive" — [[2606.16490|SAI]] and [[2606.20285|Co-VLA]] have pre-empted the directed/asymmetric framing. Cite both, and narrow C1's surviving novelty to the *typed (async/sync/ordered) coupling-vs-single-layer + the BiCoord data-floor sweep* (H2/H3-typed), which remains un-run. *First de-risking experiment*: the single resolving experiment from Prompt 2 — at matched data/compute, run typed-coupling composition vs an emergent monolith (Imaginative-Coordination-style) vs SAI-curriculum, scored on BiCoord SMT/SMP + ATOM-Bench AS/CFS, to find whether isolation strictly dominates emergence at *any* operating point. If it does not, fold C1 into C2.
- **C2 (commit)**: Run the clean structure-vs-volume ablation H1 (strip DexMimicGen's sync/ordering at matched trajectory volume on a differentiable generator, [[2604.03552|CRAFT]] / [[2505.04860|D-CODA]]), with [[2502.20382|Physics-Driven-Data-Gen]] as the pre-registered structure-free comparator and [[2507.00435|RoboEval]] progression metrics so a binary-SR tie cannot mask a coordination collapse. This is the cluster's highest-leverage, least-pre-empted experiment.
- **C3 (commit, gated)**: First confirm a platform measures through-object transmitted force (Practitioner's gate); then run shared-channel vs per-arm vs explicit-pairwise-Cf ([[2509.22421|Bimanual-Tactile-Reactive-MPC]]) on hold-and-manipulate, and lift [[2602.13689|Symmetry-Aware-VT-Fusion]]'s balance loss from two fingers to two arms on handover. No pre-emption found.

**Frontier question that would change everything.** Is two-arm coordination ever a *separable* object, or is it irreducibly a property of the joint state-action distribution that only the data-generation process (synchronized teleop, partner distribution, combined-kinematic-chain) can encode? If the latter, the entire cluster collapses into C2 — the *only* place to "install" coordination is the data, and every architectural coupling term (C1) and force representation (C3) is a downstream readout of what the data already contains.
