#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics faculty. Cluster C (Bimanual & Dual-Arm Coordination), `Capability/Manipulation.md` lines 568-817, read in full. Two load-bearing pre-emption papers (SAI 2606.16490, Co-VLA 2606.20285) fetched from arXiv to check the briefing's central recommendation; both are absent from the vault and appear only in upstream review parts (008-C-1-analyst, 009-C1-1-analyst), so the briefing inherited them rather than grounding them.*

---

##### 1. Confidence scores (1-10, reliability)

| # | Finding | Score | One-line justification |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Cluster grouping + shared bottleneck (policy/data/force; non-factorizable coupling + data scarcity) are sound | **9** | The three directions decompose along a genuinely defensible joint and the card's own tables sustain it; "all five perspectives support, none challenge" is consistent with the text. Docking 1 only because "non-factorizable" is asserted as premise, not proven — the frontier question concedes this. |
| 2 | The settled headlines are genuinely settled (composition ≥ monolith across ≥4 primitives; tactile > vision ~40%) | **8** | Well-sourced: Decoupled Bimanual +23.5% at 1/6 size (line 602), TwinVLA/EnergyAction/SkillVLA across attention/energy/skill primitives, VT-Refine ~40% lift (line 748). "Settled" is fair for the headline; the precise "≥4 primitives" count is generous but the direction is right. |
| 3 | C2 structure-vs-volume isolation + C3 shared-channel/balance-loss are real un-run whitespace | **7** | The card itself names the missing foils (Physics-Driven Data Gen 2502.20382 structure-free at line 703; Symmetry-Aware VT Fusion is two-finger-not-two-arm at line 765; no shared-vs-per-arm force comparison). Skeptic's caveat that Physics-Driven Data Gen's 26%→74% pre-shadows a C2-H1 *null* is real and the briefing under-weights it: that paper is volume-scaled AND structure-free AND already wins, which is exactly the falsifier, not just a "mild" challenge. |
| 4 | C1 "directed/typed coupling is the missing primitive" is partially pre-empted (SAI, Co-VLA) | **5** | Verified independently and the briefing is *overstated*. SAI (2606.16490) is real but is two-bimanual-**mobile-manipulator** robot-robot collaboration, not within-platform bimanual — a different setting (RoboFactory/multi-agent lane), and its "reports C1's target metrics (BiCoord SMT/SMP)" claim is unverified and almost certainly false (different benchmark). Co-VLA (2606.20285) is the genuine threat (shared+residual latent action expert, modulates "execution asymmetry") but its abstract reports no named directed/asymmetric metrics, so it does not close the "directed beats symmetric" headline either. Net: pre-emption is real but softer than stated, and both papers are uncited *and not in the vault*. |
| 5 | The bet is regime-bound (right at ~50 episodes, likely wrong at scale) | **4** | Honest scoping intuition, but unfalsified and asymmetric: the Historian's "every decompose-the-joint wave lost late" is a pattern-match, not evidence, and the card's own VLBiMan (one-demo, 85.3%) and BiCICLe (training-free) data points sit *below* 50 episodes, complicating a clean "50-episode regime." Actionable as a scoping caveat only, as the briefing concedes. |

**Mean = 6.6, min = 4.**

---

##### 2. Weakest link

**Finding 4's pre-emption claim — specifically "SAI 2606.16490 learns directed asymmetric coordination… and reports C1's target metrics."** This is the single claim the whole actionable recommendation ("do NOT lead with directed coupling; fold C1 into C2 if it loses") pivots on, and it is the least grounded: (a) the paper is not in the KnowledgeHub and was inherited from an upstream review part, not the card; (b) on direct fetch, SAI is *Sequential Asymmetric Imitation for two bimanual mobile manipulators coordinating through shared objects* — a robot-robot / multi-agent setting, not single-platform bimanual, which is C1's universe; (c) there is no evidence SAI evaluates on BiCoord SMT/SMP or ATOM-Bench AS/CFS at all. **To verify:** read SAI's experimental section for (i) platform = one bimanual robot vs two robots, (ii) benchmark = BiCoord/PerAct2/TWIN vs a custom mobile-manip suite, (iii) whether any reported metric is comparable to SMT/SMP. If SAI is robot-robot on a custom suite, it does NOT pre-empt C1; it belongs in the multi-agent generation lane (next to RoboFactory 2503.16408, already in C2) and the "reframe C1" recommendation collapses to "cite Co-VLA, sharpen H3." Co-VLA alone is a citation-and-narrowing matter, not a fold-into-C2 matter.

Secondary weak link: the briefing frames C1's "directed coupling is missing" as the exposed sub-claim, but the card never claims directed coupling is missing from the *literature* — it cites BiCICLe (2604.20348) as exactly the directed leader-follower primitive (lines 620, 638-642) and builds H3 on it. C1's actual claim is narrower (typed *and learned* async/sync/ordered + directed coupling is untested as a learned conditioning). The briefing slightly mis-aims its strongest critique at a claim the card did not make.

---

##### 3. Bias check — which perspective over-dominated

**The Skeptic over-dominated, amplified by the Historian.** The synthesis's spine — CEO summary, hidden connection, frontier question — all resolve to one Skeptic thesis: "coordination emerges without a separable term, so the cluster collapses into C2." Findings 4 and 5 and the entire "reframe C1" action are Skeptic-driven, and the frontier question is a pure Skeptic restatement. The Academic and Practitioner are cited mainly to *agree* (Findings 1-3 are consensus-flavored). The Economist appears only as a one-line "academia is forced into data-efficiency" garnish. The cost of this imbalance: the Skeptic's emergence evidence (Imaginative Coordination video-prediction, Physics-Driven Data Gen combined-kinematic-chain, SAI curriculum) is treated as *defeating* the separability premise, when at matched data it is exactly the contest the cluster proposes to run — emergence-without-a-term is a baseline to beat, not a proof the term is absent. A balanced synthesis would have let the Practitioner push back: in deployed bimanual, the coupling is instrumented and named (force/torque channels, synchronized teleop), which is evidence *for* a separable object, not against.

---

##### 4. Missing perspective (a 6th angle that would change conclusions)

**The Controls / dynamics-and-contact-mechanics perspective** (a roboticist who thinks in wrenches, closed kinematic chains, and force closure rather than in policy architectures or data pipelines). This angle changes the frontier question's answer. Both arms grasping one object form a *closed kinematic chain*: the inter-arm coupling is then not a learned abstraction at all but a hard physical constraint (the grasp matrix / internal-wrench null space), and the "shared transmitted force" in C3 is the well-defined internal force of the closed chain — a quantity classical bimanual control (Uchiyama-Dauchez symmetric formulation, internal/external wrench decomposition) has represented explicitly for thirty years. Under this lens: (a) C3's "shared vs per-arm" is not open whitespace but a re-derivation of internal-force control, and the novelty must be re-pitched as *learned* internal-wrench estimation without F/T sensing, not as the existence of a shared channel; (b) the frontier question's dichotomy is false — coordination is separable *whenever the chain is closed* (rigid co-manipulation) and emergent *only when it is open* (sequential handover, loose coupling), which would split C1/C3's claims by contact topology rather than fold everything into C2. The card's BiRP (2307.05933, classical relative-parameterization, line 621) and Intent-Aware Co-Manipulation (2409.00215) gesture at this but are treated as historical background; a controls reviewer would promote them to the load-bearing prior that reframes the whole separability debate.

---

##### 5. Overall grade + top fixes

**Grade: B+.**

A genuinely strong, intellectually honest briefing: it correctly separates settled headlines from second-order whitespace, the C2 and C3 commitments are well-targeted and least-pre-empted, the hidden cross-perspective connection (coupling lives in the data-collection process) is a real insight, and the actionable COMMIT/DE-RISK/DROP structure is decision-ready. It loses the A tier on three avoidable over-reaches, all in the C1/Skeptic spine.

**Top fixes, in priority order:**

1. **Re-ground the SAI pre-emption before acting on it.** Confirm whether 2606.16490 is single-platform bimanual or two-robot mobile collaboration (it is the latter on my fetch). If robot-robot, demote SAI from "C1 pre-emption" to "multi-agent generation lane (with RoboFactory)" and strike the "reports C1's target metrics" claim. The "fold C1 into C2" recommendation should be conditioned on Co-VLA alone, which justifies only *cite-and-narrow*, not *fold*.

2. **Stop treating emergence as a defeater; make it the registered baseline.** Reword Finding 4 and the frontier question so emergence-without-a-separable-term (Imaginative Coordination, Physics-Driven Data Gen, Co-VLA) is the comparator C1's matched-data experiment must beat, not evidence the separable object does not exist. The card's H1 (composition vs monolith at matched data) is exactly this contest; the briefing should align to it rather than imply the contest is already lost.

3. **Upgrade the C2-H1 null risk from "mild."** Physics-Driven Data Gen (2502.20382) is structure-free, volume-scaled, AND already hits 74% on real bimanual (line 703) — that is the falsifier pre-firing, not a footnote. The C2 commitment should pre-register that a structure-free volume baseline may *win*, and frame C2's contribution as the clean isolation even if the sign comes out against the thesis. A null here is still publishable as the field's first controlled structure-vs-volume measurement, and saying so de-risks the commit.

4. (Minor) Add one sentence aiming C1's surviving novelty at the right target — learned *typed* coupling — and explicitly acknowledge the card already cites BiCICLe as the hand-structured directed primitive, so the gap is "learned, not prompted" directed conditioning. This prevents the reviewer-visible mismatch between the briefing's critique and the card's actual claim.

---

### C1 (Direction review)

Topic: "Coordination-Native Bimanual Policies" — the bet that the two-arm coupling is a *separable* term (skill = transferable single-arm prior; coupling = the only bimanual-specific part), and that this coupling has **measurable structure (typed: async/sync/ordered; directed vs symmetric), a low data-floor (~50 episodes even on 4×-harder tasks), and a localizable late-stage breakdown** that composition exposes and a monolith cannot. The composition-beats-monolith *headline* is conceded as settled; surviving novelty lives entirely in H2 (data-floor), H3 (typed/directed coupling), H4 (coupling-vs-skill failure localization).

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily)**
- *Core position*: The "compose frozen single-arm priors + thin coupling" recipe is the only one that pencils out on real-robot data budgets, and the card's ~50-episode floor ([[2511.05275|TwinVLA]]) is the single most deploy-relevant number in the cluster. But the bet's *separability assumption* breaks exactly where deployment hurts most: tight handover + force balance, where the coupling and the single-arm skill are not cleanly factorable because contact forces re-enter each arm's own control loop.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: TwinVLA 76% / ~50 episodes / ~25 H100-days vs RDT-1B 45%; [[2503.09186|Decoupled-Bimanual]] +23.5% at **1/6 the model size** — capacity savings are real deploy wins. [[2509.21723|VLBiMan]] 85.3%/78.0% from **one** demo + 83.8% cross-embodiment-no-retrain is the deploy dream. Counter-signal: [[2606.20285|Co-VLA]] needed a deployment-time *Latent-Aware Controller* (stiffness modulation, low-pass filtering) on top of the structural decomposition — SAE-alone *decreased* Handover-ID (63%→57%) until LAC was added — i.e. the coupling does not cleanly survive the sim-to-deploy jitter that the card never models.
- *Only-this-perspective insight*: The card measures the coupling's data-floor in *episodes* but never in *teleop channels / operator-hours*. [[2606.16490|Sequential-Asymmetric-Imitation]] and [[2605.29298|MonoDuo] show the real bottleneck is **synchronized dual-operator demonstration**, not episode count — a coupling learnable from single-arm/single-operator data is worth more than one with a low episode-floor but requiring synchronized bimanual teleop. Reframe H2 around collection modality, not episode count.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature)**
- *Core position*: The literature already broadly confirms composition-beats-monolith across *five+* coupling primitives (attention/energy/codebook/symmetry/state-diffusion), so the card is right to retire H1. But the evidence base for the surviving hypotheses is thinner and partly contradictory: "typed coupling" (H3) is no longer untouched, and no paper yet runs the frozen-prior data-floor sweep (H2) or the coupling-vs-skill localization (H4) cleanly.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: For factorization-is-mechanism-independent: [[2603.20236|EnergyAction]] (86.4% RLBench2, +32.5% data-eff @20 demos), [[2603.03836|SkillVLA]] (0%→51% unseen pairings), [[2605.13452|CUBic]], [[2503.23271|Coordinated-Bimanual-State-Diffusion]], [[2603.08541|EquiBim]]. For H3 *partially pre-empted*: [[2606.20285|Co-VLA]] already routes **task-adaptive coordination losses by regime** — L_sparse (symmetric), L_shared (asymmetric role), L_sync (temporal) — and ablates them, finding L_shared wins role-asymmetric "Put Object Cabinet" (49%→70%) and L_sync wins temporally-coupled "Place Bread Skillet" (51%→62%). That *is* a coupling-typed-by-coordination-regime result. For the directed primitive H3 also leans on: [[2604.20348|BiCICLe]] 70.5% TWIN training-free.
- *Only-this-perspective insight*: The benchmark substrate H3/H4 *need* now exists ([[2407.00278|PerAct2]] coupling-type labels, [[2606.11901|DuoBench]] 4-type taxonomy + stage-failure, [[2606.16826|ATOM-Bench]] Atomic-Score / Compositional-Failure-Share). ATOM-Bench already separates skill-execution failure (AS 83.3%) from composition failure (compositional SR 15.8%) — so H4's *instrument* is published; the card's contribution narrows to applying it to a **composed-prior** policy with frozen single-arm skills, which ATOM-Bench did not.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks the bet is wrong or already solved)**
- *Core position*: The "settled headline + live H2/H3/H4" framing is doing a lot of load-bearing work to keep a thin slice novel after the headline was conceded — and [[2606.20285|Co-VLA]] (Jun 2026) has already eaten most of H3 by typing the coupling by coordination regime *and* showing different types need different structural losses. What's left (H2 frozen-prior data-floor + H4 localization) is an ablation suite, not a thesis.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: Co-VLA's own ablation *is* the H3 falsifier-adjacent test the card proposes to run, and it concluded coordination is regime-dependent — i.e. the card's H3 "typed beats single layer" is likely *true but already shown*. Worse for novelty: Co-VLA explicitly states "collaboration is not an action, it is a *structure over actions*" — verbatim the card's thesis. On H4: [[2604.05831|BiCoord]] *already reports* late-stage degradation, and [[2606.11901|DuoBench]] already does stage-based failure attribution showing most failures at the *earliest grasp-acquisition* stage — which *contradicts* H4's premise that the dangerous breakdown is *late* and *coupling*-localized.
- *Only-this-perspective insight*: The card never confronts that DuoBench's empirical failure mode (earliest-stage grasp acquisition, a *single-arm skill* failure) directly undercuts H4's "late degradation localizes to coupling." If the dominant real failure is early single-arm grasp, the coupling-localization story is testing the wrong stage. That is the evidence the card conveniently omits.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives)**
- *Core position*: This direction is structurally favored by academic-lab economics — it converts the one resource solo/small labs lack (bimanual-scale proprietary data + thousands of GPU-days, owned by Microsoft/π0/RDT-1B/Samsung) into the one they have (a pretrained single-arm prior + ~50 episodes + 25 H100-days). The "coupling is cheap and structured" narrative is the academic counter-move to the foundation-model data moat — which makes it *attractive* and also *crowded*.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: TwinVLA from Yonsei+Microsoft, Co-VLA from Donghua+**Samsung**, Decoupled Bimanual from SYSU+Imperial — the composition narrative is being pushed by *both* resource-poor academics (efficiency story) *and* hardware/industry labs (Samsung wants deployable dual-arm without proprietary data). Benchmark proliferation ([[2604.05831|BiCoord]], [[2606.11901|DuoBench]], [[2606.16826|ATOM-Bench]], [[2407.00278|PerAct2]], [[2606.16826|ATOM-Bench]]) signals a field racing to *define the metric* before saturation — classic pre-saturation land-grab.
- *Only-this-perspective insight*: The crowding is the risk, not the gap. Five benchmarks and ~20 coupling-method papers in ~15 months means the H2/H3/H4 ablations will be *independently run by 3+ groups within a publication cycle*; the defensible asset is not the hypotheses but **owning a metric** — e.g. a "coupling data-floor curve vs coupling-tightness" that becomes the cited denominator. Bet on the instrument, not the result.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves)**
- *Core position*: This is the bimanual replay of the **modular-vs-end-to-end** swing and the **residual-learning** lineage: "freeze a strong base, learn a thin correction/coupling on top" is exactly DR-residual-RL, ResidualPolicy, and the adapter/LoRA pattern transplanted to two arms. Those waves taught that the residual/coupling term *is* cheap and structured *until the base is wrong*, at which point the residual silently absorbs base error and the factorization story collapses.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: Co-VLA's SAE = shared latent + arm *residual* latents with additive composition (a_L = a_shared + a_residual) is literally residual decomposition; SkillVLA's scalar-α gate echoes mixture/gating-network history; the field's own pendulum already swung once here — [[2603.15469|RoCo-Challenge]] reports **end-to-end beats modular for recovery**, the canonical anti-modular result.
- *Only-this-perspective insight*: Every prior "the hard part factors into a cheap separable module" wave (options/HRL, modular RL, DR) won the easy/loosely-coupled regime and lost the tightly-coupled one — and the card's own Risk #1 admits this ("composition may cap the coordination ceiling"). History says the publishable, durable contribution is **the crossover curve** (coupling-tightness at which composition stops paying), not the claim that the coupling is cheap. Map where the factorization *breaks*, which is the one thing the monolith camp cannot characterize.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *Skeptic vs Academic on H3 novelty*: Academic says H3's benchmark substrate is newly available and the composed-prior application is open; Skeptic says [[2606.20285|Co-VLA]]'s regime-routed task-adaptive losses (L_sparse/L_shared/L_sync, ablated per coordination type) already *is* "typed coupling beats single layer" — the claim is true-but-shown.
   - *Skeptic vs card on H4*: card predicts dangerous degradation is *late* and *coupling*-localized; Skeptic cites [[2606.11901|DuoBench]]'s empirical finding that most failures cluster at the *earliest grasp-acquisition* stage (a single-arm skill failure) — opposite stage, opposite localization.
   - *Practitioner vs Academic/card on the data-floor metric*: Practitioner says the binding cost is synchronized dual-operator teleop ([[2606.16490|SAI]], [[2605.29298|MonoDuo]]), not episode count; the card's H2 measures episodes and so may optimize the wrong axis.
   - *Historian vs Academic on factorization durability*: Historian says residual/coupling silently absorbs base error and [[2603.15469|RoCo]] shows end-to-end beats modular for recovery; Academic treats factorization as broadly confirmed.

2. **Strongest vs weakest.**
   - *Strongest: Skeptic.* It is the only perspective grounded in a same-month paper (Co-VLA) that directly instantiates the card's thesis sentence and runs an H3-shaped ablation, plus a benchmark (DuoBench) whose empirical failure stage contradicts H4. Maximally specific, maximally falsifying.
   - *Weakest: Economist.* Correct about incentives and crowding but offers no falsifiable claim about whether the coupling *is* structured/cheap — it reframes strategy rather than testing the bet. Useful for the COMMIT/DROP decision, not for truth.

3. **Question that resolves the biggest contradiction (H3/H4 novelty):** *On a composed-prior policy with frozen single-arm skills, does coordination-type-conditioned coupling beat one Joint-Attention layer by a margin larger than [[2606.20285|Co-VLA]]'s already-shown regime-loss gains — and does the late-stage SR drop localize to the coupling rather than to the early grasp stage [[2606.11901|DuoBench]] flags?* If both fail, the bet collapses to a re-run of Co-VLA on frozen priors.

4. **What every perspective agrees on (likely true):** Composition of strong single-arm priors with an explicit coupling matches or beats a matched-data monolith at a fraction of the data/compute (H1) — TwinVLA, Decoupled Bimanual, EnergyAction, SkillVLA, CUBic, Co-VLA all concur. The settled headline is genuinely settled.

5. **Blind spot no perspective fully addressed:** **Force/contact closed-loop coupling.** Every cited method couples through *kinematics/attention/latents/video*; none of H2–H5 touch the regime where the coupling is a *physical force loop* (tight handover, object squeeze) in which the "single-arm skill" is itself re-defined by partner-induced contact forces — the exact regime the card's Risk #1 fences off but never instruments. The cluster's own E1/C3 tactile lanes hint at it but C1 leaves contact-coupling unmeasured.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**(1) CEO summary.** C1's load-bearing headline (compose single-arm priors + thin coupling beats a matched-data monolith at a fraction of cost) is genuinely settled and the card honestly concedes it. The surviving novelty was placed in three sub-bets — H2 (coupling data-floor stays ~50 episodes on 4×-harder tasks), H3 (typed/directed coupling beats one attention layer), H4 (late degradation localizes to coupling not skill) — and a literature check shows the middle one has been substantially pre-empted: [[2606.20285|Co-VLA]] (Jun 2026, Samsung) already decomposes into shared-coordination + arm-residual latents, routes coordination losses *by regime* (symmetric/asymmetric/temporal), and ablates them, instantiating the card's exact thesis sentence. The defensible whitespace is narrower than the card claims but real: **nobody has run the frozen-prior data-floor sweep (H2) or localized failure to the coupling-vs-skill on a *composed*-prior policy (H4)** — and a benchmark ([[2606.11901|DuoBench]]) actually contradicts H4's "late-stage" premise by finding failures at the earliest grasp stage. The honest move is to DE-RISK into the H2+H4 corner with a metric-ownership play, not COMMIT to H3 as headline.

**(2) Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *Composition matches/beats matched-data monolith at fractional cost (H1).* **Highest reliability.** Supported by all five perspectives + TwinVLA/Decoupled/EnergyAction/SkillVLA/Co-VLA. Challenged by none (RoCo's "end-to-end beats modular for recovery" is a scoped caveat, not a refutation). Already settled = not the novelty.
2. *H3 (typed/directed coupling) is partially pre-empted.* **High reliability.** [[2606.20285|Co-VLA]]'s regime-routed losses (L_sync wins temporal 51%→62%, L_shared wins asymmetric 49%→70%) are an H3-shaped result; [[2604.20348|BiCICLe]] supplies the directed primitive. Supported by Skeptic+Academic; the card's framing (typed coupling "never tested") is now stale.
3. *H4's "late-stage coupling-localized" premise is empirically contested.* **Medium-high.** [[2606.11901|DuoBench]] finds most failures at earliest grasp-acquisition (single-arm skill), [[2606.16826|ATOM-Bench]] separates skill (AS 83.3%) from composition failure (15.8%) but on skill-chains not the coupling term. Supported by Skeptic; the *localization instrument* exists, the *late-stage* assumption may be wrong.
4. *H2 frozen-prior data-floor sweep is genuine whitespace.* **Medium.** No retrieved paper sweeps bimanual-episode count with frozen single-arm priors on a 4×-harder bench ([[2604.05831|BiCoord]]); TwinVLA reports one point (~50), BiCICLe/VLBiMan/ODIL anchor the zero/one-demo extreme. Supported by Academic+Practitioner; risk is it is an ablation, not a thesis, and 3+ groups can run it within a cycle (Economist).
5. *The binding cost is collection modality, not episode count.* **Medium, contrarian.** [[2606.16490|SAI]] and [[2605.29298|MonoDuo]] reframe the floor as synchronized-dual-operator teleop. Supported by Practitioner only; if true it is a *stronger* reframe of H2 than the card's.

**(3) Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Historian's "residual silently absorbs base error" and the Practitioner's "SAE-alone *decreased* Handover-ID until LAC was bolted on" are the same phenomenon seen from theory and bench: when the coupling is a residual/correction term over frozen priors, it absorbs base-policy error and deployment jitter, so the factorization is cleanest exactly where coordination is loosest — and degrades precisely in the tight-contact regime H4 wants to study. The coupling's "structure" and its "breakdown" are not separable questions; the breakdown *is* the residual eating base error.

**(4) Actionable insight for the researcher (DE-RISK, don't COMMIT as framed).** Drop H3 as a headline (Co-VLA pre-empted it) and **recenter the bet on H4+H2 with a metric-ownership play**: build the **coupling-data-floor-vs-coupling-tightness curve** on [[2604.05831|BiCoord]]/[[2606.11901|DuoBench]] using *frozen* single-arm priors, and use [[2606.16826|ATOM-Bench]]'s AS/CFS to localize failure to coupling-vs-skill *per coordination stage*. Concrete next experiment: freeze a π0/RDT-class single-arm prior, train only the coupling, sweep bimanual episodes {1, 5, 25, 50, 150} stratified by DuoBench's 4 coordination types, and report (a) where the floor rises (H2) and (b) whether late-stage SR drop is coupling-localized **or** early-grasp-localized as DuoBench predicts (H4 — and explicitly pre-register that DuoBench contradicts the late-stage assumption). If the floor stays flat AND failure localizes to coupling, COMMIT; if failure is early-grasp single-arm, pivot the paper to "the coupling is cheap but the *base skill* is the bottleneck" — still publishable, opposite headline. Differentiate from Co-VLA by the *frozen-prior composition* (Co-VLA fine-tunes the whole π0) and the *data-floor curve* (Co-VLA reports fixed 50-demo points).

**(5) Frontier question that would change everything.** Does the separability assumption survive *force-closed-loop* coupling — i.e. when partner-induced contact forces re-enter each arm's own controller, is there still a coupling term separable from the single-arm skill, or does tight contact dissolve the factorization entirely? If contact dissolves it, the entire compose-priors-plus-thin-coupling program (TwinVLA → Co-VLA → C1) is bounded to loosely-coupled tasks and the durable contribution is the *crossover curve* where it breaks, not the coupling's structure.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical robotics professor. Mandate: find the soft claims and the over-reach, not rubber-stamp. Verification done against the C1 card (Manipulation.md L572-658), the KH notes for the cited benchmarks, and a direct alphaxiv pull of Co-VLA (2606.20285) full text.*

**Headline verdict.** This is an unusually honest briefing — it concedes its own headline (H1) is settled and refuses to dress an ablation suite as a thesis. The central empirical claim that worried me (Co-VLA pre-empts H3) I independently verified against the paper's full text and it holds *better* than the briefing claims, not worse. The real weakness is not over-reach on the surviving claims; it is a **provenance gap** (the three linchpin papers are not in the vault) and a **slightly-too-clean pre-emption framing** that risks throwing out a still-live sub-question.

---

**1. CONFIDENCE SCORES (1-10 reliability)**

1. **Composition matches/beats matched-data monolith at fractional cost (H1) — 9/10.**
   Convergent across ≥6 independent coupling primitives (TwinVLA attention, Decoupled +23.5% at 1/6 size, EnergyAction, SkillVLA, CUBic, Co-VLA). The card itself files it as settled-background. The only docked point: every one of these is the *proponent's own* result; no adversarial third-party replication, and RoCo's "end-to-end beats modular for recovery" is a genuine (if scoped) counter-signal that the briefing waves off slightly too fast.

2. **H3 (typed/directed coupling) is substantially pre-empted by Co-VLA — 8/10.**
   I pulled Co-VLA's full text. The briefing's specifics are exact: shared+residual latents with additive composition, regime-routed losses L_sparse/L_shared/L_sync, ablated, with L_shared 49→70% (asymmetric) and L_sync 51→62% (temporal), and the verbatim "collaboration is not an action, it is a structure over actions." The pre-emption is real. Docked two points for an over-statement the briefing inherits from the analyst: Co-VLA routes losses by **manually-assigned task type** and explicitly lists "automatic task-level coordination descriptors for adaptive loss routing" as *future work*. So "typed coupling beats one layer" is shown; "**learned/conditioned** typing (DexMimicGen async/sync/ordered driving the coupling)" is *not* shown. H3 is wounded, not dead. The briefing should say "the supervised-routing form of H3 is pre-empted" rather than "drop H3."

3. **H4's late-stage coupling-localized premise is empirically contested — 7/10.**
   DuoBench's KH note confirms verbatim: "a significant fraction of failures occurring in the earliest stages, particularly initial grasp acquisition." That genuinely tensions the card's "later-stage degradation" framing (which it sources from BiCoord). But this is a *tension between two benchmarks*, not a refutation: BiCoord reports late-stage degradation on *long-horizon tightly-coupled* tasks; DuoBench reports early-grasp failure on *its* task mix with *current* (non-composed, non-frozen-prior) policies. The two are not measuring the same thing, and neither used the composed-frozen-prior setup H4 actually specifies. The briefing's "DuoBench contradicts H4" is fair as a pre-registration hazard, slightly strong as a factual claim.

4. **H2 frozen-prior data-floor sweep is genuine whitespace — 6/10.**
   I credit "no retrieved paper runs the frozen-prior episode sweep on a 4×-harder bench" — TwinVLA gives one point (~50), BiCICLe/VLBiMan/ODIL anchor the zero/one-demo end, none sweep. But the Economist caveat is the real ceiling: this is an *ablation runnable by 3+ groups in a cycle*, and Co-VLA already reports fixed 50-demo points on RoboTwin 2.0, so the gap is "the curve between points others have measured." Whitespace, yes; defensible *thesis-grade* whitespace, marginal. The score reflects that it is true-but-thin.

5. **Binding cost is collection modality, not episode count (SAI/MonoDuo) — 4/10.**
   The weakest-supported finding. It rests entirely on two papers (SAI 2606.16490, MonoDuo 2605.29298) that are **not in the vault KH and that I could not independently verify** in this pass, carried by a single perspective (Practitioner). The *idea* is sharp and plausible — synchronized dual-operator teleop is plausibly the true bottleneck — but as stated it is an unverified assertion about two unread papers. If true it reframes H2; that conditional is doing a lot of work on thin evidence.

*Mean = 6.8. Min = 4.*

---

**2. WEAKEST LINK**

Finding 5 (collection-modality reframe). Needed to verify: the actual claims of SAI (2606.16490) and MonoDuo (2605.29298) — specifically (a) do they *measure* that synchronized dual-operator teleop, not episode count, is the binding constraint (an ablation isolating operator-hours from episode-count), or merely *propose* single-operator collection? and (b) are they bimanual-coordination papers at all? Neither is in the vault; both should be pulled into KH and read before this reframe is allowed to restructure H2. Secondary weak link: the H4 "contradiction" needs one fact the briefing never pins down — does BiCoord's *late-stage* degradation and DuoBench's *early-grasp* failure even arise on overlapping task types, or is the apparent contradiction an artifact of different task distributions? Without that, "DuoBench contradicts H4" is an apples-to-oranges flag, not a refutation.

---

**3. BIAS CHECK**

**The Skeptic over-dominated.** The synthesis is openly anchored on it — the analyst's own meta-note calls Skeptic "the only perspective grounded in a same-month paper (Co-VLA)" and "Strongest," and every load-bearing move in the CEO summary, findings 2-3, and the actionable insight flows from the Skeptic's Co-VLA/DuoBench pair. That is defensible (Co-VLA genuinely is the most falsifying input and I verified it), but it tilts the briefing toward *de-risking/demolition* and away from *construction*. The Economist appears only as a one-line "runnable by 3+ groups" caveat; the Academic's "newly-available benchmark substrate makes the composed-prior application open" counter to the Skeptic is mentioned in the analyst tensions but does not survive into the final recommendation. The net effect: the briefing may be **under-crediting** the live residue of H3 (learned typing) because the dominant voice wanted a clean "Co-VLA ate it." Historian and Practitioner are well-used (the residual-eats-base-error connection is the best original synthesis in the doc). Not balanced; Skeptic-led.

---

**4. MISSING PERSPECTIVE**

**The Hardware/Systems-integration engineer (the person who actually runs the frozen-prior experiment).** Every perspective here reasons about *whether* the coupling has structure; none reasons about whether the proposed experiment is *executable as specified*. Three things this angle surfaces that change the conclusion: (i) "Freeze a π0/RDT-class single-arm prior and train only the coupling" assumes a clean single-arm→coupling interface, but Co-VLA's own Phase-1 freezes the backbone for *1000 steps* then unfreezes everything in Phase-2 — its authors evidently found frozen-prior insufficient, which is direct evidence *against* the feasibility of C1's frozen-only setup and would move H2 from "whitespace" to "whitespace others tried and abandoned." (ii) BiCoord, DuoBench, ATOM-Bench, PerAct2 run on *different robots/sims* (FR3-Duo, AgileX, Franka, ALOHA) — the proposed cross-benchmark floor-vs-tightness curve may not be commensurable across embodiments, an issue no perspective raises. (iii) The deployment-jitter story (LAC, low-pass filtering) means the "coupling data-floor" measured in sim episodes may not be the deployment-binding quantity at all — the systems engineer would predict the real floor is set by sim-to-real coupling stability, not episode count, which ironically *converges* with the Practitioner's modality reframe from the opposite direction. A sixth Systems voice would likely down-grade H2's feasibility and up-grade the frontier-question (force-closed-loop) into the actual thesis.

---

**5. OVERALL GRADE — A-**

This is top-decile research-strategy writing: it concedes its headline, verifies a same-month pre-emption against the primary source (which I confirmed), proposes a *pre-registered, two-sided* experiment that is publishable on either outcome, and ends on a genuinely load-bearing frontier question (does factorization survive force closure). The intellectual honesty is the strongest feature. It is held below A by three fixable defects.

**Top things to fix (in priority order):**
1. **Close the provenance gap.** The three papers carrying the two most aggressive claims (Co-VLA for H3-preemption, SAI+MonoDuo for the modality reframe) are not in the vault KH. Co-VLA I verified externally and it holds — so *ingest it into KH and cite it as an anchor/comparator row in the C1 table* (it is the single most direct prior art and the card currently does not list it). SAI/MonoDuo must be read before finding 5 is allowed to restructure H2; right now it is an assertion about unread papers.
2. **Don't kill H3 — bisect it.** Co-VLA pre-empts the *supervised, manually-routed* form of typed coupling. It explicitly defers *automatic/learned coordination-type descriptors* to future work, and never composes *frozen* single-arm priors. State the surviving slice precisely ("learned async/sync/ordered conditioning over frozen priors, vs Co-VLA's hand-routed losses over a fine-tuned π0") instead of "drop H3 as headline." The briefing's own differentiation paragraph already contains this distinction; the recommendation should inherit it.
3. **Make the H4 "contradiction" commensurable.** Establish whether BiCoord-late-stage and DuoBench-early-grasp arise on the same task class before pre-registering "DuoBench contradicts H4." Run the localization (ATOM-Bench AS/CFS) on *one* bench with the *composed-frozen-prior* policy the hypothesis actually specifies, rather than importing a cross-benchmark, cross-policy stage statistic as if it settled the question.

Minor: the briefing should flag that Co-VLA's two-phase training (frozen warm-up then full fine-tune) is itself weak evidence *against* the feasibility of C1's frozen-only composition — a point that cuts against H2 and is currently unmentioned.

---

### C2 (Direction review)

Topic: the FP bet that **coordination structure (per-arm subtask decomposition + sync/ordering), not the volume it unlocks, causally carries bimanual generalization** — operationalized as H1: stripping [[2410.24185|DexMimicGen]]'s sync/ordering constraints (raw SE(3) replay) at *matched* trajectory volume drops the 90%-from-40 result by ≥30 pp, cleanest on a differentiable generator ([[2604.03552|CRAFT]] / [[2505.04860|D-CODA]]).

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily).**
- *Core position*: The bet is a clean ablation question, but the thing that actually decides whether structured generation beats raw replay in the real world is not the taxonomy — it is whether the replayed SE(3) trajectories are *dynamically feasible* and whether the sim-to-real gap survives. DexMimicGen's 90%-from-40 is a real-to-sim-to-real number on a GR-1 humanoid (can-sorting); the moment you strip structure you also change *which* trajectories are physically executable, so "matched volume" is doing a lot of silent work.
- *Strongest evidence*: [[2502.20382|Physics-Driven-Data-Gen]] is the practitioner's smoking gun — a structure-*agnostic* generator that treats both arms as one kinematic chain and reaches 26%→74% real iiwa zero-shot from 24 demos with *no coordination primitives at all*. If a structure-free, volume-scaled generator already hits 74% real, the "structure carries it" story is under direct empirical threat on hardware. [[2605.21710|PGDG]] (sim 38%→93%, real 35%→82%) further shows physics-validity, not coordination taxonomy, is what moves real numbers on contact-coupled tasks.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: "Matched volume" is not a controlled variable on real robots — raw SE(3) replay yields a *different feasibility-filtered subset* than structured replay even at equal trajectory count, so H1's clean ablation is contaminated unless you gate both arms through the same feasibility filter ([[2604.07335|TAMEn]]). Run H1 in sim first or the result is unpublishable.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
- *Core position*: The card's framing — "no paper isolates structure from volume" — is *over-claimed*. The field already has the meta-result: [[2410.18647|Data-Scaling-Laws-in-Imitation-Learning]] (ICLR 2025) shows demonstration *count* follows no power-law and saturates, while environment/object *diversity* drives generalization. That is the structure-beats-volume thesis, already peer-reviewed, just not phrased as "coordination structure."
- *Strongest evidence*: 2410.18647 finds the optimality gap is power-law in diversity (env/object pairs) but flat in per-instance demo count — 32 env-object pairs × ~50 demos ≈ 90% zero-shot. [[2403.05110|Efficient-Data-Collection-via-Compositional-Generalization]] (DeepMind) is the older precedent that *structure of the data-collection distribution*, not raw quantity, sets generalization.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: The card is fighting a strawman ("achieved SR proves structure"). The real literature already rejects "more demos = more generalization"; what is genuinely unrun is the *bimanual-coordination-specific* version on a *differentiable* generator. Reframe the contribution as "coordination structure is the dimension along which the known diversity-beats-volume law operates in the two-arm coupling regime," or a reviewer will cite 2410.18647 as prior art.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks it is wrong or already solved).**
- *Core position*: H1's falsifier is more likely to fire than the card admits. [[2502.20382|Physics-Driven-Data-Gen]]'s structure-free generator at 74% real and [[2509.23829|DexFlyWheel]] beating DexMimicGen (81.9% vs 45.2% with 10× less human data) via a *volume/RL flywheel* both suggest the gain travels with the *augmentation distribution*, not the sync/ordering taxonomy. The "structure" may just be a sample-efficiency convenience that volume erases.
- *Strongest evidence*: DexMimicGen's own ablations are weak for the card's thesis — it reports only +8–9% coordinated-skill gains and a 16.2% hand-position-removal drop. An +8–9% structure effect is the *opposite* of a ≥30 pp prediction. The card cites this as "never a clean isolation," but it is also the closest existing data point, and it points to a *small* structure effect.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: If you run H1 and raw replay holds 90%, the paper has a null result — and the field's reflex (and 2410.18647) will say "of course, it was diversity." De-risk by pre-registering the ≥30 pp threshold as *the* claim and being willing to publish the null as "coordination structure is redundant once volume is matched" — that null is itself the contrarian contribution.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
- *Core position*: The entire X-Gen / data-generation wave is incentive-driven: bimanual teleop data is the single most expensive line item for any humanoid lab (Figure/1X/Unitree/Galaxea), so a "structure replaces volume" narrative directly lowers the dominant cost and is therefore over-published and over-claimed. Benchmarks ([[2506.18088|RoboTwin-2.0]], [[2604.05831|BiCoord]], [[2507.00435|RoboEval]]) exist precisely to absorb this funding flood.
- *Strongest evidence*: The card itself shows benchmark saturation pressure — RoboTwin 2.0's 71.3% auto-code SR, [[2510.18316|MoMaGen]] subsuming the whole X-Gen family one level up, π0 60% real after pretrain. Every entrant claims its generator is *the* unifier; that is citation-race behavior, not converged science.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: A clean structure-vs-volume isolation has *negative* commercial incentive — labs want "our generator works," not "the structure was redundant." That is exactly why the ablation is unrun and why an academic doing it has whitespace: the result nobody is paid to find. But it also means low industry uptake; pitch it as a *measurement-instrument* contribution ([[2507.00435|RoboEval]]'s Coordination/progression metrics) so it survives even a null.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the MimicGen → DexMimicGen → MoMaGen lineage replaying the classic "is it the inductive bias or the data?" cycle that ran through DAgger-vs-BC, pixel-vs-latent world models, and end-to-end-vs-modular. Every wave, a structured method wins early at low data, then a brute-force/volume method (here residual-RL flywheels, video diffusion) erases the gap as compute drops.
- *Strongest evidence*: [[2509.23829|DexFlyWheel]] (residual-RL flywheel beats the structured-replay anchor) and [[2604.03552|CRAFT]] (video-diffusion volume) are the bitter-lesson move already in progress — generative volume subsuming hand-built taxonomy, exactly as scaling subsumed feature engineering. [[2510.18316|MoMaGen]]'s constrained-optimization "subsumes MimicGen/SkillMimicGen/DexMimicGen" is the late-stage unification that historically precedes a paradigm being absorbed.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: Time-box this. The structure-vs-volume question has a ~12-month shelf life before a flywheel/diffusion generator at scale makes the coordination taxonomy a footnote (the DexFlyWheel trajectory is already there). Publish the isolation *now* while the structured anchor is still the SOTA baseline, or the contribution evaporates.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *Practitioner vs Card (H1 design)*: Practitioner says "matched volume" is not controllable on real robots because raw replay yields a different feasibility-subset; the card asserts a clean matched-volume ablation. Specific claim in tension: H1's "matched trajectory count" — equal count ≠ equal feasible-trajectory distribution.
   - *Academic vs Card (novelty)*: Card says "no paper isolates structure from volume"; Academic says 2410.18647 (ICLR 2025) + 2403.05110 already established diversity > demo-count as a power-law. Clash on whether the whitespace is conceptual (pre-empted) or only the bimanual-coordination instantiation (open).
   - *Skeptic vs Card (effect size)*: Card predicts ≥30 pp structure effect; Skeptic notes DexMimicGen's own evidence is +8–9% / 16.2%-drop — an order of magnitude smaller. Direct clash on the magnitude of the falsifiable prediction.
   - *Historian vs everyone (durability)*: Historian + DexFlyWheel evidence say volume (flywheel/diffusion) is *already* erasing the structure gap, implying H1's falsifier is the likely outcome; the card treats structure-carries as the live hypothesis.

2. **Strongest vs weakest.**
   - *Strongest*: ACADEMIC — its claim is grounded in a peer-reviewed ICLR result (2410.18647) that directly speaks to the bet's conceptual core, and it offers a constructive reframe rather than just refutation.
   - *Weakest*: ECONOMIST — directionally correct about incentives but supplies no metric that changes the experiment; it explains *why* the gap exists rather than whether the bet is true.

3. **Question that resolves the biggest contradiction.** On a *single* differentiable generator ([[2604.03552|CRAFT]] or [[2505.04860|D-CODA]]), with both arms routed through the *same* feasibility filter ([[2604.07335|TAMEn]]) so trajectory *feasibility* is held constant, does toggling only the coordination-structure term (sync/ordering vs none) at matched feasible-volume change downstream Coordination-class SR ([[2507.00435|RoboEval]]) by ≥30 pp? — this simultaneously settles the Practitioner's "matched volume isn't controllable" objection and the Skeptic's "effect is only +8–9%" objection.

4. **What every perspective agrees on (likely true).** Structured generation *does* beat raw quantity at *low* data budgets and is the right move when teleop demos are scarce — every perspective concedes the few-shot win ([[2410.24185|DexMimicGen]] 90%-from-40-vs-0%-from-4, [[2502.20382|Physics-Driven]] 26%→74%). The disagreement is purely about *why* (structure vs the feasible-volume/diversity it unlocks) and whether the advantage survives scale.

5. **Blind spot (none addressed).** No perspective addressed **the asymmetry between bimanual coupling regimes**: tightly-coupled tasks (handovers, force-balanced lifts where the two arms form a closed kinematic chain) vs loosely-coupled parallel tasks. The structure-vs-volume answer almost certainly differs across this split — volume may erase structure on parallel subtasks while structure remains irreducible on closed-chain coupling — yet H1 tests an aggregate SR that would average the two regimes and could report a misleading null. ([[2604.05831|BiCoord]]'s later-stage degradation is the closest probe but the card uses it only in H4.)

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**CEO summary.** C2's contrarian core — a clean *structure-vs-volume* isolation on bimanual data generation — is a genuine and currently-unrun experiment, but the card overstates its novelty and likely overstates its effect size. The conceptual claim "structure/diversity beats raw demonstration count" is already a peer-reviewed result ([[2410.18647|Data-Scaling-Laws]], ICLR 2025; [[2403.05110|Compositional-Generalization]]), and a structure-*free* generator ([[2502.20382|Physics-Driven-Data-Gen]]) already reaches 74% real with no coordination primitives, so the ≥30 pp prediction is at material risk of a null. The salvageable, defensible contribution is narrower and sharper than the card's headline: isolate coordination structure *specifically* on a *differentiable* bimanual generator, hold *feasibility* (not just count) constant, score with a coordination-sensitive metric, and split tightly- vs loosely-coupled tasks — and be willing to publish the null, because "coordination taxonomy is redundant once feasible-volume is matched" is itself a publishable contrarian result with a short shelf life.

**5 key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *The few-shot win of structured generation is real and uncontested* (DexMimicGen 90%-from-40-vs-0%; Physics-Driven 26%→74%). Supported by: all five perspectives. Challenged by: none — but it is necessary, not sufficient, for the causal claim.
2. *The conceptual "structure/diversity > volume" thesis is already peer-reviewed, so the card's "no paper isolates this" is over-claimed.* Supported by: Academic (2410.18647, 2403.05110), Historian. Challenged by: the card's framing — but the bimanual-coordination-on-a-differentiable-generator instantiation does remain unrun, so it is *partial* pre-emption.
3. *The ≥30 pp structure effect is likely too large; existing evidence points to a small effect.* Supported by: Skeptic (DexMimicGen +8–9% / 16.2%), Historian (DexFlyWheel volume erasing the gap). Challenged by: card's CRAFT-Canny 21.6%-vs-10.3% hint (but that is a visual-conditioning ablation, not a coordination ablation).
4. *"Matched volume" is not a clean control without holding feasibility constant.* Supported by: Practitioner. Challenged by: none — this is a design fix the card must adopt (route both arms through [[2604.07335|TAMEn]] and run in sim first).
5. *The window is ~12 months before flywheel/diffusion volume makes the taxonomy a footnote.* Supported by: Historian, Economist (DexFlyWheel, CRAFT, MoMaGen unification). Challenged by: none, but it is a forecast, not a measurement — lowest reliability.

**Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Practitioner's "matched volume isn't controllable because feasibility differs" and the Academic's "it was diversity all along" are the *same mechanism* viewed from two sides: structured replay does not add abstract "coordination knowledge," it *implicitly filters the trajectory distribution toward feasible, diverse, coordination-valid samples*. Strip the structure and you do not just lose a taxonomy — you change which samples survive, which is exactly the *diversity* axis 2410.18647 says carries generalization. So the structure-vs-volume dichotomy may be false: structure *is* a diversity/feasibility-shaping prior, and the real experiment is structure-vs-(volume-AND-feasibility-matched), which is why the H1 control must hold feasibility fixed.

**Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK, do not commit as framed.**
- *Reframe* the thesis from "no one has isolated structure vs volume" (refutable via 2410.18647) to "coordination structure is the *specific diversity dimension* that the known diversity-beats-volume law operates along in the closed-kinematic-chain bimanual regime — and we measure where volume can and cannot substitute for it."
- *Fix the control*: run H1 in sim on one differentiable generator ([[2505.04860|D-CODA]] or [[2604.03552|CRAFT]]), routing both the structured and raw-replay arms through identical [[2604.07335|TAMEn]] feasibility filtering, so the only varied term is the sync/ordering structure. Score with [[2507.00435|RoboEval]]'s Coordination + stage-progression classes, not binary SR.
- *Concrete next experiment*: on D-CODA's Coordinated-Lift, generate N matched-feasible trajectories two ways — (a) full sync/ordering taxonomy, (b) raw SE(3) replay — and report the SR delta **separately for the tightly-coupled (handover/closed-chain) and loosely-coupled (parallel) task subsets**. Pre-register ≥15 pp on the coupled subset as the success bar (not ≥30 pp aggregate). A null on the coupled subset is the publishable contrarian result; a positive is the structure-carries proof.
- *Lower the threshold and own the null*: drop the ≥30 pp aggregate prediction (existing evidence makes it improbable) and commit to publishing either outcome.

**Frontier question.** Is "coordination structure" a *separable causal factor* at all, or is it merely a low-data sample-efficiency prior that any sufficiently diverse, feasibility-validated volume reconstructs — i.e. does there exist a coupling regime (closed kinematic chain, force-balanced contact) where *no amount* of feasibility-matched volume substitutes for explicit sync/ordering, or does volume always win in the limit? Answering that decides whether structured bimanual generation is a lasting design principle or a transitional scaffold.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics faculty. Card under review: `Capability/Manipulation.md` C2 (lines 659-737), "Scalable Bimanual Data Generation with Coordination Structure." Briefing under review: the Prompt-3 CEO synthesis. Two anchor claims independently verified below.*

**What I verified before grading.** I confirmed the two papers the synthesis leans on hardest are real and correctly characterized: 2410.18647 (Data Scaling Laws in Imitation Learning, ICLR 2025) is an empirical study whose generalization axes are *environment* and *object* diversity, not coordination structure; 2502.20382 (Physics-Driven Data Generation via Trajectory Optimization, the "PhysicsGen" line) is a contact-rich trajectory-optimization generator that treats both arms as one combined kinematic chain and deploys bimanual iiwa zero-shot. Both are exactly as the briefing describes, and both are already in the C2 table (2502.20382 is row "Physics-Driven Data Gen"). I also confirmed CRAFT (2604.03552) is literally "Canny-guided Robot Data Generation": its toggleable term is *edge/visual* conditioning, not coordination sync/ordering. That last point matters for the grade and the briefing under-flags it.

---

**1. CONFIDENCE SCORES (1-10 reliability)**

| # | Finding | Score | One-line justification |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Few-shot win of structured generation is real and uncontested | **9** | Multiple independent results (DexMimicGen 90%-from-40, PhysicsGen 26%->74%); the briefing correctly tags it as necessary-not-sufficient. Only docked because "uncontested" elides that PhysicsGen's win is itself structure-FREE, which the briefing actually uses against the card. |
| 2 | "Structure/diversity > volume" is already peer-reviewed, so "no paper isolates this" is over-claimed | **7** | 2410.18647 and 2403.05110 verified real and on-point, so the card's blanket novelty claim IS over-broad. But the pre-emption is genuinely PARTIAL: 2410.18647 isolates env/object diversity, never *coordination* structure on a bimanual generator. The briefing's own "PARTIAL pre-emption" hedge is the correct and honest read; I'd not score higher because the bridge "structure = the diversity dimension" (the Hidden Connection) is asserted, not shown. |
| 3 | The >=30 pp structure effect is likely too large; evidence points to a small effect | **7** | Directionally well-supported: DexMimicGen's own coordinated-skill deltas are single-digit-to-16%, and those are feature/skill ablations, not the structure-vs-volume ablation H1 proposes, so they bound the *plausible* effect only loosely. The CRAFT 21.6-vs-10.3 "hint" is verified to be a Canny/visual-conditioning ablation, NOT coordination, so it cannot support 30 pp either way. The "likely too large" claim is a reasonable prior, but it rests on extrapolating from non-matching ablations, so it is judgment, not measurement. |
| 4 | "Matched volume" isn't a clean control without holding feasibility constant | **8** | This is the strongest *methodological* finding and the most defensible thing in the briefing. It is a logically sound confound (raw SE(3) replay produces more infeasible samples, so volume and feasibility co-vary), and the TAMEn-in-the-loop fix is concrete and correct. Docked only because routing raw replay through TAMEn may filter so aggressively that "raw replay" stops being raw, partially collapsing the very contrast H1 wants. |
| 5 | ~12-month window before flywheel/diffusion volume makes the taxonomy a footnote | **4** | Explicitly a forecast, not a measurement, and the briefing says so. The "12 months" number has no derivation. DexFlyWheel already beats DexMimicGen with 10x less human data using volume+residual-RL, which is *evidence the erosion is underway*, but the specific horizon is unfalsifiable as stated. Lowest reliability, correctly self-flagged. |

Mean = 7.0. Min = 4.

---

**2. WEAKEST LINK**

Finding 5 (the 12-month window) is the least-confident *claim*, but it is honestly flagged as a forecast, so it is low-stakes. The weakest *load-bearing* link, the one that actually decides whether C2 should be run, is the **Hidden Connection's identity claim**: "structure IS a diversity/feasibility-shaping prior," i.e. that 2410.18647's diversity axis and coordination structure are the same mechanism. The entire reframing in the Actionable Insight rests on this equivalence, and it is currently asserted by analogy, not demonstrated.

What would verify it: a measurement showing that stripping coordination structure (raw replay) and matching volume actually *reduces trajectory-distribution diversity along the env/object axes 2410.18647 scores* — e.g., compute coverage/entropy of object-pose and contact-configuration distributions for structured vs raw-replay sets at matched count, and show the structured set is more diverse on precisely those axes. If structured and raw-replay sets are equally diverse on the 2410.18647 axes yet differ in SR, the "structure = diversity" identity is false and structure is a separable factor after all (which is the Frontier Question). Until that distributional measurement exists, the briefing's central reframing is a plausible story, not a verified mechanism.

Secondary weak link: the briefing inherits the card's framing of CRAFT/D-CODA as the place where "the structure term can be toggled continuously." Verified: CRAFT's continuous knob is Canny/visual conditioning, not coordination. Neither CRAFT nor D-CODA exposes a sync/ordering dial. So the "cleanest on a differentiable generator" premise (in both the card AND the briefing's next-experiment) needs the team to ADD a coordination toggle that does not currently exist in those generators, which is a non-trivial methods contribution the briefing treats as free.

---

**3. BIAS CHECK — perspective dominance**

The synthesis is **Academic-dominated, with the Practitioner as a strong second**. The spine of every conclusion is the literature-pre-emption argument (Academic: 2410.18647 / 2403.05110) fused with the feasibility-confound argument (Practitioner: TAMEn). The Historian supplies the window forecast and DexFlyWheel, but mostly as corroboration of the Academic line rather than an independent vector. The Economist appears once (window) and is essentially folded into the Historian. The Skeptic is present (effect-size) but its contribution is also routed through Academic evidence (DexMimicGen's own deltas).

The tell is the Hidden Connection: it explicitly *collapses* the Practitioner and Academic into "the same mechanism." That is intellectually elegant but it means two of the five perspectives are no longer independent, which inflates the apparent agreement behind findings 2 and 4. A genuinely balanced synthesis would have kept the feasibility-confound (an experimental-design issue) separable from the diversity-law pre-emption (a novelty issue), because they fail differently: you can fix the confound with TAMEn and still have a novel result, OR concede the novelty and still have a clean confound-controlled experiment. Merging them obscures that you might lose one battle and win the other.

---

**4. MISSING PERSPECTIVE — a 6th angle that would change conclusions**

The **Hardware/Embodiment Realist** (the person who has to deploy the generated data on a real bimanual platform). The entire briefing argues structure-vs-volume in the abstract and proposes settling it *in sim on one differentiable generator*. But the load-bearing empirical wins it cites are real-robot numbers (DexMimicGen 90% real, PhysicsGen 74% real iiwa, DexFlyWheel 78.3% real Lift), and the C2 risk box itself names a "Sim-to-Real Cliff" (RoCo Challenge). A sim-only H1 can produce a clean structure-vs-volume answer that *does not survive contact*: kinematic replay's infeasible-contact artifacts (the card's own H5 / PGDG point) may be invisible in sim and dominant on hardware. This angle changes the conclusion in two ways: (a) the publishable null becomes much weaker, because "structure is redundant *in sim at matched feasible volume*" is a far smaller claim than the contrarian headline implies, and a reviewer will ask whether the null holds on real contact-rich coupled tasks; (b) it argues the coupled-subset success bar should be measured on a real holding-while-manipulating task (or at minimum a high-fidelity contact sim), not D-CODA's Coordinated-Lift, which is comparatively loosely coupled. The Realist would also note that "tightly-coupled (handover/closed-chain)" tasks are exactly where sim contact models are least trustworthy, so the briefing's most-decisive subset is its least sim-faithful one.

---

**5. OVERALL GRADE — B+**

This is a strong, honest de-risking review that correctly catches the card's two real sins: an over-broad novelty claim ("no paper isolates this") that 2410.18647 partially pre-empts, and an effect-size prediction (>=30 pp) inflated past what any cited ablation supports. The recommendation to publish the null is exactly the right researcher's instinct, and the feasibility-confound catch is genuinely valuable. It lands at B+ rather than A- because (i) its central reframing rests on an unverified mechanistic identity (structure = diversity) that it presents as settled, (ii) it inherits without challenge the card's false premise that CRAFT/D-CODA already expose a coordination knob, and (iii) by merging the Academic and Practitioner perspectives it overstates internal agreement and hides that the novelty loss and the design fix are independent outcomes.

**Top things to fix (in priority order):**
1. **Demote the "structure = diversity" identity from premise to hypothesis.** Add the distributional-diversity measurement (coverage/entropy on 2410.18647's env/object + contact axes for structured vs raw-replay at matched count) as the FIRST experiment. It is cheaper than a full policy-training sweep and it directly decides the Frontier Question. If you cannot show the structured set is more diverse on those axes, the whole reframing is wrong.
2. **Stop calling CRAFT/D-CODA continuous coordination dials.** They expose visual/edge and viewpoint knobs, not sync/ordering. Either build the coordination toggle explicitly (and claim it as a methods contribution) or run H1 on DexMimicGen's actual taxonomy where the structure term genuinely exists, and use the differentiable generators only for the volume arm.
3. **Add a real-robot (or high-fidelity contact-sim) coupled task to the success bar.** A sim-only null on a loosely-coupled lift will not convince a reviewer that coordination structure is redundant in the closed-kinematic-chain regime the Frontier Question is actually about.
4. **Keep the novelty fix and the confound fix separate in the writeup.** Report (a) "we concede the diversity-law pre-emption and contribute the coordination-specific instantiation" and (b) "we contribute the first feasibility-matched structure-vs-volume control" as two distinct claims, so a partial loss does not sink both.
5. **Drop the 12-month horizon or derive it.** As written it is an unfalsifiable number; either tie it to a measured volume-erosion rate (DexFlyWheel-style gap-closing per data-doubling) or cut it to "the window is closing" without the false precision.
