### D1 (Direction review)

Topic: **D1 — Universal Cross-Morphology Hand Control.** The bet (verbatim from First-principles): *"One backbone unifies the full reach→grasp→reorient→place cycle across distinct hands (which GET cannot — it is in-hand rotation across configs of one hand; DexWM spans the cycle but on one hand family), and at the reorientation phase explicit-intent (FAAS), implicit-history (DexFormer), and graph-structure (GET) parameterizations differ measurably — best matching DexWM's 72%/58%/28% zero-shot and 83% real grasp, 5.2× cheaper than per-hand collection, at real-time latency. Falsifiable: if a joint-space/graph policy (GET-class) with equal data matches the cycle transfer on an unseen distinct hand, intent is not the invariant; if the three parameterizations tie at reorientation, the choice does not matter."*

Novelty check (alphaxiv `discover_papers` WORKED, two rounds + `get_paper_content` on LUCID 2606.11628). The "decouple hand-agnostic intent from hand-specific control" premise is being independently confirmed by very recent work the card does not cite — most threateningly **LUCID (2606.11628**, Jun 2026), which operationalizes that exact split and transfers one intent model across a dexterous hand AND a parallel-jaw gripper. But LUCID uses a *separate per-embodiment RL control policy* and is evaluated on stir/wipe/bin/push-T/cable-routing, NOT a single backbone spanning reach→grasp→reorient→place across *distinct multi-fingered hands*, and NOT the reorientation parameterization head-to-head. Verdict: **partially-pre-empted** — the principle is now consensus (the card concedes this), and the framing is no longer differentiating; the specific falsifiable bet (which parameterization carries the *full cycle*, head-to-head at *reorientation*, on distinct hands) survives as whitespace. Also surfaced uncited: DIAL 2603.29844 (intent/action decoupling via latent WM), LAP 2602.10556, DexGrasp-Zero 2603.16806, MachaGrasp 2510.06068 (all grasp-or-VLA, not full-cycle).

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys daily).**
- *Core position*: The headline numbers are sim or single-real-hand grasp; the bet's deliverable (full-cycle on a *held-out distinct* hand at real-time) has no existence proof, and the place phase is where everything dies. DexWM's own cycle is 72/58/**28**% reach/grasp/**place** — the place number is barely above its DP baseline's 8% and below deployable, and it runs MPC over a world model, which is not real-time on real hardware.
- *Strongest evidence*: DexWM's 83% real is **grasp-only on one Allegro**; UniDex's cross-hand transfer is **60%/40%** (Oymotion/Wuji), which the Risks row itself flags as "not deployment-ready"; AnyBody shows multi-embodiment policies **collapse to 0%** on extrapolation — i.e. exactly the "distinct hand" regime the bet needs. Canonical Hand's 71% real is also grasp-only.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: The MPC-over-world-model anchor (DexWM) and the "real-time latency" clause are in tension — you cannot run DexWM-style planning AND claim DexFuture's 60 Hz unless you switch backbones mid-bet. Pick the future-state controller (DexFuture, H4) as the actual deployable substrate *first*, or the latency clause is unfundable. And budget for the place-phase regrasp/reset failures (OmniReset exists for a reason).

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the literature).**
- *Core position*: The card is honest that intent-transfer is "now consensus" (DexFormer states it verbatim; GET and UniDex both transfer), so the contribution must be the *parameterization head-to-head at reorientation* plus *full-cycle on distinct hands* — and that controlled ablation genuinely has not been run. But the literature is denser than the card's snapshot: XL-VLA, UniHM, One-Policy-Fits-All, Latent Action Diffusion, House of Dextra, Cross-Embodiment World Models, and now LUCID/DIAL all occupy the codec spectrum.
- *Strongest evidence*: The card's own table already enumerates ≥7 distinct cross-hand parameterizations (FAAS, VAE-latent, VQ-VAE, contrastive-latent, graph-joint, geometry-latent, history-conditioning) — this density is evidence the *interface* question is crowded, not empty. The surviving wedge is narrow: nobody runs explicit-intent vs implicit-history vs graph-structure *at matched data on the reorientation phase*.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: Your real novel artifact is a **benchmark+protocol** (AnyBody-style morphology-distance splits extended to multi-fingered hands across the full cycle), not a new policy. The field has the policies; it lacks the controlled comparison. Frame the paper as "the head-to-head nobody ran," with the protocol as the citable contribution.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks it's wrong or solved).**
- *Core position*: The "intent is the invariant" thesis is being made true-by-redefinition and simultaneously pre-empted: LUCID already decouples embodiment-agnostic intent from embodiment-specific control and transfers across hand↔gripper, and DexFormer already shows you don't even *need* explicit intent. If implicit history (DexFormer 77.5%) and graph-joint (GET) both transfer, "which parameterization" may be a benchmark-overfitting question, not a science question.
- *Strongest evidence*: AnyBody's verdict is that *all* current multi-embodiment methods hit 0% on extrapolation regardless of parameterization — so the head-to-head may resolve to "all three tie near the floor on a truly distinct hand," which is the card's *own* second falsifier ("if the three tie at reorientation, the choice does not matter"). The bet is structured so the most likely empirical outcome (tie at the hard regime) is a null result.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: Run the cheap killer experiment first — DexFormer-class implicit-history vs GET-class graph on ONE held-out distinct hand at the reorientation phase. If they tie (likely per AnyBody), the headline bet is dead and you've spent two weeks, not a year. Pre-register that the interesting result is a *separation*, and treat a tie as the kill signal.

**4. ECONOMIST (money/incentives).**
- *Core position*: The "5.2× cheaper than per-hand collection" framing is the load-bearing economic claim, and it is borrowed from UniDex's data-pipeline number, not from a cross-hand *control* result — the incentive is to sell a data-efficiency story to a field drowning in per-hand teleop cost. The hand-vendor fragmentation (LEAP/Allegro/Oymotion/Wuji/Inspire) is precisely what makes "one backbone, any hand" a fundable narrative and a saturating benchmark race.
- *Strongest evidence*: Every anchor reports a cost/compute multiplier (UniDex 5.2×, Multi-Sensory Sparse Experts 42.6% compute cut, DexFuture ~250×, DexUMI 3.2×) — the field is optimizing *cost ratios*, a classic late-stage benchmark-chasing signal. The proliferation of near-identical canonical/latent codecs (Feb–Jun 2026: Canonical Hand, XL-VLA, UniHM, One-Policy-Fits-All, House of Dextra) is a citation land-rush on the same idea.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: The whitespace is closing fast because the topic is *cheap to publish on* (sim + retargeting + a latent), so the half-life of this direction is short — months, not years. Either commit immediately with the protocol moat (hard to replicate, slow to saturate) or the cost-ratio framing will be commoditized by the next three arXiv drops.

**5. HISTORIAN (prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the universal-action-space / morphology-generalization wave replaying the modular-vs-end-to-end and pixel-vs-latent world-model cycles. "One representation to rule them all" (Canonical Hand's literal title) has recurred — OXE/cross-embodiment-co-training, GNN-morphology-policies (NerveNet, AnyMorph), Dreamer-lineage latent WMs (DexWM is DINOv2+diffusion MPC, i.e. a dexterous Dreamer) — and each wave found the same wall: transfer works on interpolation, collapses on extrapolation.
- *Strongest evidence*: AnyBody's interpolation-works / extrapolation-0% result is the *exact same shape* as the morphology-RL literature's findings circa 2019–2021 (graph policies generalized within a family, not across families). DexWM-as-Dreamer and the future-state/action-conditioned split (DexFuture vs DexWM) recapitulate the pixel-vs-latent and value-vs-model debates.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: The bet's likely fate is foretold by history — the parameterization that "wins" the head-to-head will win on the *interpolation* regime and the whole field will quietly relabel "distinct hand" to mean "interpolated hand." Guard against this: the contribution must be defined on the *extrapolation/composition* splits, or it joins the graveyard of universal-action-space papers that only transferred within-family.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *Academic vs Skeptic on whitespace*: Academic says the reorientation head-to-head "genuinely has not been run" (live whitespace); Skeptic says AnyBody's universal 0%-on-extrapolation makes the most likely outcome a *tie at the floor*, i.e. the card's own second falsifier fires and the bet is a structured null. Same fact (no head-to-head exists), opposite value (untapped contribution vs predetermined null).
   - *Practitioner vs the card on the anchor*: Practitioner says DexWM (MPC-over-WM) cannot satisfy the "real-time latency" clause and the place phase (28%) is undeployable; the card leans on DexWM as the cycle existence-proof *and* invokes DexFuture's 60 Hz — internally inconsistent backbones.
   - *Economist vs Historian on timing*: Economist says commit *now* before commoditization; Historian says the thing you'd commit to (universal action space) has reliably failed at extrapolation across three prior waves, so speed into a known wall is the wrong lesson.

2. **Strongest vs weakest perspective.** Strongest: **Skeptic** — it is anchored in the card's *own* falsifier and AnyBody's *own* 0%-extrapolation number, so its prediction (tie at the hard regime) is the cheapest to test and most decision-relevant. Weakest: **Economist** — directionally right about commoditization but its core "5.2×" lever is borrowed from a *data-pipeline* result (UniDex), not from any cross-hand *control* economics, so the quantitative claim is the softest.

3. **Resolving question.** *On a single truly-distinct held-out hand (AnyBody extrapolation/composition split, not interpolation), scored on the full reach→grasp→reorient→place cycle at matched data, do explicit-intent, implicit-history, and graph-structure parameterizations separate — or do they tie (near the floor)?* This single experiment resolves the Academic-vs-Skeptic clash and triggers the card's own falsifier either way.

4. **Universal agreement (likely true).** Every perspective accepts that **hand-agnostic intent-level transfer is real but breaks at morphological extrapolation** — interpolation works (DexFormer 77.5%, Canonical Hand 71%), extrapolation collapses (AnyBody 0%), and 40–60% cross-hand transfer is not deployment-ready (the card's own Risks row). The unsolved part is *the hard regime*, and all five agree the contribution must live there.

5. **Blind spot (none addressed).** **Object/task generalization is entangled with hand generalization and nobody isolated it.** Every anchor varies the hand while holding a narrow task/object set; none ask whether a "cross-hand" win is actually cross-*object* or cross-*task* leakage. The protocol the card proposes controls morphology distance but not object/task distance — so a measured parameterization separation could be an object-distribution artifact, and no perspective (nor the card) factors that confound out.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

1. **CEO summary.** D1 bets that a single backbone can carry the *full* dexterous control cycle across *distinct* hands and that the choice of cross-hand parameterization (explicit-intent vs implicit-history vs graph) matters most at the reorientation phase. The underlying principle ("intent is hand-agnostic, actuation is hand-specific") is no longer novel — the card concedes it is consensus, and June-2026 work (LUCID, DIAL) now operationalizes it directly — so the *only* surviving contribution is the controlled head-to-head and the full-cycle-on-distinct-hands result, neither of which exists today. The bet is real but narrow, perishable (the codec land-rush is fast), and structurally fragile: its own second falsifier (a tie at reorientation) is the *most likely* outcome given AnyBody's universal 0%-on-extrapolation finding. Commit only if reframed as a protocol/benchmark contribution defined on the *extrapolation* regime, with a two-week kill-experiment run first.

2. **Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
   - (i) *Intent-transfer works on interpolation, collapses on extrapolation* — **highest reliability**. Supported by Practitioner, Academic, Skeptic, Historian (AnyBody 0%, DexFormer/Canonical Hand interpolation wins). Challenged by none; this is the field's bedrock.
   - (ii) *The reorientation parameterization head-to-head has not been run* — **high**. Supported by Academic (live whitespace), conceded by Skeptic (but reframed as likely-null). Challenged by Economist (closing fast). This is the surviving novelty.
   - (iii) *The bet is partially pre-empted on framing* — **high**. Supported by Skeptic and the alphaxiv check (LUCID 2606.11628 decouples agnostic-intent/specific-control and transfers hand↔gripper; DIAL 2603.29844 does intent/action latent decoupling). Challenged mildly by Academic (LUCID is hand↔gripper with per-embodiment RL, not full-cycle on distinct multi-fingered hands).
   - (iv) *DexWM-as-cycle-anchor conflicts with the real-time clause* — **medium-high**. Supported by Practitioner (MPC-over-WM ≠ real-time; place=28%). Challenged by the card's H4 (DexFuture 60 Hz) — but that is a *different* backbone, so the conflict stands until resolved.
   - (v) *The whitespace is perishable (months)* — **medium**. Supported by Economist, Historian (cost-ratio chasing, codec land-rush Feb–Jun 2026). Challenged by Academic (a protocol moat is slow to saturate even if policies aren't).

3. **Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Skeptic's "likely tie at the floor" and the Historian's "wins only on interpolation, field relabels distinct→interpolated" are the **same failure mode seen from two ends**: the bet can fail *either* by all parameterizations tying at the hard regime (null) *or* by appearing to separate only because "distinct hand" silently degraded to "interpolated hand." Both collapse onto the Blind Spot — without isolating morphology-distance (and object/task distance) on extrapolation splits, a "result" is indistinguishable from an artifact. The entire bet's validity hinges on one un-discussed axis: rigorous *distance control*.

4. **Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK, do not yet COMMIT.** The principle is pre-empted and the headline outcome is probably null, but a real protocol-shaped contribution survives. Concretely: **(a)** Run the kill experiment first — DexFormer-class implicit-history vs GET-class graph-joint (the two cheapest, both have code-class baselines) on **one** AnyBody *extrapolation*-split held-out distinct hand, scored on the full reach→grasp→reorient→place cycle at matched data; pre-register that a tie kills the headline. **(b)** If they separate, *then* commit, and ship the AnyBody-extended multi-fingered full-cycle morphology-distance benchmark as the citable artifact (Academic's moat), explicitly factoring out object/task distance (the Blind Spot). **(c)** Resolve the backbone conflict by adopting DexFuture future-state (60 Hz) as the deployable substrate from day one, not DexWM-MPC. Drop the "5.2× cheaper" headline unless you can re-derive it from cross-hand *control*, not UniDex's data pipeline.

5. **Frontier question.** *Is "which contacts to form" genuinely a hand-agnostic plan, or does the right contact plan itself change with morphology — i.e. is there any invariant at all on the extrapolation/composition regime, or does every parameterization collapse because the optimal intent is morphology-dependent?* If intent is not invariant under extrapolation, the entire cluster-D premise (and LUCID's, and UniDex's) rests on interpolation, and the field needs a *morphology-conditioned* intent, not a morphology-agnostic one.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics faculty. Grounding: D1 card (Manipulation.md L825-904), plus KH spot-checks of LUCID 2606.11628, DIAL 2603.29844, AnyBody 2505.14986, DexWM 2512.13644, UniDex 2603.22264.*

##### 1. Confidence scores (5 key findings, 1-10)

1. **(i) Intent-transfer works on interpolation, collapses on extrapolation — score 6/10.** The *direction* is real, but the briefing inflates the evidence. AnyBody's KH note shows "0% for `Arms-push` in extrapolation" — one task instance, not the "universal 0%-on-extrapolation finding" the CEO summary asserts. The actual finding is "low success rates" on extrapolation/composition. More important: AnyBody is reach+push on **arms/grippers**, not the in-hand cycle on **multi-fingered hands** (the card concedes this at L866). So the load-bearing falsifier is an *analogy* transported across a morphology class, not a measured result in D1's own regime. "Highest reliability" overstates a cross-domain extrapolation of a single 0% datapoint.

2. **(ii) The reorientation parameterization head-to-head has not been run — score 8/10.** This survives scrutiny: I find no system that runs explicit-intent vs implicit-history vs graph-joint at matched data on a held-out distinct hand scored on the full cycle. The whitespace is genuine. Docked because "reorientation phase" is asserted as the locus where parameterization matters most without any evidence it matters *there* specifically rather than at grasp/place — that localization is itself unrun and unargued.

3. **(iii) The bet is partially pre-empted on framing — score 4/10.** Over-stated, and the spot-check contradicts the briefing's "HIGH." **LUCID** transfers a hand-agnostic intent model across a dexterous hand AND a *parallel-jaw gripper* (19/30 each, push-T/cable-routing) — exactly the Academic's challenge, which is correct and arguably *fatal* to the pre-emption claim, not "mild": it is hand↔gripper on non-cycle tasks, not multi-fingered↔multi-fingered on the full cycle. **DIAL** is worse evidence: it is a single-embodiment VLA (RoboCasa GR1 tabletop) decoupling *VLM-intent / policy-action* — a different axis (cognition/motor) than D1's hand-agnostic-intent / hand-specific-actuation. Citing DIAL as cross-hand framing pre-emption is a category error. Neither paper is even cited in the D1 card; they appear only in the analyst part. The principle is consensus (the card already concedes that, citing DexFormer verbatim), but "operationalizes it *directly*" for D1's cross-hand-control framing is not what these two papers do.

4. **(iv) DexWM-as-cycle-anchor conflicts with the real-time clause — score 7/10.** Well-grounded. DexWM is MPC-over-world-model (KH L36), place=28% (KH L41), on one Franka+Allegro family. MPC planning is not self-evidently real-time, and the card's own real-time rescue (H4 DexFuture 60 Hz) is a *different* backbone, so the briefing is right that the conflict stands. Docked one point because "MPC ≠ real-time" is asserted, not quantified — DexWM may run MPC at usable rates; the conflict is plausible but the briefing supplies no latency number to close it.

5. **(v) The whitespace is perishable (months) — score 5/10.** Plausible but soft and largely rhetorical. "Codec land-rush Feb-Jun 2026" is supported by the card's own row density (XL-VLA, UniHM, Canonical Hand, One-Policy-Fits-All, Latent Action Diffusion, House of Dextra all dated within the window), so the *codec* race is real. But perishability of the *protocol/benchmark* contribution — the surviving novelty per (ii) — is the opposite: protocols saturate slowly (the Academic's challenge), and the briefing's own actionable insight reframes D1 *as* a benchmark. So (v) argues against the wrong target.

**Mean of 5 scores = 6.0. Lowest = 4 (finding iii).**

##### 2. Weakest link

Finding **(iii) "partially pre-empted on framing."** It is the least reliable claim and the one that most changes the recommendation (DE-RISK vs reframe vs drop). To verify, one needs the thing neither the briefing nor I can currently confirm: does any paper run an **embodiment-agnostic intent model across two or more distinct multi-fingered hands on the full reach→grasp→reorient→place cycle**? LUCID (hand↔gripper, non-cycle) and DIAL (single-embodiment, wrong axis) do not. The specific info needed: a direct read of LUCID's transfer table to confirm it never evaluates two *multi-fingered* hands on reorientation/place, plus a literature sweep for a multi-fingered-to-multi-fingered full-cycle transfer result. If none exists, finding (iii) should be downgraded to "framing is consensus but the *cross-hand-cycle instantiation* is unpreempted" — which strengthens, not weakens, the commit case.

##### 3. Bias check

**Over-dominant perspective: the Skeptic.** The synthesis is organized around the Skeptic's "likely tie at the floor" and folds the Historian's relabeling worry into it as "the same failure mode," then makes the Blind Spot (distance control) the hinge. Three of five findings (i, iii, plus the hidden connection) are Skeptic-led, and the headline verdict (DE-RISK, kill-experiment-first, pre-register a tie kills it) is a Skeptic's protocol. The Academic's two correct objections — LUCID is hand↔gripper not full-cycle (which I confirm), and a protocol moat saturates slowly — are both filed as *mild challenges* and then overridden, when the LUCID one is actually decisive against finding (iii). The Economist's perishability claim is accepted at face value despite pointing at the wrong artifact. Net: skeptic-weighted, with the Academic under-credited where it was right.

##### 4. Missing perspective

**The hardware/embodiment-realist (a roboticist who has actually deployed multi-fingered hands).** Every finding treats "distinct hand" as a point in a morphology-distance metric (AnyBody splits, VAE interpolation). But the binding constraint on cross-hand dexterity is not parameterization choice — it is **tendon coupling, backlash, contact-sensor noise, and actuator bandwidth that differ per hand and are absent from sim**. An explicit-intent plan that is "correct" can still fail on a real Wuji hand because the actuation head cannot realize the commanded contact at the required rate. This angle changes the conclusion: the kill experiment as specified (sim AnyBody-extrapolation split) could yield a clean separation that *evaporates on hardware*, making the pre-registered "tie kills it / separation commits it" decision rule unsafe. It also reframes the frontier question: intent may be invariant in plan-space yet non-invariant in *realizable* action-space, which is a hardware fact, not a representation choice. The briefing's whole apparatus is sim-metric; a hardware-realist would demand the kill experiment include at least one real distinct hand before any commit.

##### 5. Overall grade and top fixes

**Grade: B.** This is a genuinely sharp, well-structured briefing — the hidden cross-perspective connection (null-by-tie vs null-by-silent-relabeling collapsing onto the distance-control blind spot) is a real insight, the de-risk-before-commit instinct is correct, and the "drop the 5.2× headline" catch is exactly right (UniDex KH L42 confirms 5.2× is the data-pipeline cost cut, not a cross-hand-control result, yet the card imports it into the bet at L842). It does not earn an A because two of its five findings lean on misread or over-generalized evidence, and the most consequential one (iii) is wrong on direction.

Top fixes, in priority order:
1. **Correct finding (iii).** DIAL is off-axis (single-embodiment VLM/policy decoupling); LUCID is hand↔gripper on non-cycle tasks. Downgrade "partially pre-empted — HIGH" to "principle is consensus, cross-hand-cycle instantiation is unpreempted." This *raises* the commit case the briefing was talking itself out of.
2. **Stop calling AnyBody's result "universal 0%."** It is "low SR, with a 0% on Arms-push," and it is on arms/grippers, not multi-fingered hands. Re-state finding (i) as a cross-class analogy and lower its confidence accordingly — otherwise the entire kill-experiment design rests on an over-read number.
3. **Add a hardware leg to the kill experiment.** Pre-registering a sim-only tie/separation decision is fragile; require at least one real distinct hand, because the realist failure mode (unrealizable intent) is invisible in AnyBody sim splits.
4. **Justify "reorientation is where parameterization matters most."** Finding (ii)'s phase-localization is asserted; either cite evidence or broaden the head-to-head to score per-phase and let the data locate the separation.
5. **Re-aim perishability (v).** If the surviving contribution is the protocol/benchmark, it saturates slowly; the perishable thing is the codec race, which D1 is *not* claiming. Separate the two so the recommendation is internally consistent.

### D2 (Direction review)

Topic: Tactile In-Hand Reorientation with Sim-to-Real — the bet that the privileged **object-pose-state** interface (PTLD) beats both the **contact-feature** interface (AnyRotate) and a **tactile-sim** pipeline (Force-Based Sim2Real) on the *same* reorientation task under matched perturbations, in a clean three-way interface-controlled comparison nobody has run.

The verbatim bet (First-principles framing): "The privileged object-pose-state interface ([[2603.04531|PTLD]]) beats both the contact-feature interface ([[2405.07391|AnyRotate]]) and a tactile-sim pipeline ([[2601.02778|Force-Based-Sim2Real]]) on the *same* reorientation task under matched perturbations — reaching PTLD's +182% rotation / +57% goals, [[2210.13702|DeXtreme]]'s 27.8-vs-14.8, and holding under [[2604.11138|ViserDex]]'s adversarial lighting (~25). Falsifiable two ways: if the contact-feature interface ties the object-pose-state one, the cheaper interface wins; if a tactile-sim pipeline matches either, the simulator was never the bottleneck."

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys daily).**
- *Core position*: The whole bet is gated by one practical fact the card half-admits: PTLD's object-pose-state interface needs an **instrumented real cell** (external cameras / mocap on every training object) to collect privileged pairs, while AnyRotate's contact-feature route only needs an instrumented tactile rig and Force-Based Sim2Real needs no privileged real rig at all. A "fair" three-way comparison is not a clean knob: the three interfaces carry wildly different real-data-collection costs, so "object-pose-state wins" may just mean "I spent more on instrumentation."
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: PTLD's own note states it uses "object pose from external cameras" as the privileged signal and trains the tactile encoder "via supervised learning using collected real-world data ... using DAgger" (2603.04531). 2605.21330 (Joint-Sensor) hits **100% rotation accuracy / drop-free** on a real ORCA hand with *zero* contact sensing and *zero* external instrumentation — a proprioception-only student. If a free interface already saturates rotation accuracy on real hardware, the deploy-time value of the expensive object-pose-state interface is unclear.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: Report **wall-clock and instrumentation cost per interface**, not just SR. The bet is only interesting if object-pose-state wins *at equal real-data budget*; otherwise the practitioner reads it as "pay-to-win" and reaches for the joint-sensor or DexNDM (2510.08556) residual-dynamics route that needs almost no real data.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the literature).**
- *Core position*: The card's central scholarly claim — that the object-pose-state vs contact-feature vs tactile-sim **interface choice has never been isolated** — is *literally* true as a controlled head-to-head, and that is a legitimate measurement gap. But the card overstates the binary "AnyRotate settled simulator-is-avoidable in 2024"; 2501.05439 (Qi/Malik, UC Berkeley + FAIR, 2025) already deploys a generalizable **object-pose-state** estimator (proprioception + skill-feedback + control-error → relative pose) for real reorientation, which is the object-pose-state arm in everything but the controlled-comparison framing.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: 2407.18834 (Shape-Conditioned) shows tactile-only object-pose-state estimation gives "comparable SR to object-specific SOTA," and ablating shape conditioning drops SR ~30% / raises angle error ~89% — direct evidence the *state being estimated* (pose+shape) is what carries performance, supporting H1's prediction. 2605.21330's object-pose RMSE of **13.70 mm** from proprioception-only quantifies exactly how lossy the cheapest interface is, giving H1 a real lossiness ladder (proprio 13.7 mm → tactile → object-pose oracle).
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: The surviving novelty is **only H1** (the three-way controlled comparison). The card itself concedes this ("H1 is the three-way interface comparison carrying the surviving novelty"). Frame the paper as a *measurement/benchmark contribution* (which interface to distill, isolated), not a *method* contribution — every method already exists.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks it's wrong or already solved).**
- *Core position*: The bet predicts a *winner* (object-pose-state beats both) but the card's own evidence predicts a *tie or split*, which collapses the contribution to "we ran the obvious ablation." Worse, the comparison may be confounded: PTLD, AnyRotate, and Force-Based Sim2Real differ in hand hardware, task definition, and objects, so "matched perturbations on the same task" requires re-implementing all three in one rig — a large engineering tax for a result that, if it ties, is a negative result.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: 2605.21330 already gets **100% rotation accuracy** real with the *lossiest* interface; if proprioception-only saturates the metric, the object-pose-state interface cannot "beat" it on rotation accuracy, only on harder metrics (consecutive reorientations, OOD objects). The card's own falsifiers are alarmingly easy to trip: AnyRotate's contact-feature already does zero-shot multi-axis gravity-invariant reorientation with emergent re-stabilization — a tie there is plausible, and a tie *kills the headline*.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: Pre-register the metric on which object-pose-state is *expected* to separate (likely: consecutive reorientations under slip + OOD-object generalization, not rotation accuracy). If you can't name a metric where the interfaces *must* diverge by first principles, the experiment is a coin-flip dressed as a hypothesis.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows incentives).**
- *Core position*: This direction is cheap to *frame* and expensive to *run*, which is the opposite of what an academic budget rewards. The privileged-distillation-for-tactile narrative is currently fashionable (FAIR/Meta authors on PTLD 2603.04531 and 2501.05439; ByteDance on Force-Based Sim2Real 2601.02778), so a controlled-comparison paper rides a hot wave — but the instrumented-cell + three-reimplementation cost is a poor fit for a single-student lab.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The anchor and threat papers cluster in big-lab-adjacent teams (Meta Reality Labs on 2603.05687 CGP; FAIR/Meta on PTLD and 2501.05439; ByteDance Seed on 2601.02778). Benchmarks the bet leans on (2001.03070 In-Hand Manipulation Benchmark, 2604.09294 POMDAR, 2410.24090 Sparsh) exist precisely because the field is saturating on per-method SR and wants standardized comparison — the incentive gradient *favors* a clean interface benchmark.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: The publishable, fundable version is the **benchmark/leaderboard** ("which privileged interface to distill") not the win-claim. A benchmark survives even if every interface ties (the tie *is* the finding); a win-claim paper dies on a tie. De-risk by shipping the comparison protocol as the artifact, not the verdict.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen the waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the **privileged/asymmetric-actor-critic teacher-student** lineage (Chebotar/Pinto → DeXtreme 2210.13702 → AnyRotate → PTLD) meeting the recurring **"what do we feed the teacher / distill to the student"** question — the same pixel-vs-latent and full-state-vs-observation debate that ran through Dreamer-era model-based RL. Every such wave eventually discovers the interface is *task-and-perturbation-dependent*, not universally rankable, which is exactly what H2 already predicts (tactile wins slip, vision wins lighting).
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The card's own H2/H5 hedge toward "no universal winner" (modality-vs-perturbation split; physics-grounded CoP intermediate 2605.28812 robust under OOD pose). DeXtreme's 27.8 (VADR) vs 14.8 (manual DR) shows domain-randomization quality, not interface choice, drove a near-2x swing — historically the *randomization/curriculum* axis has dominated interface axis in dexterous sim-to-real.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: Expect the honest result to be a **decision table** (interface × perturbation × object-class), echoing how the pixel-vs-latent debate resolved into "it depends." Sell that table as the contribution from day one; a single-number winner claim is the framing that history punishes.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *Skeptic vs the bet (Academic-adjacent)*: bet predicts object-pose-state **wins**; Skeptic + Historian + H2/H5 predict a **tie/split** ("it depends"). Concrete: AnyRotate already does zero-shot multi-axis gravity-invariant reorientation, so a contact-feature *tie* is plausible — which trips the card's own falsifier ("cheaper interface wins").
   - *Practitioner vs Academic on "fair comparison"*: Academic treats interface as a clean knob to isolate; Practitioner says the knob co-varies with real-data/instrumentation cost (object-pose-state needs an instrumented cell; proprioception-only 2605.21330 needs nothing yet hits 100% rotation accuracy), so "winning" may be a budget artifact.
   - *Economist vs the bet*: bet wants a *winner* (publishable headline); Economist says a winner-claim **dies on a tie** while a benchmark survives — reframe to the comparison protocol.

2. **Strongest vs weakest perspective.**
   - *Strongest*: **Academic** — its claim is checkable and survived the alphaxiv probe: no paper runs the isolated three-way interface comparison, and the card honestly localizes novelty to H1. Most evidence-bound.
   - *Weakest*: the **bet as stated** (object-pose-state wins outright) — its own card supplies counter-evidence (2605.21330's 100% rotation accuracy from the lossiest interface; H2/H5 predicting splits), so the directional claim is the least defensible part.

3. **The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction.**
   On a single fixed rig (one hand, one object set, one task) and **equal real-data budget**, is there *any* metric (e.g., consecutive reorientations under slip on OOD objects) where the object-pose-state interface separates from the contact-feature interface by a margin larger than the seed/DR variance — or do they tie everywhere that matters?

4. **What every perspective agrees on (likely true).**
   "Simulating the tactile *sensor* is avoidable" is settled (AnyRotate 2024, PTLD, Joint-Sensor, Shape-Conditioned all bridge with no tactile sim), and the *result will be perturbation/object-dependent*, not a clean universal ranking. The genuine, defensible gap is the **controlled isolation** of the interface choice, not the directional win.

5. **The blind spot (none addressed).**
   **Failure-mode taxonomy and recovery under the chosen interface.** Every perspective argues about *which interface yields higher aggregate SR*, but none asks *how each interface fails* (does object-pose-state degrade gracefully when the external estimator drifts? does contact-feature catastrophically drop on slip? what is the recovery behavior?). For deployment, the *shape of the failure distribution* per interface matters more than mean SR — and no hypothesis, risk, or perspective touches it.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**CEO summary (one paragraph, nuance).** D2's defensible core is narrow but real: nobody has run the clean, single-rig, matched-perturbation **three-way comparison** of privileged interfaces (object-pose-state vs contact-feature vs tactile-sim) for in-hand reorientation, and the card honestly localizes its surviving novelty to exactly that (H1). The novelty-as-measurement survives a literature check — alphaxiv surfaced no controlled interface ablation, and the two freshest near-misses (2603.05687 CGP, a visuotactile IL framework; 2501.05439, an object-pose-state estimator) corroborate the object-pose-state arm rather than pre-empt the comparison. But the card's *directional bet* — object-pose-state **wins** — is its weakest claim: its own evidence (2605.21330's 100% rotation accuracy from a no-instrumentation proprioception-only interface; H2/H5 predicting modality-vs-perturbation splits) points to a tie-or-split, and a win-claim paper dies on a tie while a benchmark survives. Commit only if you reframe from "object-pose-state wins" to "here is the interface-choice benchmark and the decision table," pre-register the metric where separation is expected (consecutive reorientations under slip on OOD objects, not rotation accuracy), and report per-interface real-data/instrumentation cost so the result isn't a pay-to-win artifact.

**Five key findings (ranked by reliability).**
1. *The isolated three-way interface comparison genuinely does not exist (still whitespace as a measurement).* — Supported by Academic, Economist; the alphaxiv probe returned only the card's own anchors plus corroborating-not-competing papers (2603.05687, 2501.05439). Challenged by: nobody strongly — even the Skeptic concedes the *comparison* is unrun; the Skeptic only challenges the *win-claim*.
2. *"Tactile-sensor-simulation is avoidable" is settled, so it is NOT the contribution.* — Supported by all five (AnyRotate 2024, PTLD, 2605.21330, 2407.18834 all bridge with no tactile sim). Challenged by: none — this is the universal-agreement item; it removes the card's headline framing from the novelty budget.
3. *The result is likely a split/decision-table, not a universal winner.* — Supported by Historian, Skeptic, and the card's own H2 (tactile-wins-slip / vision-wins-lighting) and H5 (CoP 2605.28812 OOD-robust). Challenged by: the bet's H1 directional prediction. This is the load-bearing risk to the headline.
4. *Interface choice co-varies with real-data/instrumentation cost, so a "fair" comparison must equalize budget.* — Supported by Practitioner, Economist (2603.04531 needs an instrumented cell; 2605.21330 needs none yet hits 100% rotation accuracy). Challenged by: Academic's clean-knob framing. If unaddressed, "wins" is uninterpretable.
5. *Rotation accuracy is the wrong separating metric; pick a harder one or the experiment is a coin-flip.* — Supported by Skeptic, Practitioner (2605.21330 saturates rotation accuracy with the lossiest interface). Challenged by: the card's "reaching PTLD's +182% rotation" target, which leans on a metric that may already be saturated by cheaper interfaces.

**Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Practitioner's "object-pose-state needs an instrumented cell" and the Economist's "ship the benchmark not the verdict" are the same insight from two directions: the instrumentation asymmetry is *precisely why* a single win-number is uninterpretable and a *budget-normalized decision table* is the only honest artifact. The cost asymmetry isn't just a deployment footnote (the card's Risk row) — it is the confound that forces the reframe from method to benchmark.

**Actionable insight (COMMIT / DE-RISK / DROP).** **DE-RISK, then commit as a benchmark.** Do not commit to the win-claim. Concrete next experiment: on one fixed rig (single hand — ORCA or Allegro — one YCB-derived object set, one continuous-reorientation task from 2001.03070's pose-change suite), implement the three interfaces — object-pose-state (PTLD-style external-camera oracle), contact-feature (AnyRotate-style contact pose+force), tactile-sim (Force-Based Sim2Real distance-field) — plus the *free* proprioception-only control (2605.21330) as the saturation baseline, all distilled into the same student architecture, trained at **equal real-data budget**, and evaluated on **consecutive reorientations under matched slip + OOD-object perturbations** (the metric where separation is expected), with **per-interface instrumentation cost reported**. Pre-register: object-pose-state separates from contact-feature by > seed/DR variance on the slip+OOD metric. If it ties, the paper is the benchmark + decision table (still publishable); if it separates, you have the win. This converts the card's two falsifiers from paper-killers into findings.

**Frontier question that would change everything.** Is the privileged *state* the policy needs even a fixed object-pose target — or is the right interface a **learned, task-conditioned latent** (à la the asymmetric-actor-critic online latent distillation PTLD already uses) whose information content the policy selects per-perturbation, dissolving the discrete object-pose-state-vs-contact-feature-vs-tactile-sim taxonomy the entire bet is built on?

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics faculty. Card under review: `Capability/Manipulation.md` D2 (lines 905-985). I read the card before scoring; where the briefing and the card disagree I sided with the card text.

##### 1. Confidence scores (1-10, reliability of each key finding)

1. **The isolated three-way interface comparison genuinely does not exist — 8/10.** The card's own evidence base supports this: PTLD (2603.04531) "never compared to a contact-feature interface," AnyRotate (2405.07391) "runs no interface head-to-head," and no row in the 23-paper table claims the matched-perturbation ablation. The two "near-misses" the briefing leans on to harden this are weaker than advertised: 2603.05687 (CGP) is a visuotactile diffusion-IL policy for general contact-rich manip (jar-opening, box-flipping), not an interface-ablation or even reorientation-specific paper, and it is not even cited in the card; 2501.05439 is in neither the card nor KH. So the whitespace is real, but the "alphaxiv probe corroborates" support is softer than the finding's 8 implies — I dock it for resting reliability on two papers that do not actually sit on the comparison axis.

2. **'Tactile-sensor-simulation is avoidable' is settled, so it is NOT the contribution — 9/10.** Strongest finding. The card itself opens with this concession ("now settled — AnyRotate, CoRL'24, two years before PTLD") and the table is dense with no-tactile-sim routes (DeXtreme, ViserDex, 2605.21330 proprioception-only, 2407.18834 tactile-only, 2510.08556 DexNDM, 2503.02738 co-training). Universal-agreement, in-card, well-anchored. The 1-point dock is only because "settled" is a strong word for a 2024-2026 literature where the avoidance routes are heterogeneous and none is yet a community default.

3. **The result is likely a split/decision-table, not a universal winner — 8/10.** Well-grounded and the most consequential to the headline. The card's own H2 (tactile-wins-slip / vision-wins-lighting), H5 (CoP OOD-robustness), and crucially the 2605.21330 row (a proprioception-only, no-contact interface that already hits 100% rotation accuracy) all point to a tie-or-split. Note the card's H1 *falsifier* already concedes this outcome ("contact-feature ties → cheaper interface wins") and frames it as informative — so the briefing's framing of this as an unaddressed risk overstates the card's blindness. The substance is right; the novelty of the observation relative to the card is lower than presented.

4. **Interface choice co-varies with real-data/instrumentation cost; a fair comparison must equalize budget — 7/10.** This is the briefing's best original contribution and I credit it. The card has the cost asymmetry only as a Risk-row footnote ("one-time data-collection cost, not a deployment dependency") and does NOT fold budget-equalization into the H1 design — yet 2605.21330 needs no instrumented cell and saturates the headline metric while PTLD needs an instrumented real pair. Without equalizing real-data budget, "object-pose-state wins" is confounded by "object-pose-state had a richer oracle." Docked to 7 because "must equalize budget" is itself a contestable experimental-design stance: one can argue the honest comparison is each interface *at its native cost* (the deployment-relevant question), and forcing equal budget can hide that object-pose-state is simply more expensive. The briefing treats budget-equalization as obviously correct when it is a defensible-but-arguable design choice.

5. **Rotation accuracy is the wrong separating metric — 6/10.** Directionally correct but the finding misreads the card. The briefing says the card's "+182% rotation target leans on a possibly-saturated metric." True for the H1 *prediction prose* ("recovers or exceeds +182% rotation"). But the H1 *test* already specifies 2001.03070's goal-state-error pose-change suite under matched perturbations, and the card already surfaces the harder axes the fix recommends: ViserDex's 37.6 *consecutive* reorientations, DeXtreme's 27.8 consecutive (H4), and OOD-pose degradation (H5). So the card is most of the way to the recommended "pre-register consecutive-reorientations-under-slip-on-OOD" metric already; the saturation critique lands on one prose phrase, not the experimental design. The underlying point (don't headline a saturated metric) is valid, hence 6 not lower, but the finding's confidence is inflated by attacking a metric the test does not actually use.

Mean = 7.6. Lowest = 6 (finding 5).

##### 2. Weakest link

Finding 5 (rotation-accuracy-is-the-wrong-metric, 6/10) is the least reliable as stated, because its premise (the card's experiment separates on rotation accuracy) is contradicted by the card's H1 test row, which already uses goal-state-error on 2001.03070. What would verify it: extract from the actual papers whether the YCB goal-state-error suite (2001.03070) and consecutive-reorientation counts (ViserDex 2604.11138, DeXtreme 2210.13702) are themselves saturated at the top of the leaderboard, or whether they retain headroom under slip+OOD. If those harder metrics also saturate, the finding strengthens to an 8; if they have headroom (likely, given 0% drop / 1.32 cm is described as "the best method," implying a frontier still being pushed), the finding is largely moot because the card already routes around the saturated metric. This is a 20-minute check against the four anchor PDFs; it was not done in the briefing.

A secondary weak link sits inside finding 1: the corroboration leg rests on 2603.05687 and 2501.05439, neither of which is on the interface-comparison axis (CGP is a general visuotactile diffusion policy; 2501.05439 is uninspected and absent from the vault). Verifying would require reading both abstracts to confirm they are corroborating-not-competing — the briefing asserts this without showing CGP is even reorientation-relevant.

##### 3. Bias check — which perspective over-dominated

**The Economist over-dominated, with the Practitioner as its echo.** The entire synthesis funnels to one verdict ("ship the benchmark, not the verdict") and the "hidden cross-perspective connection" explicitly collapses the Practitioner into the Economist ("the same insight from two directions"). That is not a discovered connection; it is two cost-framing perspectives reinforcing each other and being counted twice. The Academic (clean-knob, novelty-as-measurement) appears only as the foil that every finding "challenges," and the Historian contributes a single line (H2/H5 lineage). The result is a briefing that is correct in its bottom line (de-risk to a benchmark) but arrives there by stacking the deck: four of five findings push toward "don't claim the win," and the one perspective that would defend the directional bet (Academic) is given no genuine advocacy. A balanced synthesis would have one finding seriously arguing *why object-pose-state should win on first-principles* (task-completeness of pose vs lossiness of contact-features is a real mechanistic argument the card makes) before dismissing it.

##### 4. Missing perspective — a 6th angle that would change conclusions

**The control-theorist / sufficient-statistic view, which the briefing only gestures at in its Frontier question.** The entire bet is built on a discrete interface taxonomy (object-pose-state vs contact-feature vs tactile-sim). A control theorist would ask: what is the *minimal sufficient statistic* of the contact state for the reorientation MDP? If the policy only needs a low-dimensional function of contact (e.g., center-of-pressure, per 2605.28812 CoP, or a learned task-conditioned latent à la PTLD's asymmetric-actor-critic), then "which interface" is the wrong question — all three interfaces are lossy *projections* of the same underlying sufficient statistic, and the winner is whichever projection happens to preserve that statistic for *this* task. This dissolves the three-way comparison into "which interface best approximates the task-sufficient statistic," which is object- and perturbation-dependent by construction — predicting a *split*, and predicting it for a principled reason rather than the briefing's empirical hunch. It also reframes the contribution: the publishable artifact becomes "the sufficient-statistic each interface preserves and loses," not a decision table of raw SRs. This perspective would harden finding 3 to a near-certainty and would change the actionable from "run the budget-matched bake-off" to "first characterize the task's sufficient statistic, then test which interface preserves it" — a stronger and more falsifiable experiment. The briefing relegated exactly this to a closing Frontier question instead of letting it reshape the recommendation.

##### 5. Overall grade and top fixes

**Grade: B+.** This is a competent, honest, well-anchored review that correctly identifies the load-bearing weakness (the directional win-claim is fragile; the defensible artifact is a benchmark) and surfaces one genuinely original confound (instrumentation-budget asymmetry, finding 4). It loses the A-range for three reasons: (a) it attacks a metric the card's test does not use (finding 5 is partly a strawman), (b) it double-counts the cost perspective and calls the redundancy a "hidden connection," and (c) it presents the tie-or-split as an unaddressed card blind-spot when the card's own H1 falsifier already concedes and reframes it. The briefing is more aligned with the card than its critical tone suggests — much of the "fix" (harder metric, falsifiers-as-findings) is already latent in the card.

Top fixes, in priority order:
1. **Re-ground finding 5 against the card's actual H1 test (goal-state-error on 2001.03070), not the prose's +182% rotation.** Then verify whether the harder consecutive-reorientation / goal-state metrics are themselves saturated before declaring the experiment a coin-flip. Right now the strongest-sounding metric critique rests on a phrase, not the design.
2. **Make the instrumentation-budget confound (finding 4) the headline contribution, but argue both sides.** Equal-budget vs native-cost is a real design fork; the briefing should state which question it is answering (deployment-relevant cost-normalized, or scientific interface-isolation) rather than asserting equalization is obviously correct.
3. **Promote the sufficient-statistic framing (currently the Frontier footnote) into the recommendation.** It predicts the split for a principled reason and turns a bake-off into a characterization study — a better paper than a decision table.
4. **Stop double-counting Economist+Practitioner.** Give the Academic one finding of genuine advocacy for the directional bet so the synthesis is adversarial, not a four-against-one pile-on toward a foregone "ship the benchmark" conclusion.
5. **Drop or downgrade 2603.05687 / 2501.05439 as corroboration.** Neither sits on the interface-comparison axis (CGP is a general visuotactile diffusion policy; 2501.05439 is not even in the vault). Citing off-axis papers as literature-check support weakens an otherwise solid whitespace claim.

---

### D3 (Direction review)

Topic: Exploration-Driven Emergent Dexterity. The bet (verbatim): "At high DoF, demonstration-free task-agnostic diverse-reset RL yields *emergent multi-phase* dexterity (not curriculum-to-single-goal) transferring zero-shot at OmniReset's 25% real (vs 4% demo-DP), and task-space/joint-space decomposition reaches 81.4% sim (vs 13.2% monolithic) with 22/26 unseen-object real."

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily).**
- *Core position*: The 25% real peg-insertion of [[2603.15789|OmniReset]] is a sim-trained, distilled, zero-shot *floor* — a number you would never ship. The honest comparison is not "25% vs 4% demo-DP" but "25% zero-shot vs what 30 minutes of on-robot refinement ([[2605.05172|Q2RL]] 3.75x in 1-2 hrs; [[2410.21845|HIL-SERL]]) buys you," and there the emergent-RL advantage may evaporate.
- *Strongest support*: The card's own risk row concedes 25% is low and proposes coupling to [[2210.13702|DeXtreme]] randomization + [[2605.05172|Q2RL]] refinement. [[2605.03363|Hierarchical-RL-QP-Grasp]]'s 22/26 unseen real is the genuinely deployable result here — and it deploys because the QP layer *guarantees* kinematic feasibility, not because RL emerged anything.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: The compute to run OmniReset-scale diverse-reset PPO (thousands of parallel envs, gSDE) is the real cost, and the "reset design" the card waves at as cheap (reaching/near-object/grasp/near-goal) is *exactly* the per-task hand-engineering you do in the trenches — relabeled. The reset-design effort is not free; it is task-knowledge in a different syntax.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
- *Core position*: The card is admirably honest that the principle is old ([[1707.05300|Reverse-Curriculum-Generation]] 2017, [[2204.02041|Example-based-Resets]], [[2104.11203|Reset]]), so the novelty rests entirely on one word: *emergent multi-phase at high DoF*. But the literature already contains demonstration-free high-DoF dexterity from cleverly-generated reset/exploration distributions — [[2303.03486|SBRL]] (G-RRT planner-generated resets, real multi-revolution reorientation on concave L-shapes, intrinsic sensing only).
- *Strongest support*: [[2603.10971|ContactExplorer]] reaches 86% over 7 tasks (vs 54-58% intrinsic baselines, 88% vs 0% on Constrained-Object-Retrieval, 76.7% real singulation, 16-DoF LEAP) using a *different* lever (contact-coverage intrinsic reward), demonstrating that the "behavior unlock" the card attributes to reset diversity is achievable by exploration-reward design at matched DoF. The bet's falsifier is therefore live, not hypothetical.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: "Emergent multi-phase" is an under-defined claim. Reverse-curriculum already produces multi-stage behavior toward a goal; the card's distinction ("multi-phase emergence" vs "curriculum-to-single-goal") needs an operational metric (phase count? phase reuse across tasks?) before it is a publishable contribution rather than a framing.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks the bet is wrong or already solved).**
- *Core position*: The card silently fuses two unrelated bets into one card. H1 (reset diversity is the emergence lever) and H2 (task/joint decomposition recovers 81.4% vs 13.2%) share *only* the word "dexterity" — [[2605.03363|Hierarchical-RL-QP-Grasp]]'s gain comes from a QP controller and CTDE multi-agent structure, nothing to do with reset distributions. A reviewer will read this as two half-papers stapled together.
- *Strongest support (the evidence the card under-weights)*: [[2502.18423|Retrieval-Dexterity]] is a high-DoF dexterous exemplar where *reward shaping itself* (potential-based) produces emergent skills (pushing/stirring/poking), +59.4% unseen retrieval, 100% generalization gain — i.e. the exact counter-position the bet says is "not the lever." The card lists it but does not resolve that it directly contradicts the headline claim. If reward shaping yields emergence at high DoF, "reset diversity, not reward" is already falsified in the literature.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: OmniReset is the *only* paper demonstrating the precise conjunction (task-agnostic + diverse-reset + emergent-multi-phase + high-DoF). A single-paper existence proof is not a whitespace — it is either (a) the field hasn't caught up, or (b) the conjunction is brittle/uninteresting. The card never distinguishes which, and that is the whole bet.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
- *Core position*: "Demonstration-free, task-agnostic, one reward, scale compute" is the narrative that flatters whoever owns the GPU simulator (NVIDIA/Isaac, ManiSkill3) — OmniReset is UW+NVIDIA, [[2511.21011|Staggered-Resets]] is UCSD/ManiSkill3. The incentive structure rewards "compute-scaling beats demonstrations" papers regardless of whether the resulting policy is the cheapest path to the capability.
- *Strongest support*: The lineage is dominated by groups with massively-parallel-sim access (IsaacGym G-RRT in [[2303.03486|SBRL]]; PPO+gSDE at scale in OmniReset). The "data is expensive, compute is cheap" framing is an institutional bet, not a neutral finding — academia chasing it because real bimanual/dexterous teleop data is the scarce resource the card itself flags (4-order gap vs OXE in Cluster E).
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: Benchmark saturation is the hidden driver — [[2605.16257|DexJoCo]] (11-task) exists precisely because the field needs a fresh leaderboard now that single-task in-hand reorientation is saturated. Committing to "emergent multi-phase" is partly committing to be measured on a benchmark whose metrics are still being negotiated, which is citation-rich but reproducibility-poor.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the automatic-curriculum / start-state-generation wave (Florensa 2017 reverse curriculum, GoExplore, asymmetric self-play) returning in dexterous-hand clothing. Each prior cycle promised "exploration solves itself if you set the right state distribution" and each delivered on toy/low-DoF then stalled on the design cost of *defining* the distribution — which is where the card's own first risk lands.
- *Strongest support*: [[2104.11203|Reset]] (2021) already ran this exact playbook demonstration-free on a 22-DoF real hand-arm (70% valve, 60 h) — and needed a *human-designed task graph* to orchestrate the "organic resets." The autonomy was real but the curriculum was hand-built. OmniReset's "diverse resets" (reaching/near-object/grasp/near-goal) are the same human-chosen interaction modes under a new name.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: The end-to-end-vs-modular pendulum is mid-swing back to modular — H2's QP decomposition ([[2605.03363|Hierarchical-RL-QP-Grasp]]) is the classic "let RL do high-level, let a model-based controller do low-level safety" move that recurs every time pure end-to-end RL hits a sim2real wall. That is a *reliable* bet historically; the emergent-RL half (H1) is the *speculative* one. The card's strength and weakness sit in different hypotheses.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *Skeptic vs the card's headline*: Skeptic says [[2502.18423|Retrieval-Dexterity]] proves reward shaping *does* yield emergent high-DoF skills (+100% generalization), directly contradicting "reset diversity, not reward shaping, gates emergence." Academic reinforces via [[2603.10971|ContactExplorer]] (exploration-reward lever, 86%). The card's bet asserts the negative; two cited papers assert the positive.
   - *Practitioner vs Economist/card*: Practitioner says the 25% floor is uncompetitive once you allow cheap on-robot refinement; the card frames 25% as a triumph over 4% demo-DP. The comparison baseline is contested.
   - *Historian vs card's novelty*: Historian says OmniReset's "diverse resets" = [[2104.11203|Reset]]'s hand-designed task graph relabeled; the card insists "task-agnostic" is the new thing. They disagree on whether the human design effort moved or just renamed.

2. **Strongest vs weakest perspective.** Strongest: **Skeptic** — it is grounded in two papers the card itself cites ([[2502.18423|Retrieval-Dexterity]], [[2603.10971|ContactExplorer]]) that instantiate the *opposite* lever at matched DoF, so the falsifier is already partly satisfied in print, not merely proposable. Weakest: **Economist** — directionally right about incentives but supplies no metric that changes the commit/drop decision; it explains *why* the narrative is popular, not whether it is *true*.

3. **The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction.** Hold task, DoF, reward-class and compute fixed, and ablate *only* the exploration lever — diverse-reset distribution ([[2603.15789|OmniReset]]) vs potential-based reward shaping ([[2502.18423|Retrieval-Dexterity]]) vs contact-coverage intrinsic reward ([[2603.10971|ContactExplorer]]) vs planner-generated resets ([[2303.03486|SBRL]]): does reset diversity produce *more* (and more reusable) emergent phases than the reward/intrinsic levers at equal compute? If not, the headline bet is false.

4. **What every perspective agrees on (likely true).** The H2 task-space-RL + joint-space-QP decomposition is a real, deployable advance: 81.4% vs 13.2% and 22/26 unseen real ([[2605.03363|Hierarchical-RL-QP-Grasp]], with [[2509.01044|Hierarchical-Reactive-Grasping]] as the true predecessor baseline) survives every lens — Practitioner ships it, Academic accepts the gap, Skeptic doesn't dispute it, Historian calls it the reliable modular swing. The decomposition is the safe asset in this card.

5. **The blind spot (none addressed).** *Object/scene generation coupled to reset diversity.* Every perspective argues about robot-state and reward, but a reset distribution is only as rich as the *objects and goals* it resets onto — none of the five (nor the card) treats procedural object/task generation as the actual coverage bottleneck. "Diverse resets" on a fixed object set is a far weaker claim than diverse resets over a generated object/goal distribution, and the card never separates the two.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**CEO summary.** D3 is two bets wearing one card. The *decomposition* bet (H2: task-space RL + joint-space QP, 81.4% vs 13.2%, 22/26 unseen real) is low-risk, deployable, and survives every adversarial lens — but its novelty is modest and its real predecessor ([[2509.01044|Hierarchical-Reactive-Grasping]]) is already in the card, so it is a solid *contribution* not a *thesis*. The *emergence* bet (H1: task-agnostic diverse-reset RL gates emergent multi-phase dexterity at high DoF, *not* reward shaping) is the genuinely novel-sounding claim, but it rests on a single existence proof ([[2603.15789|OmniReset]], 25% real), and two papers the card already cites instantiate the *opposite* lever (reward/intrinsic) at matched DoF ([[2502.18423|Retrieval-Dexterity]] +100% generalization; [[2603.10971|ContactExplorer]] 86%), so the bet's own falsifier is half-satisfied in print. The whitespace is real but narrow: nobody has run the controlled lever-ablation that would *prove* reset diversity beats reward shaping for emergence — which is exactly the paper to write.

**5 key findings (ranked by reliability).**
1. *The H2 decomposition advance is real and deployable* (81.4% vs 13.2%, 22/26 unseen real). Supported by Practitioner, Academic, Historian; not challenged by Skeptic. Most reliable — but its predecessor [[2509.01044|Hierarchical-Reactive-Grasping]] caps the novelty.
2. *OmniReset's 25% real is a zero-shot floor, not a deployment number.* Supported by Practitioner, Economist, and the card's own risk row; challenged by nobody. Reliable.
3. *The "emergence lever" claim is contested by cited evidence.* [[2502.18423|Retrieval-Dexterity]] (reward) and [[2603.10971|ContactExplorer]] (intrinsic) achieve emergent high-DoF skills via non-reset levers. Supported by Skeptic, Academic; the card lists but does not refute them. Moderately reliable as a *threat*.
4. *"Task-agnostic diverse resets" still encode task knowledge.* Reaching/near-object/grasp/near-goal are human-chosen interaction modes ([[2104.11203|Reset]]'s task graph relabeled). Supported by Historian, Practitioner; the card concedes this in Risk #1. Moderately reliable.
5. *The novel conjunction (task-agnostic + diverse-reset + emergent-multi-phase + high-DoF) is single-paper.* Supported by Skeptic; the alphaxiv sweep returned no second instance. Reliable as a *whitespace* finding, ambiguous on whether the gap is opportunity or brittleness.

**Hidden cross-perspective connection.** Practitioner ("reset design is relabeled task engineering") and Historian ("[[2104.11203|Reset]]'s task graph is the same human design") and Skeptic ("two half-papers") converge on one underlying fact: *the card's real contribution is not the emergence claim but the formalization of where human effort goes.* Reframing the bet as "we replaced per-task reward/curriculum design with per-task reset-distribution design — and measured the exchange rate" turns three independent objections into the paper's thesis. The design-effort accounting *is* the de-risked contribution hiding inside the speculative one.

**Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK, do not commit as-stated.** The card's headline bet ("reset diversity, not reward shaping") is too exposed — two cited papers can be cited *against* you on day one. Reframe and split:
- *Concrete next experiment*: run the Prompt-2 lever-ablation. Fix one high-DoF task (an in-hand reorientation + a peg insert), fix reward-class and compute, and sweep the exploration lever: diverse-reset ([[2603.15789|OmniReset]]) vs potential-shaping ([[2502.18423|Retrieval-Dexterity]]) vs contact-coverage intrinsic ([[2603.10971|ContactExplorer]]) vs planner-generated reset ([[2303.03486|SBRL]]). Primary metric: number and cross-task reuse of emergent phases per unit compute, plus the *design-effort* each lever required (the exchange-rate the card's Risk #1 asks for). This converts an unfalsifiable framing into a measured comparison and pre-empts the Skeptic.
- Keep H2 (decomposition) as the *reliable backbone* result and the lever-ablation as the *novel* contribution; do not let the card stay as two stapled bets.

**Frontier question.** Is "reset diversity" and "exploration reward" the same thing viewed from opposite ends — i.e. does *any* mechanism that broadens visited-state coverage (planner-generated resets, intrinsic contact-coverage, potential shaping) produce identical emergent-phase structure at equal coverage, making the *generation mechanism* irrelevant and *coverage* the only real variable? If yes, the entire D3 axis collapses to a single coverage metric and the field has been arguing about implementations of one quantity.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics faculty. Mandate: find soft claims and over-reach, not rubber-stamp. Card under review: `Capability/Manipulation.md` L986–1064 (D3 — Exploration-Driven Emergent Dexterity). Eight load-bearing papers verified present in `_KnowledgeHub_`; the three contested levers (OmniReset 2603.15789, Retrieval Dexterity 2502.18423, ContactExplorer 2603.10971) were cross-read against their KH notes to ground the scores below.*

---

##### 1. Confidence scores (5 key findings, 1–10 reliability)

| # | Finding | Score | One-line justification |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | H2 decomposition advance is real and deployable (81.4% vs 13.2%; 22/26 unseen real), but predecessor 2509.01044 caps the novelty | **9** | Two-paper convergence from the *same MIT lab* (2605.03363 + 2509.01044), numbers internally consistent in-card and the novelty cap is self-disclosed; the only residual risk is that "deployable" rests on the card's own reported real-world counts, not an independent replication. |
| 2 | OmniReset's 25% real is a zero-shot floor, not a deployment number | **9** | Directly verified in KH note: "25% zero-shot success rate... outperforming a diffusion policy (4%)." Self-evidently a floor; the card's Risk row concedes it. Hard to be wrong about a number that is explicitly low. |
| 3 | The "emergence lever" claim is contested by cited evidence (Retrieval Dexterity reward + ContactExplorer intrinsic achieve emergent high-DoF skills via non-reset levers) | **7** | Both counter-papers verified in KH: 2502.18423 explicitly credits *potential-based reward shaping* for emergent pushing/stirring/poking (+100% generalization); 2603.10971 is *intrinsic contact-coverage* at 16-DoF LEAP (86%). The threat is genuine. Docked because neither runs the *controlled* lever-ablation, both sit at *different* DoF/embodiment, and "emergence" is used loosely across all three — so this is a live threat, not a refutation. The briefing labels it "threat," which is correct calibration. |
| 4 | "Task-agnostic diverse resets" still encode task knowledge (reaching/near-object/grasp/near-goal are human-chosen modes) | **8** | The KH note itself lists those four modes as hand-specified "interaction modes," and the card concedes it in Risk #1. The relabel-of-2104.11203's-task-graph framing is a fair and well-supported observation. Docked one point because "encodes task knowledge" is partly a definitional argument (all priors encode *some* knowledge) rather than a measured quantity. |
| 5 | The novel conjunction (task-agnostic + diverse-reset + emergent-multi-phase + high-DoF) is single-paper (OmniReset only) | **6** | The "n=1 existence proof" claim is correct *as stated*, but the reliability ceiling is the sweep itself: "the alphaxiv sweep returned no second instance" is an absence-of-evidence claim from a degraded/limited search, not a proof of whitespace. The briefing honestly flags the opportunity-vs-brittleness ambiguity, which is the right move, but the gap could be "nobody bothered because the conjunction is a wording artifact" rather than "nobody has done it yet." |

**Mean = 7.8. Min = 6.**

---

##### 2. Weakest link

**Finding #5 (novel conjunction is single-paper), score 6.** The claim that the four-way conjunction is genuine whitespace rests entirely on a null search result. An absence returned by a semantic sweep is the weakest possible evidence type, and the briefing's own caveat ("ambiguous on whether the gap is opportunity or brittleness") concedes the conjunction may be a *labeling* artifact — i.e., OmniReset is the only paper that happens to use all four descriptors at once, not the only paper to achieve the underlying phenomenon.

**Specific info needed to verify:** (a) a definition-level operationalization of "emergent multi-phase" with a *measurable* threshold (number of distinct contact phases, cross-task phase reuse) so the conjunction is testable rather than rhetorical; (b) re-run the sweep with the phenomenon decomposed into its mechanism primitives (diverse-reset RL ∩ ≥16-DoF ∩ demonstration-free ∩ ≥2 emergent sub-skills) rather than the verbatim phrase, to distinguish "no second paper" from "no second paper that phrases it this way." Until (a) exists, the falsifier in H1 ("multi-phase appears at a single reset distribution") is not crisply decidable, which propagates uncertainty into the whole emergence bet.

---

##### 3. Bias check — which perspective over-dominated

**Skeptic-dominant, with Historian as a close second.** The synthesis is unusually well-served by the adversarial lenses: the entire CEO summary is organized around "two bets wearing one card," the half-satisfied falsifier, and the design-effort reframe — all Skeptic/Historian moves. That is appropriate for a peer-review-facing briefing and is *not* the usual failure mode (Practitioner-boosterism), so I credit it.

But the dominance has a cost: the **Practitioner and Economist voices are thin where they would bite hardest.** The briefing cites Practitioner on "reset design is relabeled task engineering" (a Skeptic-flavored point) but never invokes the Practitioner's *deployment* objection to the recommended experiment itself — the proposed lever-ablation (in-hand reorientation + peg insert, four levers, matched compute, emergent-phase counting) is a 4-arm sim study of debatable real-world payoff, and no one in the synthesis asks whether the *exchange-rate* metric is measurable to publication standard. The Economist is invoked only to second the "25% is a floor" point, never to price the ablation's cost-vs-novelty. Net: the synthesis is biased toward *de-risking the thesis on paper* and under-weights *whether the de-risked experiment is worth a robotics-lab-year*. Not "balanced," but biased in a defensible direction.

---

##### 4. Missing perspective — a 6th angle that would change conclusions

**The Reproducibility / Statistical-Methodologist.** Every headline number in this card is a single-seed, single-lab point estimate: 81.4% vs 13.2%, 25% vs 4%, 86%, +100% generalization. None carry confidence intervals, seed counts, or per-object breakdowns, and 22/26 unseen real is n=26 (a 95% CI on that proportion spans roughly 68–96%). The briefing's central recommendation — a *lever-ablation* whose primary metric is "number and cross-task reuse of emergent phases per unit compute" — is precisely the kind of comparison where unreported variance silently determines the result: at the seed counts these RL papers typically use, a 4-arm exploration-lever comparison could easily produce a non-significant ordering that reverses across seeds. This perspective changes the conclusion in two ways: (1) it demotes Finding #1 from "9" toward "the gap is real but the *magnitude* is unverified," and (2) it adds a precondition to the actionable insight — the lever-ablation must be **power-analyzed and multi-seed from the start**, or it will reproduce the field's own irreproducibility and prove nothing about reset-vs-reward. A briefing that frames "measure the exchange rate" as the thesis but never mentions variance is one significant-figure away from over-claiming.

---

##### 5. Overall grade + top fixes

**Grade: A−.**

This is a strong, intellectually honest briefing. It correctly identifies that D3 is two stapled bets, correctly ranks the low-risk H2 backbone above the speculative H1 emergence claim, and — most impressively — converts three independent objections (Practitioner, Historian, Skeptic) into a single coherent thesis (design-effort accounting / exchange-rate) that is *more* publishable than the original headline. The "reset diversity vs exploration reward are two ends of a coverage axis" frontier question is a genuinely good first-principles probe and is the right kill-shot to test before committing. The reason it is A− and not A is that it (a) leans on a single null-search result for its whitespace claim, (b) treats "emergence" as a settled construct when it is the un-operationalized hinge of the whole bet, and (c) never prices variance into the very experiment it recommends.

**Top things to fix, in priority order:**

1. **Operationalize "emergent multi-phase dexterity" before anything else.** Give it a measurable definition (distinct contact-phase count + cross-task phase reuse) with a threshold. Without it, H1's falsifier is undecidable and Finding #5's whitespace is a wording artifact. *(This is the single most important fix.)*
2. **Power-analyze the recommended lever-ablation and run it multi-seed.** Add the 6th (Statistical) perspective: pre-register seed count, report CIs, or the reset-vs-reward result will not survive replication.
3. **Demote the whitespace claim from "search returned nothing" to a decomposed mechanism sweep.** Re-run the absence check on primitives, not the verbatim conjunction, and label the result opportunity-vs-brittleness explicitly (the briefing gestures at this; make it a deliverable).
4. **Keep the split, but state H2's novelty ceiling in the thesis row, not buried in the table.** The card's Thesis (L991) still reads as one unified emergence bet; 2509.01044 already caps the decomposition half's novelty, so the card should lead with "solid contribution (H2) + speculative ablation (H1)," matching the briefing's own correct read.
5. **Add an Economist line to the actionable insight.** State the cost (lab-time, robot-hours) of the 4-arm ablation against its expected novelty payoff, so "stop stapling two bets" is a resourcing decision, not just a framing one.

---

### D4 (Direction review)

Topic: D4 — Force-Safety-Constrained Dexterous Control. The bet (verbatim from the card's First-principles framing): "A fragile gentle-force *hard projection* over a learned dexterous policy bounds contact force below DexSkin's 1.53 kPa / Multi-Sensory Sparse Experts' ~10 N with *zero* force-violation, and beats the soft-penalty Stress-Guided RL (36.5% stress cut) at *matched* SR — keeping Hierarchical RL-QP Grasp's 81.4% (vs 13.2% unconstrained) and zero-shot steerability."

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily).**
Core position: The hard-projection-vs-soft-penalty dichotomy is real on paper but the binding constraint in the lab is *force observability*, not the filter math — a QP can only project to a force bound you can measure at control rate, and on a multi-fingered hand under occlusion you usually cannot. The card's own anchors quietly admit this: DexSkin (2509.18830) needed a custom interpretable-force skin to even hit 1.53 kPa, and DOBCBF (2411.07833) needs electromagnetic tactile + a Kelvin-Voigt contact model to estimate fingertip force/torque.
Strongest supporting evidence: ContactRL (2512.03707) does NOT bound force directly — it bounds *kinetic energy* via an eCBF and gets force <10 N (mode ~1 N) *implicitly*, precisely because measuring energy from velocity is cheap and measuring true contact-force-magnitude per fingertip is not. Tabero (2605.27886) reports per-step force only because it has a GelSight on a parallel jaw — a 2-contact, well-instrumented case, not a 23-DoF hand.
The one thing only this perspective says: Do not bet on "explicit kPa-ceiling QP over a learned dexterous policy" until you have a deployable, control-rate, per-fingertip force estimate. The H1 experiment will silently degrade into ContactRL's *energy-proxy* ceiling on real hardware — which is already published. Run the H1 head-to-head first in sim where ground-truth particle stress exists (Stress-Guided RL's exact setting), and be honest that the real-robot version inherits E2's cross-sensor estimation problem.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
