Core position: The card's literature read is accurate and unusually self-aware — it already names the three papers that would normally kill the novelty (Safe Robot Foundation Models 2503.07404 = hard projection over a learned policy; ContactRL 2512.03707 = QP shield over learned RL; GraspSense 2604.05697 / DOBCBF 2411.07833 = fragile force ceilings) and correctly argues each misses the *exact* conjunction. But the framing "the generic hard-filter claim is now settled, the unfilled slot is the fragile gentle-force ceiling over a learned policy" is a four-way intersection, and intersections are weak novelty: each axis is individually occupied.
Strongest supporting evidence: 2503.07404 shows zero-violation hard *action-projection over a pre-trained VLA* with SR maintained/improved — the card concedes this is "the hard-projection-over-a-learned-policy cell," only missing the fragile-force ceiling. 2510.14959 (CBF-RL, Ames group, alphaxiv) gives a closed-form CBF-QP over a learned RL policy and explicitly frames the *runtime-filter vs reward-shaping* tradeoff the card builds H1 on — but argues the policy should *internalize* the filter and deploy *without* it, directly threatening the premise that a runtime hard projection is needed at all.
The one thing only this perspective says: The contribution is not a method, it is a *measurement protocol* — H1/H2 (per-step force-violation rate AND SR AND SoGraB integrity, on the same fragile task, projection vs penalty). Position the paper as "the head-to-head nobody ran," not "a new filter," because every component filter already exists.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks it is wrong or already solved).**
Core position: The bet is engineered down to a slot so narrow it risks being a tie. The falsifier the card itself wrote is lethal: "if a soft-penalty policy matches the hard projection on *both* SR and force-violation rate at matched fragile-object integrity, the hard projection buys nothing." On fragile objects, a well-tuned soft stress penalty (Stress-Guided RL: 41.9%→10.0% water loss, zero-shot tofu) may already drive violations low enough that the *measured* gap vanishes — leaving only the theoretical "per-step guarantee" with no empirical daylight.
Strongest supporting evidence: ContactRL already reports 0.20% violation + 87.17% SR over a learned policy with a hard shield, and GraspSense already reports *zero* violations + 8x–12x admissible force on glass/paper/plastic cups — model-based, but on the exact fragile-cup objects D4 targets. So the "zero-violation fragile ceiling" half of the bet is demonstrably achievable; the only fresh claim is "over a *learned* policy AND beating a penalty at matched SR," which is a tie-prone comparison.
The one thing only this perspective says: The card hides that the soft penalty may be *good enough*. The honest framing is: a hard projection's value is the *guarantee*, not a number on a benchmark — and a reviewer can reject "guarantees matter" without new evidence. Pre-register the matched-SR operating point and show a regime (high task-force tasks, where the penalty must trade SR for safety) where the penalty *provably* cannot match. If no such regime exists, drop to a workshop paper.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
Core position: "Hard safety guarantee" is a fundable, citable, industry-friendly narrative (regulators, insurers, fragile-goods logistics, food handling all want it), which is exactly why the slot is crowded — Stanford+TRI (2605.21811), Caltech/Ames (2510.14959, 2411.07833), TU Darmstadt/Peters (2503.07404), ByteDance (2601.02778) are all racing the same "safe contact" framing. A solo bet on the *intersection* competes against labs with hardware, tactile-skin IP, and sim infrastructure you do not control.
Strongest supporting evidence: The anchor set is dominated by well-resourced groups with custom sensing (DexSkin's interpretable skin, DOBCBF's EM tactile, Tabero's GelSight VLA). The cheap, defensible asset here is the *benchmark/protocol* (SoGraB's gripper-agnostic Density-Aware Chamfer metric, 0.517→0.940) — protocols get cited across all the competing method papers regardless of who wins the method race.
The one thing only this perspective says: Compete on the axis where you are not outgunned — own the *evaluation*. A standardized "fragile-force-violation-rate vs SR vs integrity" leaderboard (extending SoGraB + Tabero's per-step protocol) is a lower-capex, higher-citation bet than racing TRI/Ames/ByteDance on the filter itself.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
Core position: This is the classic *hard-constraint-vs-soft-cost* pendulum that has swung in every robot-learning subfield — safe RL (CPO/Lagrangian penalties) vs shielding/CBF-QP (Ames lineage), and earlier optimal-control hard constraints vs RL reward shaping. The recurring lesson: hard filters win the *guarantee* argument and lose the *flexibility/SR* argument, and the field settles on hybrids (filter during training, soften at deploy — exactly CBF-RL 2510.14959).
Strongest supporting evidence: The card's own H1 prediction ("hard projection drives violation to zero while *matching* penalty SR") is the same promise CBF shielding made in safe-RL benchmarks circa 2018–2021; the empirical history is that the hard filter usually costs a few points of SR (the card even lists this as a Risk: "QP clamping can hurt task SR"). Hierarchical RL-QP Grasp's own 81.4% comes *with* clamping-induced tracking errors.
The one thing only this perspective says: The interesting, durable result is not "hard beats soft" (the pendulum has swung both ways) but *where the crossover is* — characterize the fragility/required-force regime in which the guarantee is worth the SR cost. That regime map outlives whichever filter is fashionable, the way "when does model-based beat model-free" outlived individual Dreamer-vs-PPO bake-offs.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

(1) Direct clashes:
- **Skeptic vs the card's own thesis (the tie risk):** Skeptic says Stress-Guided RL's soft penalty may already drive fragile-object violations low enough that H1's measured gap vanishes (the card's own falsifier fires); the card asserts a per-step *guarantee* is categorically different from an expected-reward penalty. Specific claim in tension: does the *measured* fragile-object violation-rate gap exist at matched SR, or only the theoretical one?
- **Practitioner vs Academic:** Practitioner says the binding constraint is per-fingertip force *observability* at control rate (so the explicit-kPa-QP collapses into ContactRL's energy proxy on real hardware); Academic treats the contribution as a measurement protocol runnable in sim where ground-truth stress exists. Claim in tension: is D4 a real-robot result or a sim protocol?
- **Historian/CBF-RL vs the runtime-filter premise:** CBF-RL (2510.14959) argues the safe behavior should be *internalized during training and deployed without* a runtime filter; D4's bet presumes a runtime hard projection is the thing to ship. Claim in tension: is a deployment-time projection even necessary, or does training-time shielding subsume it?

(2) Strongest vs weakest perspective: **Strongest = Academic** — its read is directly checkable against the card's cited papers and the alphaxiv hits, and it correctly reframes the only survivable contribution (the protocol, not the filter). **Weakest = Historian** — directionally right about the pendulum but offers analogy over a falsifiable prediction for *this* fragile-force setting; its "characterize the crossover" advice is sound but non-specific.

(3) The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction: **On the SAME fragile task at MATCHED success rate, is there a measurable force-violation-rate (and SoGraB-integrity) gap between a hard force-magnitude projection over a learned policy and a tuned soft stress-penalty policy — and if so, in which required-force regime does it appear?** This is H1/H2 run honestly; its answer decides COMMIT vs DROP.

(4) What every perspective agrees on (likely true): Hard CBF-QP / projection filters over learned policies *work* and are now a mature, consensus tool (2605.21811, 2503.07404, 2512.03707, 2510.14959 all demonstrate zero/near-zero violations) — so the *generic* "hard beats soft for safety" claim is settled and not the contribution. The fragile gentle-force *ceiling* (force-magnitude upper bound, opposite of force-closure) has been enforced only model-side (DOBCBF, GraspSense) or softly (Stress-Guided RL, DexSkin), never as a hard projection over a learned policy with a head-to-head vs the penalty.

(5) Blind spot none addressed: **Per-object force-tolerance acquisition.** Every method assumes the kPa/N ceiling is *given* (1.53 kPa for berries, ~10 N elsewhere). Where does the tolerance come from at test time for a novel fragile object? The card's own Risk note ("force tolerances are object-specific... make the bound a per-object parameter") flags it but no perspective and no cited method *closes* it — GraspSense estimates admissible force from wall-thickness geometry, which is the nearest thing, but it is model-based. A wrong ceiling makes the "guarantee" vacuous; this is the unaddressed load-bearing assumption.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

(1) CEO summary: D4 is a sharply-defined, unusually self-aware bet that has been narrowed to a four-way intersection — (force-magnitude ceiling) × (over a learned policy) × (fragile object) × (head-to-head vs a soft penalty at matched SR) — where every individual axis is already occupied by strong work, but the exact conjunction is genuinely unrun. That makes the novelty real but thin and tie-prone: the card's own falsifier (a tuned soft penalty matching the hard projection on both SR and violation rate) is a live outcome on fragile objects, where soft penalties (Stress-Guided RL) already work well. The durable, fundable contribution is not "hard beats soft" (a settled pendulum) but the *measurement protocol* and the *crossover regime* — where the per-step guarantee is empirically worth its SR cost — plus solving the unaddressed problem of where the per-object force tolerance comes from.

(2) Five key findings, ranked by reliability:
- F1 (most reliable): The *generic* hard-filter-over-learned-policy claim is settled, not novel. Supported by Academic, Historian, Skeptic, Economist; the card itself concedes it. Challenged by none. Evidence: 2605.21811, 2503.07404, 2512.03707, 2510.14959.
- F2: The surviving novelty is the exact conjunction (fragile force-magnitude ceiling, over a learned policy, vs soft penalty at matched SR), and it is currently unrun. Supported by Academic, the card. Challenged by Skeptic (calls it tie-prone), Economist (calls it crowded). Evidence: no cited paper or alphaxiv hit lands all four axes; DOBCBF/GraspSense are model-based, ContactRL is energy-proxy + human-handover, 2503.07404 is generic collision.
- F3: The bet is tie-prone — the card's own falsifier may fire because soft penalties already drive fragile violations low. Supported by Skeptic, Historian, Practitioner. Challenged by the card's first-principles "guarantee ≠ expectation" argument. Evidence: Stress-Guided RL 41.9%→10.0% water loss; ContactRL 0.20% violation; GraspSense zero violations.
- F4: Force observability at control rate is the real binding constraint on real hardware; the explicit-kPa QP risks collapsing into an energy/proxy ceiling. Supported by Practitioner. Challenged (scoped away) by Academic (run it in sim). Evidence: DexSkin's custom skin, DOBCBF's EM tactile + contact model, ContactRL's energy proxy.
- F5 (least settled, highest leverage): Per-object force-tolerance acquisition is unsolved and makes the guarantee vacuous if wrong. Supported by the card's Risk note; no method closes it. Challenged by none (it is a blind spot, not a contested claim). Evidence: GraspSense's geometry-from-wall-thickness is the only partial answer, and it is model-based.

(3) Hidden cross-perspective connection: Practitioner ("you cannot measure per-fingertip force"), Economist ("compete on the protocol/sensing you control"), and the F5 blind spot ("where does the tolerance come from") are the *same* problem wearing three hats — the entire bet rests on a force quantity (both the *measured* contact force and the *per-object ceiling*) that is hard to observe/specify. Whoever owns reliable per-object force *grounding* (estimation + tolerance) owns D4, regardless of whether the filter is hard or soft. This connects D4 directly to E1/E2 (force-awareness without a runtime sensor, cross-sensor encoder) — the substrate, not the filter, is the moat.

(4) Actionable insight for the researcher: **DE-RISK, do not COMMIT yet.** Concretely: (a) Run H1/H2 first in sim, in Stress-Guided RL's exact deformable setting (ground-truth particle stress = clean force-violation oracle), as a pre-registered matched-SR head-to-head: hard force-magnitude projection over the learned policy vs the tuned soft stress-penalty, reporting violation-rate AND SR AND SoGraB Density-Aware Chamfer integrity. (b) The go/no-go gate is F3: if the soft penalty ties on all three at matched SR, pivot — DROP the "hard beats soft" framing and instead ship the *protocol + crossover regime map* (Economist's lower-capex bet) and the *per-object tolerance acquisition* method (F5, the blind spot), which are both defensible and uncrowded. (c) Only escalate to the real 23-DoF hand once the sim head-to-head shows a regime with a real gap, and treat the real-robot force estimate as an explicit E1/E2 dependency, not an assumption.

(5) Frontier question: Can a learned policy *internalize* a fragile force ceiling during training (CBF-RL-style shielding) such that it deploys with zero runtime filter AND zero fragile-object violations AND no SR loss — collapsing the hard-vs-soft distinction the card is built on? If yes, D4's runtime-projection premise is obsolete before it ships; if no (the ceiling provably cannot be internalized and must be enforced online), that impossibility result is itself the strongest version of the paper.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical robotics faculty. Grounded against `Capability/Manipulation.md` L1065–1143 and KH notes for 2503.07404, 2512.03707, 2510.25405, 2411.07833, 2604.05697.*

The briefing is unusually honest about its own thinness, which is to its credit and also the source of its problem: a card that pre-concedes its falsifier may fire is one revision away from conceding it has no paper. I largely agree with the synthesis but think it is too kind to F2 and too quick to treat F1 as fully closed without naming the one cell that is genuinely empty.

---

##### 1. Confidence scores (1–10, reliability)

| Finding | Score | One-line justification |
|---|---|---|
| **F1** — generic hard-filter-over-learned-policy is settled, not novel | **9** | Directly verifiable: 2503.07404's KH note confirms hard ATACOM action-projection over a pre-trained VLA with zero violations + maintained SR. The card concedes it and the Survey 2512.11908 catalogs it as mature. Near-fact, not opinion. |
| **F2** — surviving novelty is the exact four-way conjunction, unrun | **6** | The conjunction is plausibly unrun, but "unrun" is an absence-of-evidence claim resting on a non-exhaustive cited set, and the four axes are so narrow that the contribution may be a tie or a non-result rather than novelty. Reliability is capped by the unprovable negative. |
| **F3** — bet is tie-prone; the card's own falsifier may fire | **8** | Strongly supported by the KH data the card itself cites: Stress-Guided RL drives water loss 41.9%→10.0%, GraspSense reports zero violations, ContactRL 0.20%. When the soft baselines already sit near the floor, the headroom for a hard filter to *measurably* win is thin. The "guarantee≠expectation" rebuttal is real but does not produce a number a reviewer can plot. |
| **F4** — force observability at control rate is the real binding constraint; explicit-kPa QP risks collapsing into an energy proxy | **8** | ContactRL's KH note confirms the proxy collapse concretely (eCBF bounds *kinetic energy*, "implicitly" max force, not explicit N/kPa). DexSkin needed custom skin; DOBCBF needs a contact model. The "scoped to sim, ground-truth stress oracle" rebuttal is valid but is exactly the move that makes the sim win non-transferable. |
| **F5** — per-object force-tolerance acquisition is unsolved; makes the guarantee vacuous if wrong | **7** | The logic is airtight (a hard ceiling at the wrong threshold guarantees the wrong thing) and GraspSense's wall-thickness force-map is correctly identified as the only partial model-based answer. Docked because "unsolved" is again a literature-completeness claim, and the Risk note's "per-object parameter coupled to A1/A3" is a partial, in-card mitigation the finding under-credits. |

**Mean = 7.6, Min = 6 (F2).**

---

##### 2. Weakest link

**F2 ("the exact conjunction is unrun").** It is the load-bearing claim — it is what converts F1's "settled" into "but a slot remains" — and it is the least falsifiable, because it is a universal negative over the literature ("no paper lands all four axes"). The briefing's own evidence shows how close the walls already are: 2503.07404 is hard-projection-over-a-learned-policy (axes 1–2), DOBCBF/GraspSense are hard-or-model-based force *ceilings* on fragile objects (axes 1, 3), ContactRL is a hard shield over a *learned* policy bounding max force (axes 1, 2, 3 — failing only "explicit magnitude" and "fragile dexterous" and "head-to-head vs soft"). The conjunction is unrun only because of the *fourth* axis (a matched-SR head-to-head against a tuned soft penalty), which is a **protocol gap, not a method gap** — and a protocol gap is much weaker novelty than the card's framing implies.

*To verify:* (a) a structured search for any deformable/fragile RL paper that reports a matched-SR hard-vs-soft ablation on the *same* force-violation metric — the negative must be demonstrated, not assumed; (b) confirmation that ContactRL's energy-shield, re-tuned for an explicit force bound on a fragile object, would *not* already constitute the H1 experiment (if it would, F2 collapses to "re-run ContactRL on a berry"). The single fact that would most move F2: whether any of Stress-Guided RL, GraspSense, or DOBCBF reports an apples-to-apples SR-matched comparison against an alternative enforcement mechanism. If even one does, the "unrun" claim is wrong.

---

##### 3. Bias check

**The Skeptic over-dominated, with the Economist as enabler.** Four of five findings (F1 settled, F2 tie-prone-and-crowded, F3 falsifier-fires, F5 blind-spot-vacuous) are framed as reasons the bet is weak, and the actionable insight is "de-risk, do not commit" with a pre-built pivot. That is the correct *posture* for a thin bet, but it means the synthesis never seriously steel-mans the strongest pro-D4 case: that a **provable per-step zero-violation guarantee** is categorically more valuable than any expectation-bound penalty in a safety-certification or deployment-liability context, *independent of whether the violation-rate plot ties* — because a regulator or a fleet operator buys the guarantee, not the mean. The card states this ("guarantee≠expectation") but the synthesis files it under "challenged by the card" and moves on. The Academic perspective, which would have pressed the formal CBF-feasibility question (does a per-step force-magnitude projection even remain feasible without inducing grasp slip?), is the quietest voice and appears mainly to scope F4 to sim. So: **not balanced — tilted skeptical/economist, under-weighting the formal-guarantee Academic case.**

---

##### 4. Missing perspective

**The Certifier / Safety-Standards angle (a regulatory-and-liability economist, distinct from the cost economist).** The entire hard-vs-soft debate changes character the moment the buyer is a certification regime rather than a benchmark leaderboard. Under that lens:

- F3's falsifier becomes **non-fatal**: a soft penalty that *ties* on empirical violation rate still cannot be *certified*, because certification requires a per-step worst-case bound, which only the hard projection provides. The "tie buys nothing" conclusion is a benchmark-frame artifact; in a certification frame the hard filter wins by construction even at parity.
- F5 becomes **the central deliverable, not a blind spot**: the per-object tolerance is exactly the *specification* a standard would have to fix, so "where the tolerance comes from" is reframed from a risk into the contribution's primary intellectual product.
- The frontier question's CBF-RL internalization ("zero runtime filter") becomes *less* attractive, not more, because an internalized ceiling is unauditable — you cannot point a certifier at a reward term.

This perspective would flip the actionable insight from "pivot if it ties" to "the tie is the paper": *hard and soft are empirically equivalent on the floor, therefore the contribution is the certifiable per-step guarantee plus the tolerance-specification protocol.* That is a stronger, more defensible thesis than the current "hard beats soft," and the synthesis does not see it because no perspective in the panel is buying safety rather than success rate.

---

##### 5. Overall grade + top fixes

**Grade: A−.**

This is a genuinely good briefing — self-aware, correctly grounded against the KH notes (I spot-checked 2503.07404, 2512.03707, 2510.25405 and the characterizations hold), and it ranks its own findings by reliability honestly. It loses the plus because (i) it lets its strongest finding be a tie-prediction without resolving whether a tie is actually fatal, and (ii) it under-examines the seam between "novelty" (F2) and "protocol gap" — the place where a reviewer would push hardest.

Top fixes, in priority order:

1. **Resolve the frame question before the experiment.** Decide explicitly whether D4 is a *benchmark* contribution (where F3's tie kills it) or a *certification/guarantee* contribution (where the tie is the result). The current de-risk plan hedges; a thesis cannot. The missing Certifier perspective (§4) makes the second framing defensible and converts the falsifier into a feature.
2. **Downgrade F2 from "novel" to "protocol-novel" and own it.** The four-way conjunction is unrun primarily because the *head-to-head protocol* has not been run, not because the *mechanism* is missing (2503.07404 + ContactRL + DOBCBF jointly cover three of four axes). Stating this plainly makes the contribution honest and, paradoxically, more fundable — "we built the measurement protocol the field skips" is a cleaner sell than "we found an empty cell."
3. **Promote F5 to a co-equal hypothesis, not a Risk bullet.** Per-object tolerance acquisition is the only fully-uncrowded sub-problem the briefing identifies (the synthesis's own §3 connection says as much). It deserves an H6, not a footnote in the Risks callout.
4. **Fix one numeric-provenance wobble.** The card cites Stress-Guided RL's "36.5% stress cut," but the KH note records mean stress 1747 vs 2584 Pa (≈32%) and water loss 41.9%→10.0%. Reconcile the number to the source before it ships, since the whole H1/H2 bet is calibrated against this baseline.
5. **Make the sim-oracle dependency explicit in the go/no-go.** The de-risk plan's clean-violation-oracle (ground-truth particle stress) is exactly the quantity F4 says vanishes on hardware. State that a sim win is necessary-but-not-sufficient and that the real binding test is the E1/E2 force-grounding dependency — which the actionable insight already gestures at but should hard-gate on.

---

### E (Cluster review)

Cluster "E — Tactile Foundations & Data Substrates" (E1 Sensor-Free Force-Aware Policies + E2 Cross-Sensor Tactile Foundation Models): is the grouping carved at the right joint, and is the shared bet ("contact-rich, multi-modal data scarcity is the substrate A-D consume tactile *from*; force-awareness can be deployed with no runtime tactile sensor and made portable across the sensor ecosystem") sound enough to COMMIT?

The cluster's own framing: shared bottleneck = "Contact-rich, multi-modal data scarcity (4-order gap vs OXE) — the substrate A-D consume tactile *from*"; intro = "The foundation layer beneath force-aware manipulation that needs no runtime tactile hardware — getting force-awareness to deployment with the sensor dropped, and a cross-sensor representation that makes any such policy portable across the sensor ecosystem."

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys robot policies daily).**
- *Core position*: The cluster's deploy-side promise (drop the runtime sensor) is the genuinely valuable half, because tactile hardware on a fielded robot is the part that breaks: GelSight gel tears, wiring snaps, calibration drifts. E1 Route-2 already delivers this and FD-VLA's 61.1% > raw-sensor 38.9% is the result a practitioner actually wants. But E1 Route-1 (the surviving novelty: ~20k-hr ego-video-only force objective) is a research-lab bet, not a deploy bet — nobody ships a 20,854-hr pretraining run.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: FD-VLA (2602.02142) 61.1% sensor-free beating its own with-sensor input 38.9%; HapticVLA (2603.15257) 86.7% sensor-free, +45 pp on the egg; TA-VLA (2509.07962) charger 0/20→17/20 from motor-current torque with zero sensor. These are the rows a deployment engineer trusts because they need no new hardware.
- *Only this perspective says*: The card targets "≥80% of ForceVLA's 5 tasks" but a deployed contact policy's real failure mode is the long tail of slip the card itself flags as the "vision-to-tactile noise floor" — the 23% of tasks where fingertip pressure is load-bearing is exactly the 23% you bought the sensor for. Route-1 cannot be a *replacement*, only a *prior*; commit to it as pretraining, never as the deployed stack.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
- *Core position*: The cluster is honest that Route-2 is "settled" and bets the paper on Route-1, which is the right scholarly move — but the literature it cites already contains a force-objective-from-human-video result the card does NOT list (Feel-the-Force / FTF, 2506.01944, Abbeel/Pinto), whose Appendix Table-3 force-masked variant predicts desired force from vision+state alone "with no degradation." The bet's exact mechanism is partially demonstrated; the unclaimed delta is narrower than "no paper yet."
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: FEEL (2603.15847) establishes the precondition the bet hinges on — force IS predictable from ego video (95.6% Binary-Contact, +4.85% verb / +6.26% noun frozen on EPIC-Kitchens). EgoScale (2602.16710) supplies the log-linear curve and +54% on 22-DoF. But FTF (2506.01944) 77% across 5 force-sensitive tasks, zero robot-specific data, already shows force-prediction-then-PD-reproduction transfers human→robot — the card's Route-1 thesis in embryo.
- *Only this perspective says*: The literature contradicts the card's "every ego backbone omits the force head" framing: FTF *and* FEEL both carry a force objective from human data; what is genuinely un-done is the *scale* (FTF is 30 demos / fixed-camera / a force glove, not 20k hr ego-video-only). Reframe the novelty from "first force objective from ego video" to "first force objective at EgoScale scale with NO instrumented signal at any stage" — FTF's glove is the instrumented signal the strict Route-1 claim forbids.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks the bet is wrong or already solved).**
- *Core position*: The two directions do not share a bottleneck — they share a *vibe* ("tactile is hard to get"). E1 is about *dropping* the sensor at runtime; E2 is about making a representation *portable across* sensors that are present. These are opposite problems (no-sensor vs many-sensors) and stapling them into one cluster hides that E1's surviving novelty is thin and E2's is a measurement protocol, not a method.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The card itself concedes E1 Route-2 is "settled" (FD-VLA, ViTacGen 86%, HapticVLA 86.7%) and E2's core ("can it transfer?") is "settled" by UniForce (2602.01153, z5↔Fz r=−0.74, wiping 20%→80% zero-shot cross-type). FTP-1 (2606.13102) already reports +31.6% on *two unseen* sensors across 21 training sensors — the cross-sensor held-out *policy*-SR number E2 claims is missing. E2's residual ("a strict N−1 *encoder-held-out* sweep vs sensor count") is one ablation table away from FTP-1, not a paper.
- *Only this perspective says*: The whitespace in BOTH directions has collapsed to a *protocol/scale* claim, not a *capability* claim. That is publishable as a benchmark paper but it is a weak publication bet — a reviewer who knows FTF (E1) and FTP-1 (E2) will ask "what's new besides the axis you swept?" The card's defense ("FTP-1 fixes diversity, never sweeps N") is true but is exactly the kind of gap that gets pre-empted in 3 months by the FTP-1 authors adding one figure.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
- *Core position*: This cluster is shaped by a hardware-cost asymmetry: tactile sensors are a vendor margin and a fragility liability, so "drop the sensor" has industrial pull (NVIDIA/Berkeley behind EgoScale; the ego-video corpora are scaling-law plays). The incentive gradient pushes everyone toward Route-2 deploy wins (cheap, shippable) and away from the expensive 20k-hr Route-1 the card bets on — which is why Route-1 is "unattacked": not because it's a secret, because it's expensive and the cheap route already pays.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: EgoScale's 20,854-hr corpus and log-linear curve is a scaling-law artifact only a well-funded lab runs; HumanNet (2605.06747) "1,000 hr ego ≈ 100 hr real-robot" reframes ego-video as a cost-arbitrage substrate. The benchmark-chasing tell: the bet is pinned to ForceVLA's 5 tasks + EgoVLA's Ego Humanoid Benchmark + ManiFeel — all three are *standardized substrates* that exist *because* the field is benchmark-saturating, which inflates "the testbed now exists" into "the bet is timely."
- *Only this perspective says*: The 20k-hr Route-1 requirement is a moat that favors NVIDIA-scale labs; an academic committing here is racing on the one axis (compute/data scale) where they are structurally disadvantaged. De-risk by owning the *protocol* (E2's N−1 retention law, ManiFeel-cheap) rather than the *scale* (E1's 20k-hr run), because the protocol is where a small lab can plant a flag a big lab will cite.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: "Drop the expensive modality at runtime, keep it as a training teacher" is the privileged-information / asymmetric-actor-critic pattern (Dreamer-era, depth-as-privileged, sim-privileged-state distillation) replayed on touch — and that pattern historically *works* for the in-distribution case and *fails* on the exact tail the privileged signal was protecting. E1 is reliving the pixels-vs-latent and privileged-distillation cycles; the card's own Risks section has already discovered the historical lesson (bound to vision-correlated force).
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: PTLD (2603.04531) privileged-oracle→real-pair estimator, +182% rotation — textbook privileged distillation. Force-Based Sim2Real (2601.02778) 25.1 vs 1.1 in-hand rotations is the sim-privileged-teacher lineage. The cross-sensor E2 story rhymes with the "universal encoder" wave (one DINO/CLIP to rule them all): T3 (2406.13640) +24% across 13 sensors, then UniForce's "DINOv2-for-touch" — every modality eventually gets its foundation-model land-grab, and they plateau at a representation, not a policy.
- *Only this perspective says*: Every prior "drop the sensor / unify the sensors" wave ended the same way — the distilled/unified version owns the easy 80% and the instrumented version is retained for the safety-critical 20%, and the field quietly stops claiming replacement. Expect E1's honest landing to be "Route-1 is a strong *prior* that closes most of the gap," not "Route-1 replaces tactile"; write the paper to claim the prior, not the replacement, and it ages well.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

**(1) Where 2+ perspectives directly clash.**
- *Novelty alive vs collapsed*: ACADEMIC says the Route-1 mechanism is *partially demonstrated already* (FTF 2506.01944 force-masked variant, FEEL force-from-video) so the unclaimed delta is "scale, not capability"; the card's framing ("no paper yet trains a force-objective policy from ego video alone") treats it as fully open. SKEPTIC pushes further: both directions' whitespace has collapsed to a protocol/scale claim. These directly contradict the card's "genuinely unattacked half."
- *Cluster coherence*: SKEPTIC says E1 (no-sensor) and E2 (many-sensors) are *opposite* problems wrongly stapled; the card asserts a shared bottleneck (data scarcity) and a synergy ("E1's policy is what E2 makes portable; E2 is what D2's estimator inherits"). PRACTITIONER tacitly sides with the card's deploy-value but only for Route-2, not the bet.
- *Where to plant the flag*: ECONOMIST says own E2's cheap N−1 *protocol*; the card pins the headline bet on E1's expensive 20k-hr Route-1. Direct clash on which half is the real publication bet.

**(2) Strongest vs weakest perspective.**
- *Strongest*: ACADEMIC — it is the only one that names a specific, real, uncited paper (FTF 2506.01944) whose force-masked ablation directly tests the bet's mechanism, turning a vibe-level objection into a falsifiable narrowing of the novelty claim. Evidence-bound and actionable.
- *Weakest*: HISTORIAN — correct and wise, but its conclusion ("claim the prior not the replacement") is already pre-empted by the card's own Risks section (which bounds the claim to vision-correlated force). It adds framing, not a new fact.

**(3) The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction.**
Does FTF (2506.01944) — force-objective learned from human demos, force-masked variant predicting desired force from vision+state with "no degradation" — already constitute the Route-1 claim, or does it fail the strict "no force/tactile signal at ANY stage" bar because its training force comes from a worn tactile glove? Answering this decides whether E1's novelty is "first ever" (card's framing) or "first at EgoScale scale with zero instrumented signal" (the survivable, narrower claim).

**(4) What every perspective agrees on (likely true).**
Route-2 (distill a tactile teacher, drop the sensor at runtime) is settled and works for in-distribution contact — FD-VLA 61.1% > with-sensor 38.9%, HapticVLA 86.7%, ViTacGen 86% are not contested by anyone. The deploy-side value of dropping the runtime sensor is real and already banked; the open question is purely on the *training-side* signal (Route-1's "no teacher at all") and the *measurement protocol* (E2's N−1 sweep). All five also agree the claim must be *bounded to vision-correlated force* — the slip tail needs the sensor.

**(5) The blind spot (none addressed).**
No perspective addressed *safety/force-bound certification*: a sensor-free force policy has no runtime force measurement, so it cannot *bound* the force it applies — it can only predict it open-loop. For the cluster's own downstream consumers (Cluster D4's QP/force-safety, contact-rich assembly), "we dropped the sensor" silently drops the only signal that could enforce a force ceiling on a fragile object. The card reports SR on eggs but never asks: when Route-1 is wrong about the force, what breaks, and can you even detect it without the sensor you removed?

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**(1) CEO summary.** Cluster E is two adjacent-but-distinct bets bundled under one honest bottleneck (contact data scarcity). The deploy-side half (drop the runtime tactile sensor) is genuinely settled and valuable — FD-VLA, HapticVLA, ViTacGen, FTP-1 are real, replicated wins — which the card forthrightly concedes. The live novelty is correspondingly narrow: E1 bets on a force *objective* learned from ego-video *alone* with no instrumented signal at any stage, and E2 bets on a leave-one-sensor-out *policy-SR retention scaling law*. Both survive a literature check, but only as *protocol/scale* claims, not capability claims — and one uncited paper (Feel-the-Force, 2506.01944) already demonstrates the Route-1 *mechanism* at small scale with a human force glove, so the defensible E1 claim must be re-scoped to "at EgoScale scale, zero instrumented signal," and the strongest, cheapest flag to plant is E2's N−1 retention protocol, which a small lab can own before the FTP-1 authors add one figure.

**(2) Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *Route-2 (drop runtime sensor) is settled and works in-distribution.* Most reliable — every perspective agrees; evidence FD-VLA 61.1%>38.9%, HapticVLA 86.7%, ViTacGen 86%. Supported by all; challenged by none. Implication: do NOT bet the paper on Route-2.
2. *E1's surviving novelty is partially pre-empted in mechanism, intact in scale.* High reliability — ACADEMIC names FTF (2506.01944) force-masked variant + FEEL force-from-video as the precondition already met. Supported by ACADEMIC/SKEPTIC; the card's "no paper yet" framing challenges it. Implication: re-scope to "20k-hr ego-video-only, no instrumented signal at any stage."
3. *E2's whitespace has collapsed to a measurement protocol (N−1 encoder-held-out sweep).* High reliability — SKEPTIC + the card itself: UniForce settles transfer, FTP-1 (+31.6% on 2 unseen sensors) settles cross-sensor policy-SR; only the *strict N−1 vs sensor-count curve* remains. Supported by SKEPTIC/ECONOMIST; the card frames it as a "scaling law" worth a paper. Implication: cheapest, most ownable flag for a small lab.
4. *The cluster bundles two opposite problems (no-sensor vs many-sensors).* Medium reliability — SKEPTIC's structural objection; card defends with a D2-inheritance synergy. Supported by SKEPTIC; challenged by the card's synergy argument and PRACTITIONER's deploy framing. Implication: present E1 and E2 as two papers, not one thesis.
5. *Sensor-free force policy cannot bound applied force at runtime (safety blind spot).* Medium-low reliability (un-evidenced because un-studied) — raised by none of the five as their core, surfaced as the universal blind spot. Implication: a force-safety failure-mode analysis is a free differentiator and a real contribution.

**(3) Hidden cross-perspective connection.** ECONOMIST (the 20k-hr moat favors NVIDIA-scale labs) and ACADEMIC (FTF already shows the mechanism at 30-demo scale) point at the same move from opposite ends: the *capability* is cheap and partly demonstrated, only the *scale* is expensive — so an academic who bets on the expensive scale axis (E1 Route-1 at 20k hr) is racing a big lab on the big lab's axis, while the *mechanism question FTF leaves open* (does it hold without ANY instrumented signal, and how far does it scale) is answerable cheaply and is exactly where E2's protocol-ownership instinct also points. Both perspectives independently say: own the protocol/mechanism question, not the compute.

**(4) Actionable insight for the researcher — DE-RISK (do not commit as-framed; do not drop).** The cluster is worth a publication bet but NOT the one currently headlined. Concretely: (a) **Split** — publish E2's N−1 encoder-held-out policy-SR retention sweep as the primary, ownable paper (cheap on ManiFeel/roto-2.0, plants the flag SKEPTIC says FTP-1 authors will otherwise take); (b) **Re-scope E1** from "first force objective from ego video" to "first force objective at EgoScale scale with zero instrumented signal at any stage," explicitly distinguishing FTF (2506.01944, uses a human force glove) and FEEL (uses a force glove) as the instrumented-teacher precedents the strict claim excludes; (c) **De-risk concrete next experiment**: run the FTF force-masked variant *against* a Route-1 ego-video-only force-objective policy on ManiFeel's with-tactile-vs-without ablation (the card's own H1 test), and *add the safety blind-spot read-out* — report, per task, the applied-force overshoot distribution when the policy is wrong, since that is the metric that separates "strong prior" (publishable, ages well) from "replacement" (overclaim, ages badly). Go/no-go: if the ego-video-only policy cannot clear ~80% of instrumented SR on the vision-correlated subset, Route-1 is a prior not a method — pivot fully to E2.

**(5) Frontier question that would change everything.** Is there a *runtime* signal — derivable without a tactile sensor (motor current, joint-torque residual, vision-predicted contact, as in TA-VLA / FACTR-2 / Adaptive Vision-Torque Fusion) — rich enough to *certify* an applied-force bound, not just *predict* a force? If yes, "sensor-free" stops meaning "force-blind at runtime" and the entire cluster's deploy value (and its safety story) changes: the bet shifts from "predict force well enough" to "bound force tightly enough without the sensor," which is a harder, more defensible, and currently unclaimed thesis that unifies E1 and E2 under a single real bottleneck.

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics faculty. Mandate is to find the soft claims, not to rubber-stamp. Grounded against the card (Manipulation.md L1144-1318), the two sibling analyst parts (017-E-1, 018-E1-1), and the KH notes for the load-bearing papers. The alphaxiv MCP is documented-degraded across all prior audits, so independent re-verification of out-of-vault papers (FTF 2506.01944) was not possible from this seat; I flag where my confidence therefore inherits from an upstream pass rather than a first-hand read.*

---

**1. CONFIDENCE SCORES (1-10 reliability)**

1. **Route-2 (drop runtime sensor) is settled and works in-distribution — 9/10.** Every number traces cleanly to a KH note I read first-hand: FD-VLA 61.1% mean and the 38.9% raw-force comparator (2602.02142 L40-42), HapticVLA 86.7% (2603.15257 L40), ViTacGen 80-86% (2510.14117 L41). Five distinct papers agree, none dissent. The one wording caveat that keeps this off a 10: "FD-VLA *beats the with-sensor baseline*" overstates — 38.9% is *raw-force-input to the same VLA*, an ablation arm, not a deployed instrumented policy; the honest claim is "a distilled force token beats raw force input," which is still decisive for the sensor-drop thesis. Capability is settled; the *framing* is one notch hot.

2. **E1's novelty is pre-empted in mechanism, intact in scale — 7/10.** The structural claim is sound and is the single most valuable finding in the briefing. FTF (2506.01944) is genuinely uncited in the card and absent from KH, and its force-masking ablation (force inferred from vision+state with "no degradation") directly tests E1's mechanism. I dock three points because the whole finding rests on a paper I cannot read from this seat: the only evidence it says what is claimed is sibling-part 018's note that the ablation was "confirmed via alphaxiv get_paper_content" in an upstream pass. That is a single, unreproducible source for a load-bearing pre-emption. If FTF's masked variant is fragile-deformable-only (lift-and-hold-bread is the one example cited), the pre-emption may not extend to the vision-uncorrelated-slip regime E1 actually claims — in which case the novelty is *less* collapsed than the briefing says. The re-scope ("EgoScale scale, zero instrumented signal") is the right move regardless.

3. **E2's whitespace has collapsed to an N−1 measurement protocol — 6/10.** The structural logic is correct (existence is settled by UniForce; FTP-1 supplies the first cross-sensor held-out *policy*-SR number at +31.6%, verified in KH 2606.13102 L39; only the strict encoder-held-out-vs-N curve remains). But the finding's quantitative spine has a hole I verified directly: the headline "UniForce 90-120% retention" appears **nowhere in the vault** — not in UniForce's KH note (2602.01153), not anywhere under `_KnowledgeHub_/`. UniForce's actual reported numbers are r=-0.74 / 55.2% variance and wiping SR 20%→80%, where 80% requires **bilateral** tactile (single-sensor is 50-60%). "Retention" is not even a metric UniForce reports. So the card's "current: UniForce 90-120% retention" — repeated verbatim in the briefing as the bar E2 must beat — is an unsourced figure presented as a hard datapoint. The protocol gap is real; the number anchoring it is not traceable. That is exactly the soft claim a reviewer is paid to find.

4. **The cluster bundles two opposite problems — 7/10.** The structural objection is correct and well-posed: E1 (no sensor at any stage) and E2 (many sensors, made portable) genuinely point opposite directions, and the card's D2-inheritance synergy is a post-hoc tie, not a shared thesis. The "present as two papers" prescription is right. Held at 7 not higher only because this is a presentation/framing judgment, not a falsifiable claim — reasonable people could defend the umbrella under "contact-data scarcity" as a legitimate venue-level grouping.

5. **Sensor-free force policy cannot bound applied force at runtime (safety blind spot) — 5/10.** Identified as the most valuable *free* differentiator and correctly flagged as un-studied. The reliability is medium-low *by construction*: it is an absence-of-evidence claim, and the sibling 018 part (L50) raises the sharper, related point the briefing under-weights — the real deployment bar is *closed-loop* force regulation under disturbance, not open-loop force *bounding*. A truly sensor-free Route-1 policy has no runtime error signal to close any loop on. The briefing's frontier question gestures at this (motor-current / joint-torque residual as a runtime certificate) but does not resolve whether any of those proxies can actually *bound* rather than merely *predict* force. Honest, valuable, and appropriately tentative — but it is a hypothesis about a gap, not a verified gap.

Mean of the five: (9+7+6+7+5)/5 = **6.8**. Lowest: **5**.

---

**2. WEAKEST LINK**

Finding 3, specifically the **"UniForce 90-120% retention"** anchor. It is the quantitative floor the entire E2 bet is measured against ("beyond UniForce's 90-120%"), and I could not source it anywhere in the vault. To verify: pull UniForce (2602.01153) full text and locate the exact table/figure reporting a cross-sensor *retention* metric in the 90-120% band — if it is a force-estimation MAE-retention number it must not be conflated with *policy*-SR retention, which is the metric E2's whole bet is about; if no such number exists, the card's "current:" line should be corrected to UniForce's real figures (r=-0.74, wiping SR 20%→80% bilateral / 50-60% single-sensor) and the bet re-anchored to TaF-VLA's *sourced* 60.3% family-level ceiling, which is the only verified cross-sensor held-out number in the cluster. Until that figure is sourced or removed, the E2 target ("≥80% retention ... beyond UniForce's 90-120%") is internally incoherent: it asks the new protocol to *exceed 80%* while citing a prior at *90-120%*, which would mean the prior already clears the bar.

---

**3. BIAS CHECK**

**ACADEMIC over-dominated**, with SKEPTIC as a close second amplifier. The entire spine of the briefing — the FTF pre-emption, the "re-scope to EgoScale scale," the "protocol/scale not capability" framing, the hidden ECONOMIST-ACADEMIC connection — is ACADEMIC's literature-completeness move, and sibling-part 017 itself names ACADEMIC as "strongest." The cost of that dominance: the briefing inherits ACADEMIC's blind spot. ACADEMIC reasons about *what is claimable as novel*, so it frames the whole cluster as a novelty-accounting problem and converges on "own the protocol." It under-weights the PRACTITIONER/ENGINEER question of whether the N−1 sweep is even *runnable* cheaply — running a leave-one-sensor-out *policy*-SR sweep requires physical hardware for each held-out sensor (roto 2.0's 4 morphologies, ManiFeel's sim sensors), and "cheap on ManiFeel/roto-2.0" is asserted, not costed. ECONOMIST appears only as the 20k-hr-moat half of the hidden connection, never as a check on E2's own cost. The synthesis is *analytically* rich but *operationally* thin — classic ACADEMIC over-rotation.

---

**4. MISSING PERSPECTIVE (the 6th angle)**

**The closed-loop / temporal-control perspective** — call it the CONTROL THEORIST. Every result the cluster leans on (FEEL, FTF, FD-VLA, ForceVLA, UniForce) is a per-frame or short-horizon force *magnitude* claim. None addresses reactive force *regulation* over a horizon: re-grasp on detected slip, modulate grip during a 10-second insertion, recover from a disturbance. This changes the conclusions in two ways. (a) It reframes the safety finding (#5) from a "free add-on differentiator" into the *actual* central bottleneck: a sensor-free Route-1 policy has no runtime error signal, so it can do open-loop force *prediction* but not closed-loop force *control* — which is the real deployment bar for contact-rich tasks, and which the briefing's own frontier question circles without naming. (b) It re-ranks the bets: if closed-loop regulation is the prize, E2's cross-sensor portability is *more* defensible (a portable encoder feeds a runtime controller) and E1's open-loop ego-video objective is *less* (it has nothing to close the loop on without the very sensor it dropped). A control-theoretic reviewer would push the whole cluster from "predict force well enough" to "regulate force tightly enough without the sensor," exactly the harder/unclaimed thesis the briefing's frontier question gestures at — but as a *correction* to the headline bet, not a footnote.

---

**5. OVERALL GRADE: B+**

A strong, intellectually honest briefing that does the hardest thing well — it refuses to rubber-stamp its own card, correctly identifies that the deploy-side is settled, and lands one genuinely valuable, specific, falsifiable finding (the FTF pre-emption with a concrete re-scope). The DE-RISK actionable (split E2 out, re-scope E1, add the force-overshoot read-out) is the right call and is specific enough to execute. It loses the A-range on three things a Stanford committee would not let pass.

**Top fixes, in priority order:**
1. **Source or kill the "90-120% retention" figure.** It is the quantitative anchor of E2 and it is untraceable in the vault; as written it makes the E2 target self-contradictory (asks for >80% while citing a 90-120% prior). Replace with UniForce's real numbers and re-anchor the bar to the *sourced* TaF-VLA 60.3% ceiling. This is non-negotiable for a benchmark-paper pitch where the *number* is the contribution.
2. **Mark the FTF pre-emption as upstream-verified, not first-hand.** The single most important finding rests on one unreproducible alphaxiv pass on an out-of-vault paper. Before betting the re-scope on it, ingest 2506.01944 into KH and confirm the force-masking ablation generalizes beyond the one deformable-object example — if it is fragile-grasp-only, E1's vision-uncorrelated-slip novelty survives more intact than the briefing concedes.
3. **Cost the N−1 sweep and promote the closed-loop bar.** State the hardware/sim budget for a leave-one-sensor-out *policy*-SR sweep (it is the PRACTITIONER gap the ACADEMIC-dominated synthesis skipped), and fold the missing CONTROL-THEORIST angle into the headline: the real differentiator is bounding/regulating force at runtime without the sensor, not predicting it — which also converts the safety "free add-on" into the load-bearing thesis that unifies E1 and E2.

### E1 (Direction review)

Topic: the bet that a force-*objective* policy pretrained on ~20k hr of ego *video alone* (no force/tactile at any stage, "Route 1") reaches >=80% of a tactile-instrumented policy's SR on ForceVLA's 5 tasks, clearing the bar the settled drop-the-sensor distillers (Route 2: FD-VLA 61.1%, HapticVLA 86.7%) already replicate.

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily).**
- *Core position*: The deploy-side win is already banked: FD-VLA ([[2602.02142|FD-VLA]]) distils a force token and *beats* the with-sensor baseline at inference (61.1% vs 38.9%), and HapticVLA ([[2603.15257|HapticVLA]]) hits 86.7% sensor-free. What the card calls the "live" half (Route 1, ego-video-only) buys the researcher nothing a deployed system can feel: the robot still carries no sensor either way. The differentiator is purely *training-side data economics*, which is a real but much narrower prize.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: EgoScale's log-linear curve (+54% on a 22-DoF hand from 20,854 hr) is real and is the only thing that makes the data-economics argument land. But the practitioner notes the FTF result ([[2506.01944]] "Feel the Force") buried in alphaxiv: its force-masking ablation (Appendix Table 3) shows the policy infers desired force from vision+state with *no degradation* on lift-and-hold-bread. That means in the regimes the card targets, the force *objective* may be almost free-riding on visual deformation cues, i.e. the ego-video force head might not be doing the work the bet credits it with.
- *Only-this-perspective tells the researcher*: The 80%-of-ForceVLA target is on a 5-task, 244-trajectory in-house benchmark; ForceVLA's own "20% success in highly unstable socket insertion" is the regime where vision-uncorrelated slip dominates, and that is exactly where an ego-video-only policy has no signal. Pick the parity bar on the *easy* contact tasks and you will clear 80% trivially and prove nothing; pick it on socket-insertion and you will likely fail. The benchmark slice silently decides the result.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
- *Core position*: The card's central premise "no paper learns force-awareness from ego video alone" is true *as stated for a full closed-loop policy*, but the literature has already established every load-bearing sub-claim, so the residual novelty is an integration claim, not a discovery. FEEL ([[2603.15847|FEEL]]) already proves force is predictable from ego video (95.6% binary-contact, +4.85/+6.26 pp downstream) and FTF ([[2506.01944]]) already proves a human-data force-prediction policy transfers zero-shot to a robot for fragile-object tasks (77% over 5 tasks).
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: FTF is the closest prior art and it is a NeurIPS-pedigree group (Abbeel, Pinto). It predicts *desired* force, decouples learning from execution, and crucially shows force-input can be masked at train time. The card's H1 ("ego-video force objective clears the instrumented bar") is a recombination of FTF's force-prediction + EgoScale's scale + ManiFeel's ablation harness.
- *Only-this-perspective tells the researcher*: The card conflates two senses of "no sensor at any stage." FEEL's ego-video force *labels come from a piezoresistive glove*; FTF's come from an AnySkin glove and it needs an AnySkin sensor on the robot at deploy. So the only way to get a genuinely sensor-free *dataset* is synthetic/predicted force (Sparsh-X teacher, as the card's own H2/risks concede). The instant you admit a synthetic-tactile teacher on "a small fraction," Route 1 collapses into a weak-supervision variant of Route 2, and the clean "no force at any stage" framing is gone.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks the bet is wrong or already solved).**
- *Core position*: The whitespace is an artifact of an over-precise definition. The card concedes Route 2 is "settled" and then carves out "a force-*objective full policy* from ego video *alone*" as the surviving novelty: that is a definition narrow enough that nobody has filled it because nobody needs to: FTF already delivers force-sensitive manipulation from human data, and EgoScale already delivers the scaling. The remaining gap is the *conjunction* "no force at any stage AND full policy AND clears instrumented bar," and the bet's own risks section admits the first conjunct probably needs a synthetic teacher.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: FTF's masking ablation is the killer. If force can be inferred from vision with no degradation on the deformable tasks, then "force is upstream of vision, vision->force is a well-posed inverse" is not a *new* bet, it is a *demonstrated property* the card is re-betting on. And FTF's stated limitation ("loss of shear-force information," "could explore egocentric cameras") names the exact next step the card claims as whitespace.
- *Only-this-perspective tells the researcher*: The falsifier is structurally rigged. "If Route 1 cannot clear 80%, force-awareness is not learnable without a teacher" is too strong: failure could equally mean the *projection* failed (H5's embodiment gap) or the benchmark slice was adversarial, not that the premise is false. A falsifier that can be explained away by three other knobs is not a clean publication bet.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
- *Core position*: The narrative is shaped by who owns the data moat. EgoScale is NVIDIA; the 20k-hr-ego-video story is an NVIDIA/Apple (EgoDex)/Meta (Aria) play because they own the egocentric capture pipeline and the compute to ride the log-linear curve. An academic betting on Route 1 is renting the most capital-intensive part of the stack (20k+ hr pretraining) to attack a gap the incumbents will close as a byproduct of scaling.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The card itself lists EgoScale (NVIDIA), Being-H0 (BeingBeyond), HumanNet (1M-hr), VITRA (1M-episode) as the "Route-1 substrate awaiting a force objective." Every one is an industry-scale data engine. The force head is a cheap bolt-on; whoever already paid for the 20k-hr backbone adds it for marginal cost. The academic's comparative advantage is *not* there.
- *Only-this-perspective tells the researcher*: The defensible academic wedge is the *benchmark and the ablation protocol*, not the scaled policy. FEEL's pretraining protocol and ManiFeel's with/without-tactile harness are cheap to extend and hard for an industry lab to be bothered to standardize. The publishable unit is "here is the force-from-ego-video parity benchmark and the regime map of where it holds," not "we trained the 20k-hr policy."

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the pixel-vs-latent and modular-vs-end-to-end cycle replaying on the force modality. "Learn the privileged signal, then drop it at deploy" is the teacher-student / privileged-information lineage (Chen et al. learning-by-cheating, DAgger-with-privilege); Route 2 is that lineage applied to force, and it always "settles" first because privileged distillation is the path of least resistance. Route 1 ("derive the signal from raw observation alone, no privileged channel ever") is the harder, recurring dream that periodically over-promises.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The DR/sim2real history rhymes: every modality (depth, then tactile) goes privileged-teacher -> distill-away -> "can we skip the teacher entirely." The skip-the-teacher step historically works only where the target signal is *recoverable* from observation (depth-from-RGB succeeded; true fingertip slip-from-RGB has not). FTF's masking result is the optimistic precedent; the card's own "vision-to-tactile noise floor" risk is the pessimistic one.
- *Only-this-perspective tells the researcher*: Bound the claim the way successful prior waves did. Depth-from-mono won by *scoping to where the inverse is well-posed* and reporting the floor, not by claiming to replace all depth sensors. Route 1 should claim "vision-correlated force is recoverable; vision-uncorrelated slip is not, and here is the measured boundary," which is a durable contribution, rather than the unbounded "force-awareness is learnable without a teacher."

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *Practitioner vs Academic/Skeptic on what the bet buys*: Practitioner says deploy-side is already banked (FD-VLA beats the sensor), so the only prize is training-data economics; Skeptic says even that prize is illusory because FTF's masking ablation shows force is inferred for free in the targeted regimes. Specific claim in tension: card's H1 "ego-video force objective clears the instrumented bar" vs FTF Appendix Table 3 "force-masked variant shows no degradation."
   - *Academic vs the card on "no sensor at any stage"*: card frames Route 1 as sensor-free end-to-end; Academic shows FEEL's and FTF's ego-video force *labels* come from instrumented gloves, so a truly sensor-free dataset needs a synthetic teacher (Sparsh-X), which the card's own H2/risks concede, collapsing Route 1 toward Route 2.
   - *Economist vs everyone on who should make the bet*: Economist says the scaled-policy version is an incumbent's byproduct; the others implicitly assume the academic can win the policy claim.

2. **Strongest vs weakest perspective.**
   - *Strongest*: SKEPTIC, because it is anchored in the single most decisive piece of new evidence (FTF's force-masking ablation, confirmed via alphaxiv get_paper_content) and it attacks both the premise (vision->force is already-demonstrated, not a fresh bet) and the falsifier's logical validity.
   - *Weakest*: HISTORIAN, persuasive but analogical; the depth-from-mono parallel motivates the scoping recommendation but does not by itself settle whether *this* inverse (ego-video -> contact force for manipulation) is well-posed enough.

3. **The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction.**
   On ForceVLA's 5 tasks (or ManiFeel's 13), does an *ego-video force-objective head* outperform a *vision-only policy with no force head at all* by a margin that survives on the vision-*uncorrelated*-slip subset (socket insertion, single-cup unstack)? If the force head adds nothing beyond what FTF's masked variant already gets from visual deformation cues, the bet is pre-empted; if it adds margin specifically on vision-uncorrelated contact, Route 1 is real whitespace.

4. **What every perspective agrees on (likely true).**
   The deploy-side question is genuinely settled (a force-aware policy can run with no runtime sensor and match or beat the instrumented baseline: FD-VLA 61.1% > 38.9%, HapticVLA 86.7%, FTF 77%), and EgoScale's log-linear ego-data scaling is real. The contested part is entirely on the training side and on the vision-uncorrelated-force regime.

5. **The blind spot (addressed by none).**
   The temporal/closed-loop dimension of force. Every cited result (FEEL, FTF, FD-VLA, ForceVLA) is essentially a per-frame or short-horizon force *magnitude* claim; none address whether an ego-video-pretrained policy can do *reactive force control under disturbance over a horizon* (re-grasp on detected slip, modulate during a 10-second insertion). FTF's PD controller does this only because it has a runtime sensor; a truly sensor-free Route-1 policy has no error signal to close the loop on, and no perspective flagged that closed-loop force regulation, not open-loop force prediction, is the real deployment bar.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

1. **CEO summary.** E1's deploy-side claim is already won by the field and E1 correctly concedes it; the surviving bet is the narrow conjunction "a *full* force-objective policy pretrained on ego *video alone*, no force at any stage, clears the instrumented ForceVLA bar." That residual is real whitespace only under a strict reading, and it is squeezed from two sides: FEEL already shows force is predictable from ego video and FTF (NYU/Berkeley) already shows a human-data force-prediction policy transfers zero-shot to fragile-object manipulation at 77%, including an ablation where force can be masked with no loss. The defensible contribution is therefore not the scaled policy (an NVIDIA-scale byproduct) but a *scoped parity benchmark plus a regime map* of where vision-correlated force is recoverable and where vision-uncorrelated slip is not.

2. **Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
   - (i) *Deploy-side sensor-drop is settled.* FD-VLA 61.1% > raw-force 38.9%, HapticVLA 86.7%, FTF 77%. Supported by Practitioner, Academic, Skeptic, Historian; challenged by none. (Highest reliability.)
   - (ii) *EgoScale's log-linear ego-data scaling is real and is the bet's only genuine lever.* +54% on 22-DoF, 20,854-hr curve. Supported by all; Economist adds it is an incumbent-owned lever.
   - (iii) *"No force at any stage" is not literally achievable from real ego data* because FEEL/FTF force labels come from instrumented gloves; a sensor-free dataset needs a synthetic teacher, collapsing toward Route 2. Supported by Academic, Skeptic; the card's own H2/risks concede it.
   - (iv) *The force objective may free-ride on visual deformation cues in the targeted regimes*, per FTF's masking ablation: it may add little over a vision-only head except on vision-uncorrelated slip. Supported by Practitioner, Skeptic; challenged by the card's H1 framing. (Decisive but single-source; the resolving experiment in Prompt 2.3 settles it.)
   - (v) *The closed-loop / reactive-force dimension is unaddressed* and is the real deployment bar. Raised by synthesis; no perspective or cited paper covers it. (Speculative but high-leverage.)

3. **Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Economist's "the force head is a cheap bolt-on the incumbents add for free" and the Skeptic's "FTF shows force is inferred from vision for free" are the *same fact* seen from two angles: if the force objective is nearly free to add (vision already carries most of it), then it is both (a) not a defensible academic moat and (b) not a strong scientific claim. Cheapness-to-add and weakness-of-effect are the same coin.

4. **Actionable insight: DE-RISK, do not COMMIT as framed.** Do not run the 20k-hr Route-1 policy as the headline (incumbent-owned, expensive, and the falsifier is explainable-away). Instead re-pitch E1 around the *resolving experiment*: a controlled force-head-vs-no-force-head comparison on ManiFeel's 13-task with/without-tactile harness and ForceVLA's 5 tasks, with the result reported *split by contact type* (vision-correlated vs vision-uncorrelated slip). Concrete next experiment: take one EgoScale/Being-H0-class backbone, add a FEEL-style force-prediction head, and ablate it against (a) the same backbone with no force head and (b) FTF's masked variant, on the vision-uncorrelated-slip subset (socket insertion, single-cup unstack). The publishable unit is the regime boundary, not the policy. Drop the unbounded "force-awareness is learnable without a teacher" claim; keep "vision-correlated force is recoverable from ego video, here is the measured floor." If even the head-vs-no-head margin vanishes on the slip subset, DROP Route 1 and fold the surviving value into E2's cross-sensor portability story.

5. **Frontier question.** Can an ego-video-pretrained policy perform *closed-loop reactive force regulation under disturbance with no runtime sensor*, by learning an internal force *predictor* whose error against predicted-vs-expected-visual-consequence substitutes for the missing tactile error signal? If yes, that reframes Route 1 from "predict force open-loop" to "imagine the contact and correct on prediction error," which is a genuinely new and incumbent-resistant claim; if no, sensor-free force-awareness is permanently capped at open-loop magnitude and the deployment ceiling is set.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical robotics faculty. The briefing is sharp and largely honest about its own residual, but it leans one decisive claim (iv) on a paper that is not in the card under review, and it imports two external anchors (FTF) without flagging that they sit outside the evidence base it is supposedly reviewing. That asymmetry — strict reading of the card's own claims, loose sourcing of the rebuttals — is the main thing to fix.*

##### 1. Confidence scores (1–10, reliability)

| # | Finding | Score | One-line justification |
|---|---|---|---|
| (i) | Deploy-side sensor-drop is settled | **9** | Directly checkable in the card: FD-VLA 61.1% > raw-force 38.9%, ViTacGen 86%, HapticVLA 86.7% all present and consistent. The only soft spot is "FTF 77%" — FTF (2506.01944) is *not* in E1's card, so one of the four pillars is unverifiable against the document under review. Settled-ness survives even dropping it. |
| (ii) | EgoScale log-linear scaling is the only genuine lever | **8** | +54% / 22-DoF / 20,854-hr matches the card verbatim. "Real" is fair for a single-paper scaling curve; calling it *the* lever is a judgement, not a fact, and "incumbent-owned" is asserted, not measured. Down-weighted because one log-linear curve from one lab is thin ground for "the bet's only genuine lever." |
| (iii) | "No force at any stage" is not literally achievable; needs a synthetic teacher → collapses toward Route 2 | **8** | Strongest *reasoning* in the briefing and the card concedes it in H2/Risks (Sparsh-X on a small instrumented fraction). The "collapse toward Route 2" framing is slightly overstated: a synthetic teacher trained once and never queried at the target task is not the same as Route-2 per-task instrumented distillation. Directionally right, label too strong. |
| (iv) | Force objective may free-ride on visual deformation cues; adds little except on uncorrelated slip | **5** | The briefing's own "decisive but single-source" tag is honest, and the single source (FTF masking ablation) is *external to the card*. The card's FEEL row actually cuts the other way (force-pretraining gives +4.85% verb / +6.26% noun *frozen*, i.e. signal beyond vision). So the claim is contestable from inside the card and unverified from outside it. This is the weakest load-bearing finding. |
| (v) | Closed-loop / reactive-force is the real bar and is uncovered | **6** | Correct that every card row is open-loop predict-or-distill, so the gap is real and high-leverage. But not *fully* uncovered: Adaptive Vision-Torque Fusion (2604.01414) already carries the "when force matters / contact-gating" reactive dimension (30%→68% from gating alone) — consumed-sensor, yes, but the briefing's "no cited paper covers it" overstates by ignoring it. Speculative-but-valuable is the right caliber; the absoluteness is wrong. |

Mean = 7.2, min = 5.

##### 2. Weakest link

Finding **(iv)** — "the force objective adds little over a vision-only head except on vision-uncorrelated slip." It is the briefing's pivot (it justifies the entire DE-RISK recommendation and the re-pitch), yet it rests on a single ablation in **FTF (2506.01944), which is not a row in E1's card**, and it is directly contradicted by the card's FEEL row (frozen force-pretraining beats vision-only by +4.85/+6.26 on EPIC-Kitchens). What would verify it: the actual FTF masking-ablation numbers (SR with force-head vs force-masked, *split by contact type*), plus confirmation that the masking was at policy time rather than a label-dropout regularizer. Until then, "cheapness-to-add = weakness-of-effect" is an elegant aphorism, not an established equivalence — FEEL is a counterexample where a near-free objective still moves a frozen-backbone metric.

##### 3. Bias check

The **Skeptic** over-dominated, in coalition with the **Economist**. Four of five findings are framed as deflation ("already won," "free-rides," "not literally achievable," "is the same fact"), and the headline "hidden connection" *is* the Skeptic-Economist merge stated as a law. The Practitioner appears only as a co-signer, never as a dissent (e.g. no practitioner voice arguing that a scoped parity benchmark on real hardware is itself a multi-month contribution regardless of moat). The Historian is decorative — cited once on (i), absent from the regime-map argument where it would matter most (touch-vs-vision has a long oscillation history that bears on whether "uncorrelated slip" is a permanent or temporary wall). Net: the synthesis is internally consistent but tilted toward "this is cheap and weak," with no perspective tasked to steelman "the scoped benchmark is the win."

##### 4. Missing perspective

A **Metrologist / benchmark-validity reviewer** (sixth angle). The entire actionable insight hinges on "report SPLIT by contact type (vision-correlated vs vision-uncorrelated slip)" — but no one in the synthesis interrogates whether that split is *operationally definable and measurable* on ManiFeel's 13 tasks and ForceVLA's 5. If "vision-uncorrelated slip" cannot be labelled per-trial without the very fingertip sensor the policy is trying to avoid, the proposed publishable unit (the regime boundary) is unmeasurable, and the resolving experiment cannot resolve anything. This angle would change the conclusion from "DE-RISK to the regime map" to "first prove the regime map is instrumentable, else the de-risked pitch has the same teacher-dependency problem as Route 1." It also reframes the frontier question: the prediction-error substitute for tactile only works if you can ground-truth it, which again needs a sensor.

##### 5. Overall grade and top fixes

**Grade: B+.** Genuinely good adversarial reading — it correctly identifies that E1's surviving bet is a narrow conjunction, it ties cheapness-to-add to weakness-of-claim in a way the card does not, and it converts a "commit 20k-hr" pitch into a falsifiable controlled experiment. It loses the A tier on three things: (a) it builds its decisive claim and one of its five reliability pillars on FTF/2506.01944, a paper outside the card, without flagging the provenance gap; (b) it overstates absoluteness twice ("no perspective covers closed-loop" ignores Adaptive Vision-Torque Fusion's contact-gating; "collapses toward Route 2" conflates a one-time synthetic teacher with per-task distillation); (c) it never stress-tests its own remedy for measurability.

Top fixes, in order:
1. **Source-discipline the FTF claim.** Either pull FTF (2506.01944) and its masking numbers into the card as a real row, or demote finding (iv) from "decisive" to "hypothesis to test" — it cannot be both single-source-external *and* the pivot of the recommendation.
2. **Reconcile (iv) with FEEL.** FEEL's frozen +4.85/+6.26 is in-card evidence that the force objective is *not* free-riding noise. Address it head-on or the regime-map thesis is built on a contradiction the card already contains.
3. **Make the regime split measurable before betting on it.** Add the metrologist check: define how vision-uncorrelated slip is labelled on ManiFeel/ForceVLA without a runtime sensor. If it needs the sensor, the "publishable unit is the regime boundary" claim inherits the teacher-dependency the briefing used to deflate Route 1.
4. **Soften two absolutes.** "No cited paper covers closed-loop" → "only a consumed-sensor row (Adaptive Vision-Torque Fusion) touches the reactive dimension"; this strengthens, not weakens, the frontier question.
